#CH 211
#TITLE: Sending Servants to a Brothel to Fetch the Groom
Qianli Xun had set down only a handful of characters when Matron Wei walked in. Seeing her young mistress engrossed at her desk, the matron hated to interrupt, yet spoke up all the same. "Miss, why haven't you sent someone to bring the young master back?"
Having written so little, Qianli Xun had no worry that Matron Wei would see anything. She set down her brush and looked up. "Chen Haoyu and I have an agreement not to meddle in each other's affairs. Why would I bother looking for him?"
"Tomorrow is different," Matron Wei reminded her. "It is the third-day return visit to your maiden home. The groom cannot be absent, or people will call it bad luck. You had better bring him back, Miss. Besides, you promised Old Madam Xia, and you cannot go back on your word."
"Promising the old madam was just a stopgap. Going back to Zhu Manor without Chen Haoyu will let my father see for himself how unreliable his handpicked son-in-law is. As for good luck or bad luck, I could not care less."
Matron Wei understood her mistress, though she did not agree. "I understand how you feel, Miss, yet living in this world means you cannot think only of yourself. You have to weigh your surroundings and keep your actions within reason. Not for anything else, just to spare yourself trouble and buy some peace and quiet. Think it over: what will happen if you bring the young master back, versus if you don't?"
Qianli Xun turned it over in her head. If she left Chen Haoyu out there, Old Madam Xia would be back at her door making a scene. Showing up at Zhu Manor without him might send a pointed message to Zhu Xiyan, yet it would also hurt her parents and sour their opinion of the Chen family. Word might even spread among Lin'an's elite, whose wives and daughters had nothing better to do than chew over other households' gossip. Bringing Chen Haoyu back would nip every bit of that trouble in the bud.
Life left a person with so few clean choices, she reflected. Living in the mundane world meant playing by its rules, or inviting endless headaches.
In that instant, she saw Master Jingguang's words about "Right Thought and Right Mindfulness" in a new light. What counted as "right" depended on the situation, and right now, hauling Chen Haoyu home was the smartest move. "One word from you wakes the dreamer, Matron. I'll send someone to track him down right away."
Matron Wei smiled. "I'll leave you to it, Miss. It's been a tiring day, so I'll go rest."
Once Matron Wei left, Qianli Xun told Kuqiao to fetch Qinggua.
Stepping into the main room, Qinggua felt as if he had wandered into a stranger's house. The place looked nothing like before, and he dared not look around too much. This new young madam was formidable, unafraid to bully his master and bold enough to talk back to Old Madam Xia herself.
"Young Madam, you sent for me?"
With no mood left for her huaben and a runaway husband to hunt down, Qianli Xun turned the half-written sheet face down on the desk. "You heard Old Madam Xia tell me to bring Chen Haoyu back. You follow him around often, so you must have the best idea where he went."
Qinggua jumped straight into airing his grievances. "Young Madam, you just married in, so you wouldn't know. Young Master never takes me along when he goes out, and he loves kicking me for no reason. He only hangs around street hoodlums who tag along to sponge off his money, unlike me, who's devoted to him heart and soul. Yet he never appreciates it, and he doesn't even realize they're a bad influence..."
Qianli Xun had no patience for rambling. "I get that you have it rough. Right now, just tell me where to find Chen Haoyu."
Qinggua thought for a moment. "He never takes me with him, so it's hard to say what he gets up to outside or where he goes. Still, since last year he's been fond of haunting brothels. He might be in one right now."
Chunxiang Brothel sprang straight to Qianli Xun's mind. "Kuqiao, go call Hongdou."
Kuqiao fetched Hongdou at once.
"Hongdou," Qianli Xun instructed, "take seven of our male servants and head to Chunxiang Brothel. Bring Chen Haoyu back to me. If he refuses, tie him up with rope and haul him back."
Hongdou hesitated. "Miss, Chunxiang Brothel is a house of ill repute. I..."
"Why are you being so dense? You don't have to step inside. Just send the seven men in."
Relief washed over Hongdou's face. "Yes, Miss. I'll take them right now."
Once Hongdou left, Qinggua felt awkward lingering. "Young Madam, I'll go wait by the front gate for Young Master."
"Go on." Qianli Xun flipped her manuscript page back over and picked up her brush, meaning to resume writing, yet her mind stayed blank. She tossed the brush back down. "Too many people and too much drama in Chen Manor. It's killed my mood to write. Tomorrow will do."
Kuqiao stepped over and gathered the sheet with its few lines of ink. "I'll put this away for you, Miss. You can pick it back up when inspiration strikes."
After stowing the manuscript, Kuqiao walked back with a smile. "Miss, from now on you don't need to worry about Matron Wei seeing you write huaben."
"Why?" Qianli Xun asked, puzzled.
"She guessed ages ago that Master Qianli Xun is you, Miss. She just never called you out to your face."
"Nothing ever gets past Matron Wei. Just as well; now I won't have to hide it from her." A second thought crossed Qianli Xun's mind. "Does anyone else in this courtyard know?"
"You and I covered our tracks well, Miss. Aside from Matron Wei, nobody has a clue."
"That puts my mind at ease." Since she had no spark for fiction tonight, she might as well jot down a few of Zhu Shuzhen's poems for Kuqiao to keep on hand, so she would not have to scramble the next time Huayu came asking.
Meanwhile, Hongdou led seven martial-arts-trained male servants in two carriages to the doors of Chunxiang Brothel. She turned to one of them. "Asheng, take the boys inside and fetch the young master. I'll wait out here. Every last one of you listen up: don't let the women in there bewitch you and make you forget your errand!"
"Don't worry, Sister Hongdou," Asheng answered. "Even if we weren't scared of you, we're scared of Miss. Her fists don't mess around."
Hongdou cracked a smile. "Glad you remember. Go on."
Asheng and six others hopped down from the carriages and strode straight into Chunxiang Brothel, where the air reeked of perfume and rouge.
Spotting seven unfamiliar men dressed like household servants, the brothel madam stepped forward. "Don't recognize your faces, boys. Here for a good time, or looking for someone?"
"We're here for our young master, Young Master Chen," Asheng said.
"Ah, servants from the Chen household. Young Master Chen is upstairs enjoying himself in Chun Jinmo's room right now. I doubt he'll be coming back with you." Having no use for seven penniless servants, the madam turned to walk away.
Asheng shot a look to his companions, and three of them stepped straight across her path.
Asheng moved up. "Be so kind as to tell Young Master Chen that our young mistress requests his return. She also said that if he won't come down, we are to tie him up and drag him home."
The madam had never run into servants this brazen, yet she saw no sense in picking a fight. Spotting Chun Jinqiao finishing a performance on stage and heading for the stairs, she called out loud, "Jinqiao! Go upstairs and call Young Master Chen. Tell him someone's downstairs looking for him."
Lin Jue had not shown up today, leaving Chun Jinqiao with time to spare. She glanced at the men blocking the madam and called back, "Hold on, Madam, I'll go fetch him."
The madam forced a wide smile. "He'll be right down, boys. Step aside now; I've got work to do."
The three servants moved out of her way.
Before long, Chen Haoyu descended the stairs flanked by three martial-arts bodyguards, with Scarface and the rest of his lackeys trailing behind. He scanned the hall as he came down, seeing nothing but regular brothel patrons until his gaze snagged on seven men in servant livery who looked familiar.
Asheng recognized him at once. As soon as Chen Haoyu reached the bottom step, Asheng stepped forward and bowed. "Young Master, our young mistress asks you to return home."
Only then did Chen Haoyu place their faces, and a scowl crossed his brow. "She's sticking her nose in far too wide. Go tell her I'm not coming back yet. I'll go home once I've had my fill of fun."
"Tomorrow is the return visit to Zhu Manor, Young Master," Asheng pressed. "You must return tonight."
"Well now! Who the hell do you think you are, ordering me around? 'Must,' is it? Let me tell you something: I'm not going back, and let's see what you can do about it!"
In a crowded brothel, Chen Haoyu had no intention of earning a name as a henpecked husband. Besides, with three hired fighters at his back, seven servants meant nothing to him.
Asheng uncoiled a length of rope from behind his back. "Young Master, our mistress said if you refuse, we are to tie you up and carry you home."
Every head in the hall swiveled toward them; even the courtesans on stage stopped performing.
Chen Haoyu would never lose face before a crowd. He waved at his three bodyguards. "Teach them a lesson for me! Show them how proper servants behave!"
The three fighters held the seven servants in total contempt, assuming rich men's lackeys knew only how to pour tea and run errands. Each fighter figured he was worth two of them and could flatten all seven with ease. Snarling, they threw their fists forward.
The seven servants split into three squads on the spot, one group of three and two pairs, and met the fighters head-on.
Patrons scrambled as far back as they could. The madam ducked behind cover, shouting, "Stop fighting! Stop fighting! Break anything and you're paying for it!"
Seeing the seven servants unable to gain ground at first, Chen Haoyu crowed, "Relax! I'm right here. Whatever gets smashed, I'll pay full price!"
The madam shut her mouth and tucked herself into a corner to watch the show. With a deep-pocketed patron footing the bill, Chunxiang Brothel stood to lose nothing.
One-on-one, none of the servants could guarantee a win against these hired fighters, yet two or three against one was another story. Numbers told, and their months of fist training under Qianli Xun were no joke. Inch by inch, the seven servants seized the upper hand.
Within a quarter of an hour, all three bodyguards lay groaning on the floor with swollen faces and bruised noses. The seven servants had taken a few knocks themselves, nothing more than scrapes.
Asheng wiped a smear of blood from the corner of his mouth, unhitched the rope he had retied around his waist for the brawl, and faced Chen Haoyu. "Will you come home on your own two feet, Young Master, or should I tie you up?"
Chen Haoyu flipped his tune in a heartbeat. "Put that rope away! Why wouldn't I want to go home to my own house? I'm coming, I'm coming."
He strolled over to the three downed fighters and jeered, "You three need to practice harder instead of taking my silver for nothing. Can't even beat my own household servants, and you have the nerve to call yourselves masters. Hmph!"
One fighter winced through the pain. "Young Master Chen, we haven't seen a single wen for guarding you today. When do you plan to pay us?"
"When have I ever been stingy about money?" Chen Haoyu blustered. "I'll pay you another day, no question."
The brothel madam hurried over too. "Young Master Chen, look at the wreck your servants made of Chunxiang Brothel. When will you settle for the damages?"
"Relax, I'll pay you next time I'm here," Chen Haoyu declared without blinking.
Watching Chen Haoyu's retreating back, Scarface muttered to a crony, "What's gotten into Young Master Chen today? Going out without a scrap of silver? That's rare. Think that tigress he married has him on a tight leash and cut off his allowance?"
"Looks like it," the lackey agreed.
Chen Haoyu resented the whole affair, yet his feet stayed obedient as the seven servants escorted him out of Chunxiang Brothel. Along the way, he tried sweet-talking them. "Seven grown men like you—what's the fun in tagging along behind Zhu Shuzhen? Come work for me instead. I'll treat you to the best food and fun in Lin'an. Beats being someone's lackey."
"Thank you for thinking so high of us, Young Master," Asheng replied. "We belong to Miss's household, and every move we know we learned from her. She is both our mistress and our martial master. What kind of disciple abandons his master?"
Chen Haoyu gave up and climbed into one of the Chen carriages, only to spot Hongdou sitting inside. "Get out at once. I always ride alone."
As long as the groom came home without fuss, Hongdou obeyed as a servant should. She hopped down, crammed five of the men into the other carriage, and walked alongside Chen Haoyu's carriage with Asheng.
Sitting alone inside grew dull fast. Thinking back on how skilled Zhu Shuzhen's people were, Chen Haoyu wished he had loyal fighters like them at his beck and call.
He poked his head out the carriage window. "I have a soft heart, and I'd hate to see you walk yourselves ragged. I'll allow you two to ride inside with me."
Neither Hongdou nor Asheng stood on ceremony. Once the driver pulled up, both climbed aboard.
Chen Haoyu fished for an opening. "Like it or not, I'm your young master, half an owner of this house. From now on, shouldn't you follow me wherever I go so nobody bullies me? Take that Master Jiang, for instance. You clashed with his men last time, so you know him. Who knows when he might come hunting trouble with me again?"
"Protecting you is no problem, Young Master," Hongdou answered. "The moment Miss gives the word, we'll guard you."
"Forget I said anything." Chen Haoyu shut his mouth. He knew without asking that Zhu Shuzhen would never agree. He had hoped to poach one or two of them, only to get shut down before he even warmed up.
Still, he felt a pang of envy toward Zhu Shuzhen for keeping such devoted people around her. When would he ever have followers like that—obedient, tough, and not just hanging around for his silver?
#CH 212
#TITLE: Visiting the Princess to Sound Out the Truth
Qinggua had been waiting by the main gate of Chen Manor the whole time. The moment two carriages rolled up, he bounced forward. "Young Master, you're back!"
Out climbed a crowd of servants just like himself. His grin vanished. "Why is it just you lot? Where's Young Master?"
One servant jabbed a thumb behind him. "In the carriage behind."
Qinggua plastered his smile back on and trotted over. "Did you have a good time out, Young Master?"
The sight of Qinggua set Chen Haoyu's temper flaring as he stepped down. "Look at them, every one a trained fighter. And look at you, grinning like an idiot and sticking to me like a dog-skin plaster the second I step through the door."
Stung by the scorn, Qinggua kept smiling all the same. "Don't put it all on me, Young Master. They're Young Madam's people, and you know how formidable she is."
That stung worse. Chen Haoyu aimed a kick at him. "What's that? You're sneaking in a jab saying I'm weak? Watch your miserable hide!"
Seasoned by long habit, Qinggua had braced for it and hopped clear.
Hongdou and the others chuckled at the sight of the young master taking his frustration out on Qinggua.
"From now on," Chen Haoyu barked, "train with them and learn real martial arts. Get good at it and I'll treat you with fresh respect."
Qinggua pulled a sour face. "Young Master, you're asking the impossible."
"If you don't train hard, I'll boot you out of the back courtyard and back into the West Courtyard. Forget about sleeping in the same room as me."
Qinggua had tasted two nights of sleeping in the master's quarters and found it far too comfortable to give up for a crowded servants' dorm. "Fine! Since you say so, Young Master, I'll... I'll train hard!"
Inside Chen Manor, Chen Haoyu reached the entrance of his own courtyard and stopped short, convinced he had taken a wrong turn. He glanced back. No, the path was right. Dark as it was, he had walked this route for over a decade; how could he miss it?
Yet he remembered no bamboo outside his gate. Where had these thick clumps of green stalks flanking both sides come from?
Before he could ask, Qinggua spoke up. "This is your courtyard, Young Master. Young Madam had the gardeners plant the bamboo today. Look up, too—your courtyard even has a name now."
Chen Haoyu tilted his head up at the plaque reading Moxiang Courtyard and boiled over. "Vulgar! Beyond vulgar! Zhu Shuzhen, you've gone too far!"
He stormed through the gate toward the main hall. By the moonlight, he spotted a lush tree planted on the eastern side. With nowhere else to vent his rage, he spun around and kicked Qinggua square in the thigh. "Didn't you know I hate trees in my courtyard?"
Caught off guard this time, Qinggua clutched his thigh. "How is that my fault, Young Master? Young Madam ordered it transplanted here. What can a servant do to stop his mistress?"
Chen Haoyu looked up again and read the plaque over the main room: Suxin Thatched Hall. "Calling it something that shabby, and she calls herself a poetess. Hmph!"
Inside, Qianli Xun was reading a book. She glanced up to find Chen Haoyu standing before her, fuming.
She set the book down. "You got back faster than I expected. Not bad. Cooperate with me from here on out, and we'll stay out of each other's hair."
"Zhu Shuzhen, you've gone too far!" Chen Haoyu snapped. "Planting bamboo by my gate, sticking a tree in my courtyard, renaming every single room—and worst of all, turning my hall into this bare, penniless dump! Is this even fit for a human being to live in?"
Unbothered by his tantrum, Qianli Xun rose and walked over. "Open your eyes and take a proper look. What's shabby about this? Every piece of my furniture is carved from fragrant camphorwood in clean, understated tones—nothing like that gaudy, tacky heap you used to keep in here."
Furious as he was, Chen Haoyu had no leverage against her. He swallowed his temper. "Didn't we agree not to interfere with each other? I was having a fine time out there. Why did you send people to drag me home over something trivial like a return visit?"
"Don't blame me," Qianli Xun explained. "Old Madam Xia came looking for trouble and handed me that task. If I hadn't fetched you back, it would have meant bigger headaches later, so I sent Hongdou and the men. Relax—I honor my contracts. I won't restrict your freedom."
Hearing that Zhu Shuzhen had bent because Old Madam Xia came making trouble turned Chen Haoyu's anger into glee. "Heh! Didn't you act fearless of heaven and earth? Why are you scared of my grandmother?"
"Which ear heard me say I'm scared of Old Madam Xia? I just hate hassle. Less drama beats more drama, and dragging you home saves me a mountain of trouble. As for you, behave tomorrow for the return visit to Zhu Manor. You stay happy, I stay happy, everyone stays happy, and we leave each other in peace."
At last Chen Haoyu had spotted a chink in her armor: she hated trouble. From now on, he would stir up plenty of it to make her miserable and pay her back for stealing his courtyard, gutting his rooms, and dismissing his maids.
"I won't stoop to arguing with you. I'm going to bed so I can make it to Zhu Manor tomorrow."
Stepping out of Suxin Thatched Hall into the back courtyard, Chen Haoyu glanced at the plaque reading Boya Study without flaring up as before. He had a weapon against Zhu Shuzhen now. After the return visit tomorrow, he would pay his grandmother a call and put that sharp blade to good use.
Then a snag occurred to him. Yesterday, after Old Madam Xia drove Zhu Shuzhen off during the tea ceremony, she had praised the girl as sensible and admitted she liked her. Maybe Grandmother was only pretending to dislike her.
No matter, Chen Haoyu decided. Pretend or real, so long as she gave Zhu Shuzhen grief, he would make use of it.
Earlier that afternoon, when Shen Jianchen returned to Youlin Retreat from Zuiyun Tower, he ran right into Miao Zhiyu and Bai Meiruo arriving at the gate.
Both women bowed to Prince Yi at once.
Shen Jianchen instructed a servant to escort them to Qiao Mengrong. Walking in with them meant painful small talk with nothing to say, so he seized the excuse to visit the Divine Willow instead, turning back into the deep woods with Bai Feng in tow.
Watching Prince Yi's retreating figure, Bai Meiruo murmured, "You don't think Prince Yi dislikes us coming to visit the princess?"
"Not at all," Miao Zhiyu replied. "Prince Yi has always been aloof, distant toward every woman except the princess."
Bai Meiruo dropped her voice. "I wouldn't be so sure. Have you forgotten?"
Miao Zhiyu put on a show of sudden realization. "Ah, right. She is the exception."
A retreat servant guided them to the princess's private courtyard and announced at the door, "Your Highness, the young madams of the Qiao and Lin households request an audience."
Having lost Shuzhen's friendship, Qiao Mengrong felt hollow and eager for new companions. Both women outside had every reason to harbor grudges against Zhu Shuzhen; inviting them in for a chat would let her test the waters.
Reading her mistress's mind, Xilan stepped to the doorway. "Her Highness invites both madams inside."
They entered at an unhurried pace. Qiao Mengrong sat at her desk, pale and draped in an outer cloak, her wound far from healed. Both guests curtsied. "Greetings, Princess Jiahe."
Qiao Mengrong laid down her scroll. "Xilan, bring seats."
Xilan arranged the chairs, and another maid brought in fine tea.
Miao Zhiyu spoke first. "It has been ages since we last saw Your Highness. With nothing pressing today, we agreed to pay our respects. We hope we are not intruding."
"Nonsense," Qiao Mengrong said. "Coming to visit, keep me company, and lift my spirits—I can't thank you enough. How could it be an intrusion?"
Her warmth put both visitors at ease.
"Has Your Highness recovered from your injury?" Bai Meiruo asked.
Qiao Mengrong realized they knew all about her brother raiding the wedding procession. A scandal that loud had no doubt traveled across all of Lin'an.
"So you already heard. In all my years, this is the first time I've become the talk of the town—all thanks to Zhu Shuzhen."
Hearing the raw bitterness in her voice, the two guests traded a subtle smile.
"Your Highness need not fret," Miao Zhiyu said. "Who would dare speak out of turn about Prince Yi or Your Highness? Folks are only indignant on your behalf."
"Don't try to flatter me. There's bound to be gossip. If you count me as a friend, give me the truth. What are people saying?"
Those words signaled that Princess Jiahe wanted them in her circle; their trip had already paid off.
"Since Your Highness is willing to take us as friends," Bai Meiruo said, "promise you won't hold my bluntness against me."
"Friends speak without holding back. Why would I hold it against you?"
No fool, Bai Meiruo picked the exact tidbits the princess craved. "Everyone says Youqi Jushi has talent without virtue—that failing to land a good marriage herself, she set out to seduce Prince Yi and climb above her station. They say she has a knack for bewitching men, making them lose their heads and do foolish things without even realizing how she hooked them. People are also saying last year's rumors about her wandering eyes and flirting behind closed doors were true all along."
Qiao Mengrong marveled at how fast feelings could turn. Last year, she had bristled in defense of Shuzhen; today, those same jabs felt vindicating, even accurate.
"Without realizing" and "flirting behind closed doors" hit home hardest. In her eyes, Zhu Shuzhen had used every visit and chat at Youlin Retreat as cover to seduce her brother, clouding his mind until he committed a wedding raid nobody could fathom.
"I didn't know people saw through her so clear. How I regret failing to recognize her true face sooner. I handed her my whole heart as a confidante, and she left me wounded body and soul."
"You only knew her a short while, Your Highness," Miao Zhiyu said. "It's natural you missed her true nature. I grew up knowing her, and even I didn't see through her until after marrying Wenyuan. It only proves how skilled Youqi Jushi is at wearing a mask."
Qiao Mengrong gave a cold laugh. "She wears it well indeed. Still, Madam Miao, you did better than me. You knew her from childhood, shared a love of poetry, and attended salons together, yet you never made her your close friend. That shows you and she were never cut from the same cloth."
"I used to wonder about that myself," Miao Zhiyu explained. "At first I couldn't figure out why, until a close friend opened my eyes with a single remark. She pointed out that though I attend poetry banquets too, no scandal has ever touched me because I always guard a gentlewoman's propriety. Youqi Jushi follows her impulses and forgets feminine decorum, which invites gossip. At our core we are opposites, so we could never be true friends."
Qiao Mengrong sighed. "Indeed. What propriety does she have? Neither her morals nor her conduct fit a well-bred lady. And to think last year I defended that line she wrote, 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed'! Looking back, only someone as shameless as her could pen such words. All those years I admired her poetry in secret—never again will I be such a fool."
Bai Meiruo seized the opening. "Once word gets out about her lack of virtue, her title as the Poetess of Jiangnan won't last long."
That sparked an idea in Qiao Mengrong. "Since she showed no kindness, she can't blame me for showing no mercy. Once I return from Bianliang, I'll take steps to strip her of that title."
Both visitors brightened at the news.
"Why is Your Highness returning to Bianliang?" Miao Zhiyu asked.
Only then did a genuine smile touch Qiao Mengrong's lips. "His Majesty issued an edict summoning Brother and me back to Bianliang to wed."
Both women offered their congratulations at once. "Congratulations on seeing your wish fulfilled, Your Highness!"
Qiao Mengrong nodded in satisfaction.
"What a pity we cannot attend your grand wedding," Bai Meiruo said. "Once you return to Lin'an, we will come offer our congratulations in person."
"When that day comes, it won't just be the two of us," Miao Zhiyu added. "The Qiaos, the Lins, and every noble household in Lin'an will come to celebrate."
Qiao Mengrong's spirits lifted further. "Come visit me often from now on, both of you. Madam Miao, introduce your close friends to me as well. By the time I return to Lin'an, I hope to count every well-bred lady in the city among my friends."
Both guests caught the subtext: Princess Jiahe meant to unite Lin'an's entire gentlewoman circle to freeze Youqi Jushi out. Delighted, they felt their visit could not have gone better.
With the real business settled, they chatted a while longer and took their leave as the sun dipped toward the horizon.
#CH 213
#TITLE: He Rides to Bianliang, She Visits Her Maiden Home
Beneath the Divine Willow, Shen Jianchen sat still, replaying his meeting with Qianli Xun at Zuiyun Tower. He had laid his heart bare to her: even married to Mengrong, he would stay chaste for the woman he loved.
Right or wrong in other people's eyes did not matter; what mattered was his own conscience. He had always seen Mengrong as a sister, and what kind of older brother could bring himself to lay hands on his own sister?
Dusk settled and the moon climbed into the sky. Seated on the boulder with his back against the willow's trunk, Shen Jianchen listened to the truest voice inside him, and it said the same thing: he could not let Qianli Xun go.
In silence, he spoke to the tree. "Divine Willow, the nun up at the nunnery told me to follow fate instead of clinging to it. I'll follow this fate and set off for Bianliang tomorrow to marry Mengrong, yet I still can't let Qianli Xun go. Even with both of us married, I still want her. Tell me what I'm supposed to do."
A gust of wind shook loose a red silk ribbon from the upper branches. It drifted through the air and brushed his cheek before landing at his feet. Opening his eyes, he picked it up and smoothed it out. Two names stood inked across the top: Chu Linfeng and Yun Qingwan—refined names that spoke of a cultured young couple.
Below the names ran a couplet: "No water's wide enough when you have crossed the sea; / No cloud is beautiful but that which crowns the peak." [TN: Translated by Xu Yuanchong]
Shen Jianchen knew those famous lines by Tang poet Yuan Zhen from "Thinking of My Dear Departed." They cut straight to his own heart. Was the Divine Willow urging him not to give up?
Further down the ribbon sat a quatrain titled "Longings in Sorrow":
"Gulls and mandarin ducks swim in the same pond, / Everyone knows they don't belong together. / The Lord of the East has failed as master of flowers, / Why must we be intertwined branches all our lives?" [TN: Translated by Ronald Egan]
Those four lines carried Shen Jianchen straight back to a memory before his transmigration.
One day, he had brought his disciples to Qianli Fist Manor for a scheduled spar, only to walk in on Sun Heng proposing to Qianli Xun. Sun Heng was a textbook rich second-generation heir—wealthier than Shen Jianchen, young, handsome, and rumored to have dated several actresses.
Outside the manor gates, Sun Heng had arranged roses into a giant red heart with a crimson velvet box at the center holding a diamond ring whose size screamed astronomical price.
Sun Heng bellowed toward the doors, "Qianli Xun! If you don't agree to marry me today, I'm not budging!"
His entourage took up the chant: "Marry him! Marry him!"
Passersby crowded in to gawk. Shen Jianchen had no idea how Qianli Xun had crossed paths with a tycoon like this, and watching the spectacle drained every ounce of confidence from his chest.
A disciple fretted beside him. "Master, with competition like that, you don't stand a chance."
Nervous inside, Shen Jianchen kept his chin up. "Aside from money, how does Sun Heng beat me at anything? Qianli Xun doesn't even look twice at me. Why would she want him?"
Qianli Xun strode out of Qianli Fist Manor with her students at her heels, unmoved by the roses and wearing a look of pure disdain. "Sun Heng, did you forget your meds? I've met you twice in my whole life and you show up proposing? You think I'm some cheap pushover who'll say yes just because you threw down a bunch of flowers?"
"It's precisely because you aren't a pushover that I'm proposing," Sun Heng declared.
Qianli Xun did not buy a word of it. Wanting him gone fast, she set her terms. "You want my yes? Either fight me a round, or appreciate a poem. Pick one."
"With your skills, I'd never beat you in a fight," Sun Heng admitted. "I'll take the poem."
Her disciple Er'gang pulled out a sheet of paper and recited Zhu Shuzhen's "Longings in Sorrow": "Gulls and mandarin ducks swim in the same pond, / Everyone knows they don't belong together. / The Lord of the East has failed as master of flowers, / Why must we be intertwined branches all our lives?"
While Er'gang read, Sun Heng looked it up on his phone. The moment Er'gang finished, Sun Heng announced, "That's by the Song poetess Zhu Shuzhen—a lament about marrying the wrong man. Am I right?"
Qianli Xun grinned. "Spot on. Now that you know what it means, sweep up your roses, pack up your ring, and give my dojo back some peace and quiet."
Sun Heng blinked in confusion. "Wait—didn't you say if I picked one condition, you'd say yes?"
Unable to hold back a second longer, Shen Jianchen stepped out of the crowd and addressed him loud and clear. "Still don't get it? Qianli Xun is saying you're the gull and she's the mandarin duck—stuck in the same pond, completely mismatched. Even the Lord of the East, the god of spring, wants nothing to do with your roses, so why would he play matchmaker for you two? She just turned you down with a poem. Couldn't be clearer. Pack it up and leave."
Sun Heng fumed, yet dared not throw a punch. This was Qianli Fist Manor, and the man lecturing him was the heir to Shen Fist; he stood no chance in a brawl. He ordered his men to grab the ring, stomp the roses to pulp, and stormed off.
After Sun Heng left, Qianli Xun gave Shen Jianchen an approving nod. "Your explanation was a bit stretched, yet you got the gist right. Thanks for chasing him off."
It was the first time Qianli Xun had ever thanked him, and he had never forgotten it. That day had also sealed his respect for her; how many women in the world could shrug off a billionaire heir like Sun Heng without blinking?
Back beneath the Divine Willow, Shen Jianchen held the ribbon and murmured, "Looks like this couple shares our fate—hearts set on someone they can't be with, pinning their last hope on a strip of silk. Let me tie it back up for them. Maybe the Divine Willow will lend them a hand."
He stood, knotted the ribbon onto a low willow branch, and, judging that Miao Zhiyu and Bai Meiruo must have left by now, walked back along the pond toward Youlin Retreat.
Bai Feng trailed behind, debating what fun trinkets to buy for Kuqiao in Bianliang.
At daybreak, two carriages rolled out of Youlin Retreat and left Lin'an on the road to Bianliang.
Shen Jianchen had told none of the Lin'an officials his departure hour. He wanted to ride back to the capital without fanfare and meet his fate in peace. His mood was bleak enough without an audience; slipping out spared everyone the bother.
Qiao Mengrong's wound had not healed all the way, yet joy put fresh color in her cheeks, leaving her radiant compared with the day before.
That same morning, a carriage departed Chen Manor with Qinggua and Kuqiao walking alongside.
Riding in the same carriage with Chen Haoyu for the first time left Qianli Xun stiff and out of sorts. Chen Haoyu, by contrast, slumped back and started snoring the instant he climbed aboard.
Listening to his snores filled Qianli Xun with self-pity and sharper sympathy for Zhu Shuzhen. Four lines popped into her head and slipped off her tongue: "Gulls and mandarin ducks swim in the same pond, / Everyone knows they don't belong together. / The Lord of the East has failed as master of flowers, / Why must we be intertwined branches all our lives?"
That roused Chen Haoyu, who had only been faking sleep to avoid talking to her. He cracked his eyes open with a scowl. "Can't you act normal? We're just making a return visit and you're reciting poetry? You ruined my nap!"
Shaking off her gloom, Qianli Xun decided to chat instead. "Hey, Chen Haoyu, what sort of woman do you actually like?"
He shot her a mocking grin. "What, fishing for my tastes so you can mold yourself to fit and make me fall for you?"
"Mold my foot! Can't you talk like a normal person?"
Chen Haoyu sat up straight and grinned. "No harm telling you. I like untouched maidens, so divorced women like you can step to the back of the line. She has to be pretty—you pass on that count. Most important, she needs a sweet, melting voice and a gentle temper. Someone coarse like you who starts swinging fists at the drop of a hat doesn't even make my list."
Qianli Xun did not take offense; she liked him no better. "As your primary wife, I'm willing to let you take a concubine or two. Want me to scout around for someone matching your tastes?"
Her real aim was practical: tie Chen Haoyu down at home with women he liked so he would quit gallivanting outside, and maybe the Chen elders would stop nagging her to whip their wayward boy into shape.
Chen Haoyu squinted at her. "You're being this generous? What's the catch?"
"No catch," Qianli Xun said with a straight face. "Just looking out for all of us. As your primary wife, it's my duty to think of your comfort. Why not buy Chun Jinmo's freedom and bring her home? Beats running to Chunxiang Brothel and giving folks something to gossip about."
He smirked again. "You really aren't like other women. Did watching Lin Jue haul courtesans home lower your standards so much that I look like a prize by comparison, making you want a real marriage with me? Guess I owe Lin Jue a big thank-you."
Qianli Xun flared up. "Talk more trash and see if I don't deck you!"
"A couple of sentences and the mask slips right off," Chen Haoyu gloated. "Let me tell you, I'm not done playing around. I'll think about concubines once I've had my fun. Besides, I don't like Chun Jinmo enough to take her as a concubine—she's just someone to pass time with when I'm bored. Mind your own business and quit fretting over mine."
Qianli Xun itched to thrash him on the spot, yet Zhu Manor was almost in sight, so she held back. "You're pure scum!"
"So what if I am? Look at you, the celebrated Poetess of Jiangnan, and you still ended up married to scum like me! Hmph. Seems to me you're fated to marry brothel-hoppers for life. Accept your fate, Zhu Shuzhen!" Chen Haoyu leaned back against the carriage wall, grinning ear to ear.
That push did it. Qianli Xun threw a punch, yet a split second of hesitation slowed her arm, letting him duck clear.
"Hit me and I'll tattle to my father-in-law the second we arrive," he crowed. "Let's see him bring out the Zhu family rules on you."
Qianli Xun pulled back her fist. "Don't think I have no way to handle you. Today's the return visit, and I won't upset my mother. You get a pass for now. Once we're back at Chen Manor, I've got plenty of ways to deal with you."
Chen Haoyu let out a huff and held his tongue as the carriage pulled up outside the side gate of Zhu Manor.
Kuqiao called from outside, "Miss, Young Master, we're here. Please step down."
Qianli Xun hopped out first, meeting her brother and sister-in-law waiting at the gate. "Brother, Sister-in-law."
"Married life in the Chen household agrees with you, Shuzhen," Chen Wenxin praised. "Your complexion looks even brighter."
Climbing down behind her, Chen Haoyu overheard his sister and chimed in, "Of course she looks great—she spent the whole ride driving me up the wall."
Chen Wenxin marched right up and pinched her brother's ear. "Spout more nonsense and I'll twist this ear right off! Shuzhen is educated and refined; why would she give you grief? You must have been provoking her nonstop, am I right?"
"Ow, ow, ow!" Chen Haoyu yelped. "What a miserable life—married to a tigress and stuck with a fierce older sister! Brother-in-law, aren't you going to save me?"
Watching her sister-in-law handle Chen Haoyu gave Qianli Xun pure delight.
"All right, Wenxin," Zhu Jingyuan interceded. "Haoyu came along for Shuzhen's return visit today. Let go of his ear."
Chen Wenxin released him. "For your brother-in-law's sake, I'll let you off this once. Behave yourself today, understand?"
"Understood," Chen Haoyu answered like a scolded puppy.
Seeing him turn meek amazed Qianli Xun. Her sister-in-law's ear-pinch worked better than her own fists.
The return visit ran its usual dull course: paying respects to the elders, trading small talk, and sitting through a banquet where Chen Haoyu met every senior member of the Zhu clan. To his credit, he did not embarrass the Chen family, bowing properly to every last one.
After the feast dispersed, Chen Haoyu stayed in the front hall chatting with his father-in-law and brother-in-law, while Qianli Xun talked domestic matters with Chen Wenxin and Madam Xiang.
Midway through, a prefectural official called on Prefect Zhu. They spoke in the outer hall for a few moments before the visitor excused himself.
Returning to the inner room, Zhu Xiyan remarked, "Prince Yi set off for Bianliang first thing this morning without telling a soul. I had planned to lead the city officials to see him off, yet we never got the chance."
Remembering his promise to hand Zhu Shuzhen over to Prince Yi, Chen Haoyu worried the prince might not come back. "Father-in-law, will Prince Yi return to Lin'an?"
"Of course," Zhu Xiyan said. "He only went back to Bianliang to hold his wedding."
Zhu Jingyuan filled in the rest. "His Majesty's decree arrived on your wedding day, moving up the marriage between Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe and ordering them back to Bianliang at once."
Chen Haoyu sighed to himself. Even an imperial prince could not choose his own bride with an emperor for a father. Compared with that, his own lot was not bad—especially since he had the foresight to sign a contract with his tigress.
Glancing at Zhu Xiyan's beaming face, Chen Haoyu grumbled inside: You played matchmaker blind, ruining my life and your own daughter's—what's there to grin about?
"Haoyu," Zhu Xiyan said, "tell your father when you get home that once Prince Yi returns from Bianliang, I will host a wedding banquet with all the officials of Lin'an. Have him prepare a generous gift and attend."
Choking back his private complaints, Chen Haoyu answered dutifully, "Yes, sir."
#CH 214
#TITLE: The Chen Brothers Each Mind Their Own Affairs
While his son and daughter-in-law paid their visit to Zhu Manor, Chen Jianglin rode by carriage to the Qiantang Gang.
As the eldest Chen brother and head of the family fortune, Chen Jianglin warranted respect. For him to call in person meant urgent business.
Only two or three days had passed, so Master Jiang's wounds were nowhere near healed. Still, he propped himself up in a chair to receive his guest.
Stepping inside and seeing Master Jiang battered and bruised, Chen Jianglin felt a surge of quiet glee. Whoever had worked him over had done the Chen family a favor. Keeping his thoughts to himself, he took a seat without waiting to be asked.
"What happened to you, Master Jiang? Who had the gall to beat you into this state?"
Master Jiang dared not breathe a word about Prince Yi. "Not worth mentioning. Walk by the river long enough and your shoes get wet. I can't hold the upper hand every day; got to let folks blow off steam now and then. You're a rare visitor, Master Chen. Usually your eldest nephew handles dealings with me. What brings you here in person today?"
"Do you need me to remind you what you pulled?"
Catching on at once that this was about the wager, Master Jiang chuckled. "You ought to thank me. Without my nudge, would Chen Haoyu ever have agreed to marry that tigress?"
"Reluctant or not, Haoyu was going to marry sooner or later. What puzzles me is why Master Jiang took such a sudden interest in my family's affairs—wagering the entire Chen fortune, winning, and then walking away without touching a coin. Makes no sense."
True to underworld code, Master Jiang refused to spill the real story. "Just playing a joke on your boy for hogging the woman I wanted at Chunxiang Brothel. In my line of work, you learn not to reach for what isn't yours. Sure, the Chen fortune makes my mouth water, yet I'd choke trying to swallow it. I just took the roundabout path to push your son into marriage so his tigress could rein him in and leave Chun Jinmo to me."
Chen Jianglin did not buy a word of it. "Who knew Master Jiang was such a romantic—valuing one courtesan above the Chen family's entire estate?"
Wary of letting anything slip if the talk dragged on, Master Jiang shifted to the offensive. "Instead of interrogating me, Master Chen, you'd better rein in your son. He's a married man now. Tell him to stay out of Chunxiang Brothel before Prefect Zhu finds out and takes a whip to his hide."
Hearing the dismissal in his tone, Chen Jianglin saw no point pushing further; he would pry nothing out of the man today. Before rising, he delivered a parting warning. "The Chen family did not reach the top of Lin'an's wealth by letting anyone push us around. Take a word of advice, Master Jiang: when you play around with Haoyu outside, know your limits. Push too far, and calling in the Lin'an garrison to wipe out the Qiantang Gang wouldn't cost me a sweat."
With that, Chen Jianglin stood and walked out.
That parting threat stung Master Jiang's pride, yet his battered ribs kept him from jumping up to roar back. All he managed was a tight, venomous "Don't let the door hit you!"
Once Chen Jianglin was gone, Master Jiang spat in disgust. "Act all high and mighty! Your Chen household looks shiny on the outside, yet it's rotten enough on the inside!"
Back at Chen Manor, inside Chen Jiangshu's quarters, Madam Bao and Chen Haodong had dismissed every servant and shut the doors and windows for a private huddle around the table.
"That bride Haoyu married is no pushover," Madam Bao said. "She even talks back to Old Madam Xia! Third Sister-in-law and I stirred the old madam up until she marched over to make trouble, armed with three solid grievances—and that girl talked her way out of every single one. Wasted the whole trip."
"The tougher she is, the better," Chen Haodong said. "I can't wait to see Haoyu suffer under a fierce wife. I'll wake up laughing from my dreams."
"Old Madam Xia put her in charge of disciplining Haoyu, though," Madam Bao worried. "What if a wife that tough actually whips him into shape and threatens your future?"
"You're worrying over nothing, Mother. Even if Haoyu turns into a saint, he can't hold a candle to me. I started learning business at ten. At ten, he was climbing trees for bird nests, catching crabs in streams, and teasing neighborhood girls. How could he compare?"
Chen Jiangshu chimed in. "Of course he can't match Haodong. Business takes years of building contacts and judgment. Even if Haoyu reforms, he starts from zero. Put your mind at ease."
"True enough." Reassured by father and son, Madam Bao brightened—then paused. "Wait. If my son is that far ahead, why worry about Haoyu inheriting the estate at all? Why go through all that trouble setting up that play with Master Jiang?"
Chen Jiangshu answered with mild condescension. "Women never think far enough ahead. Listen: Haodong beats Haoyu in skill and experience, yet when it comes time to pick an heir, Old Madam Xia's vote carries huge weight. She only has eyes for Haoyu; when has she ever looked twice at Haodong? To be safe, we had to get Haoyu tied down fast to a Zhu daughter with a ruined name..."
Chen Haodong cut in. "Mother, you don't know—one of the Chen clan's rules for choosing the family heir is that his primary wife must come from an upright family, possess virtuous character, and hold an untarnished name. The Zhu girl only passes the first test. Virtuous and untarnished? Not a chance. Every clan elder aside from Grandmother takes that rule to heart. That's why I went to such lengths to push Haoyu's wedding through."
"So that's why! Brilliant, brilliant! You two scheme outside, and I'll watch for openings inside the house—all for my son." Madam Bao nodded, then added a caution. "Keep every word of this from Anya. She's close with First Sister-in-law, and she seems quite fond of the Zhu girl too. Did you see how warm she was toward her at the tea ceremony?"
"Don't worry, Mother, I know my limits," Chen Haodong said, before a frown returned. "Word has it Haoyu signed a prenuptial contract with the Zhu girl saying they won't meddle in each other's lives. I'm worried she'll leave him alone to keep playing around, wasting half my hard work."
"Not a chance," Chen Jiangshu said. "We still have a trump card. If push comes to shove, we play it, and Haoyu won't escape getting burned."
Chen Haodong paused a beat before lighting up. "You're right, Father! How did I forget something that huge? When the time comes, we spill it, and the Zhu girl will be mad enough to murder Haoyu on the spot."
Madam Bao blinked. "Talking in riddles right in front of me? What is it?"
Chen Haodong leaned over and whispered in her ear. Madam Bao burst into laughter. "So Haoyu was behind that! What a piece of work—not only did he saddle himself with a disgraced bride, he's going to pay for it too. Total disaster for him."
A sour thought crossed Chen Haodong's mind. "Thinking of Haoyu's personal properties sitting in the Zhu girl's hands still irks me. The day I take over the family, I'm taking every last one back."
Chen Jiangshu waved it off. "Compared with the entire Chen fortune, those holdings are a drop in the bucket. Not worth sweating over."
"You're right, Father."
Returning from the Qiantang Gang, Chen Jianglin reported everything to Yao Qingyan.
"So Haoyu got the promissory note back, and the Chen estates are safe," Yao Qingyan mused. "Still, I find it hard to believe Master Jiang gave up the Chen fortune over a courtesan."
"I don't buy it either," Chen Jianglin agreed. "There's more to it. Since he won't talk and we can't force him, as long as Haoyu and the family aren't in danger, let's drop it."
Yao Qingyan nodded. "Fair enough. We shouldn't make enemies of underworld gangs, nor should we get tangled up with them. Better to leave it there. Go report to Old Madam Xia now."
"Aren't you coming with me?"
"I'm afraid seeing us walk in together will only set her off. Better I stay behind."
Chen Jianglin went alone to his mother's courtyard.
With the spring weather turning warm, the old lady still loved soaking up the sun, sitting in the yard watching maids embroider. The second her eldest son stepped in, her smile vanished. Noticing he was alone, she baited him. "Aren't you glued to her hip? Why did you come by yourself?"
"Qingyan meant to come, Mother," Chen Jianglin answered straight. "She stayed back for fear of upsetting you."
That only fanned Old Madam Xia's temper. "Both of you talk a big game about filial piety, yet heaven knows what kind of monster you paint me as in your heads! Afraid of upsetting me? Sounds to me like she's making excuses to stay out of my courtyard!"
At the old madam's outburst, the maids set aside their needles and rushed to soothe her.
"Mother, please don't get angry the moment you lay eyes on me," Chen Jianglin pleaded. "You make me afraid to set foot in here."
Old Madam Xia let out a heavy sigh. "My own fault for teaching you nothing but business, and never teaching you how to run a household or raise a child."
"How could it be your fault, Mother? I'm the one at fault."
She waved the subject away. "What brought you here?"
Only then did Chen Jianglin state his business. "I went to the Qiantang Gang this morning to see Master Jiang. After talking with him, I confirmed Haoyu and the family estates are safe. I gave him a stern warning and came straight back to report to you."
"You didn't stop by your wife's room to tell her first?"
Anxious not to provoke her further, Chen Jianglin fibbed, "Of course I came to you first, Mother."
Old Madam Xia thumped her cane against the paving stones. "Lies roll right off your tongue! One second ago you said your wife meant to come with you and stayed back to avoid upsetting me, and now you claim you came straight to me! Think I've gone senile and will swallow any fib you spin? Hmph!"
Catching his own slip, Chen Jianglin dropped to his knees. "Mother, I didn't mean to lie. I was so scared of making you angry that I tripped over my own words and made it worse. Beat me or scold me all you want—I won't breathe a word of complaint."
Old Madam Xia rapped her cane twice more. "That's the exact attitude I can't stand—acting so polite, docile, and dutiful that you box me in! When will you ever understand what I actually want?"
Chen Jianglin stared up in bewilderment. "Tell me whatever you want, Mother, and I will fulfill it without fail."
"Stop orbiting around your wife all day!" she snapped. "Can you do that?"
Chen Jianglin knelt in silence.
Old Madam Xia sighed again. "You can't do a single thing I ask, so why waste my breath? If you were half as filial as you claim, I wouldn't have to spell it out. Forget it, I'm tired of losing my temper with you. Finish your report and get out of my sight."
Still kneeling, Chen Jianglin spoke. "I suspect there is a hidden story behind Master Jiang's gamble with Haoyu. He refused to talk, and I had no way to press him. Since Haoyu and the estates are safe, I figured we should leave the rest alone."
Her temper cooled a notch. "Any fool can tell there's more behind it. Keep your eyes peeled; the truth will float to the surface sooner or later."
"Yes, Mother. I will keep watch." He stayed on his knees.
"Get up and get out!" she barked.
Chen Jianglin rose, bowed, and took his leave. He could not see what he had done wrong, nor why the sight of him and Qingyan always grated on his mother. Outsiders assumed she resented him doting on his wife, yet he knew that could not be it. When each of the three Chen brothers married, their mother had lectured them to treat their wives well, insisting a man had to cherish his wife to hold onto family wealth. All three Chen brothers were famous across Lin'an for spoiling their wives, Chen Jianglin most of all. He had followed her own rule to the letter; why would she resent him for it?
Try as he might, he could not puzzle it out. In business he kept every ledger crystal clear, yet family affairs left him lost in a fog. He did better than both his younger brothers, so why did his mother always vent her ire on him and drag Qingyan into it too?
Because custom forbade a new bride from spending the night at her maiden home on the return visit—a rule that annoyed Qianli Xun to no end, though ancient tradition left no room to argue—she rode back to Chen Manor with Chen Haoyu before dark.
As their carriage halted outside the main gates of Chen Manor, a waiting party loomed ahead: the madam of Chunxiang Brothel, the three hired fighters, and Scarface with his pack of street cronies.
The moment Chen Haoyu stepped down from the carriage, the whole lot swarmed around him.
#CH 215
#TITLE: Returning from Zhu Manor to Settle Debts
Confronted by the crowd, Chen Haoyu stopped in his tracks. "What are you all doing here?"
Quickest with her tongue, the madam of Chunxiang Brothel stepped up first. "Young Master Chen, word got around that your fierce new wife has locked up your money and cut off your spending. We worried you wouldn't pay up, so we came to collect in person."
One of the three hired fighters spoke next. "You always paid upfront before, Young Master Chen. Yesterday we fought a brawl for you without seeing a coin—look, our faces are still bruised. Isn't it time to settle?"
Chen Haoyu patted his empty sleeves and tried to bluff. "I've been swamped these past few days and haven't had time to draw silver from the shops. Once I finish my errands today, I'll fetch it myself. Go home and stop worrying."
Already tipped off by Chen Haodong, Scarface sidled closer. "Young Master Chen, rumor says every property under your name now belongs to Young Madam. Seems you can't draw silver anymore..."
Spotting Scarface and his crew joining the circus set Chen Haoyu's blood boiling. "What are you lot doing here? Didn't we call it even on your errand money?"
Scarface grinned. "We're not here for silver, Young Master. Just wondering if you're taking us out for fun today."
Being cornered at his own front gate over unpaid bills pushed Chen Haoyu past his limit. "Scram as far as your legs can carry you! I'm in no mood to hang out today!"
Already down from the carriage, Qianli Xun watched the spectacle from the side, growing disgusted at the lowlifes Chen Haoyu kept for company. Still, none of this concerned her—they had a non-interference pact. Turning on her heel, she started through the gates with Kuqiao.
Seeing Chen Haoyu had no silver on him—and hearing Scarface mention that the new wife held his assets—the brothel madam pivoted fast. "Hold on, Young Madam!"
Qianli Xun halted and turned back. "You calling me?"
"Anyone can see you're not the type to leave your husband hanging, Young Madam," the madam wheedled. "Besides, your servants started that brawl with these three men yesterday and smashed up half of Chunxiang Brothel. The damage wasn't cheap. You ought to compensate me."
Qianli Xun eyed the three bruised fighters. "My servants fought you three yesterday?"
"Right here," one fighter confirmed. "Look at the marks still on us."
Qianli Xun turned back to the madam. "If they joined the brawl and broke your furniture, shouldn't you collect from them too? Why come only to me?"
"Young Master Chen promised out loud he'd cover every copper himself," the madam explained.
"If he promised it, go collect from him. Why waste breath on me?" That shut the madam up. Satisfied, Qianli Xun added, "Since my people took part in the fight, I'll pay half the damages. Tally it up. How much silver?"
The madam broke into a bright smile, glad to salvage what she could. "How generous of you, Young Madam! I tallied it up—twenty-five taels of silver in total. What do you say?"
Qianli Xun did not blink. "I'll give you fifteen taels. Collect the other ten from these three."
"Many thanks, Young Madam!" The madam pushed her luck. "One more thing: Young Master Chen spent a full day and night in Chun Jinmo's room. Our girls can't entertain young lords for free, so that comes to another ten taels of silver..."
Qianli Xun bristled, reminded once again what a dud she had married. "Was I the one hiring courtesans? Why are you telling me?"
Done with them, she turned to walk inside.
Chen Haoyu stepped into her path, dropping his voice. "Do me a favor and help me send them packing."
"Pay your own debts," she murmured back, sidestepping him.
He blocked her again, swallowing his pride to whisper, "What if I take an advance on my monthly allowance?"
That sounded workable. "How much did you promise those three?"
"Ten taels each."
"Ten taels? Think Chen silver blows in on the wind?"
"Ten taels is cheap! I used to pay Lu Yuanfeng fifty!"
Qianli Xun raised a fist, then checked herself midair. With half the street watching, he was still her husband on paper; no sense humiliating him further or leaving creditors camped outside the Chen gates. "Thirty taels plus the fifteen makes forty-five..."
"Plus ten for the courtesan," Chen Haoyu muttered.
Her fist twitched again before she forced it down. "Fifty-five taels, every single copper docked from your allowance."
"Hey," he haggled under his breath, "fifteen of those taels were your servants' doing. You can't pin that on me."
"Keep haggling and I walk inside right now."
"Fine, fine, you win! Have it your way."
Chen Haoyu cursed himself all over again for signing away everything just to get that gambling slip back. Overnight his life had plunged from heaven to mud, boxed in on every side. He needed a way out, fast.
Qianli Xun sent Kuqiao inside for fifty-five taels of silver and paid off the three fighters and the madam on the spot.
"Listen well, all of you," she announced before leaving. "From today on, any debt Chen Haoyu runs up is none of my concern." Leaving him standing there, she marched inside with Kuqiao.
The madam wasted no time stepping up to the three fighters with an open palm. "Now that you've been paid, gentlemen, cough up the ten taels for Chunxiang Brothel's damages."
Knowing the brothel was not to be crossed, the three begrudgingly dug out silver until ten taels sat in her hand, sending her off satisfied.
Chen Haoyu called after the three fighters. "You took my silver, so you're still guarding me whenever I go out."
"We lost over three taels apiece paying for a mess you should've covered," one fighter countered. "Your ten taels bought a month before; now you get half a month."
"Fine, half a month," Chen Haoyu waved it off. "Just show up on call whenever I head out."
"Need us today?"
Empty-pocketed and needing to sort things out indoors, Chen Haoyu shook his head. "Not today. That visit with my wife wore me out. I'll summon you once I've rested."
With a chorus of "See you tomorrow," the three bodyguards left.
Scarface and his crew had lurked nearby sizing up the situation, wondering if Chen Haoyu was still worth leeching off. His funds were tight, sure, yet a starved camel still outweighed a horse. They hurried over before he could slip inside. "Going out for fun, Young Master Chen?"
"Didn't you hear me? I'm tired today. Not going out."
"First time I've ever heard Young Master Chen say he's too tired to party," Scarface baited him. "Don't tell us you're broke and scared to treat the boys?"
Stung where it hurt, Chen Haoyu puffed out his chest. "Do I look like a man short of silver? Wait right outside the gate. I'll be out in a minute to take you all out!"
"That's what we like to hear!" the hoodlums cheered.
Leaving Scarface's gang lounging outside the gates, Chen Haoyu hurried back inside to scare up some cash. His first stop would be Steward Fang at Xinglong Pavilion, where he had always drawn silver from his shops.
Trailing behind him was Qinggua, whom he had barely noticed during the commotion at the gate. Now the boy looked like an eyesore. Chen Haoyu swung a foot at him. "Still tailing me? Get lost!"
Qinggua hopped aside, grinning. "I'm your personal servant. Where else would I go?"
"Get back to Moxiang Courtyard and practice your martial arts! Follow me another step and I'll kick you into next week!"
Qinggua stopped in his tracks, watching his master march toward Xinglong Pavilion, and muttered, "No money makes Young Master's temper even nastier. Better keep my distance."
Inside Xinglong Pavilion, clerks bustled about as usual. Chen Haoyu ignored them and headed straight for Steward Fang, who sat bent over a ledger. "Busy?"
Looking up, Steward Fang rose at once. "Second Young Master! Please, have a seat."
Chen Haoyu plopped into a chair without ceremony. "No fancy errands—you know the drill. Draw me two hundred taels like usual."
Steward Fang stayed on his feet, bowing slightly with a polite smile. "Drawing silver is no trouble at all, Second Young Master. I can handle it right away—provided you bring a written order signed by Young Madam's own hand and stamped with her private seal."
"All that red tape?" Chen Haoyu groaned. "Should never have signed my properties over to her."
Steward Fang kept smiling. "Because you dote on Young Madam, of course you hand her the best of everything. I'm sure she won't make things difficult. Bring her authorization and I'll hand over the silver right away."
Dote on her my foot! I had no choice! Chen Haoyu fumed inside, saying aloud, "Why bother with paper? Just hand me the silver."
"Forgive me, Second Young Master. Eldest Master laid down that rule, and I dare not break it."
Pulling his father's name out left Chen Haoyu stuck. He got up. "Fine, wait here. I'll go get her note."
He stalked out knowing full well he dared not ask Zhu Shuzhen for a copper. Instead, he headed straight for his grandmother—both to stir up trouble for Zhu Shuzhen and to coax out some spending money.
Old Madam Xia had just woken from her nap. Seeing the afternoon sun slanting west, she figured her grandson should be back from Zhu Manor, and right on cue his voice rang out.
"Grandmother! Grandmother! I came to see you!"
Even before he crossed the threshold, the old lady beamed. The moment he stepped inside, her face broke into wrinkles of delight. "You're back! Come sit right beside me!"
Chen Haoyu slid onto the couch and threw an arm around her. "Grandmother, I wish I could hug you like this every single day."
"Sweet-talking me again." She did not believe a word, yet she glowed all the same. "If your father had half your knack for cheering me up, I wouldn't stay mad at him all the time."
Chen Haoyu poured it on thicker. "Father puts all his heart into Chen business and my mother. Only I save all my heart for you, Grandmother."
Glowing from ear to ear, she patted his arm. "Stop hugging me and sit across so we can talk."
Chen Haoyu let go and settled across from her.
"How was the return visit at Zhu Manor?" she asked with concern. "Did they give you a hard time?"
"Landing a son-in-law like me is a blessing the Zhus earned in a past life! They were overjoyed—why on earth would they give me a hard time?"
Old Madam Xia tapped his wrist with a laugh. "More nonsense! Did you forget why your father-in-law forced you to marry the Zhu girl?"
"A real hero doesn't rehash ancient battles. Please drop that subject, Grandmother. If the tigress overhears, I'll never hear the end of it."
Wanting to coax out more silver, Chen Haoyu kept her laughing and switched tracks. "Grandmother, I pray every day for your health and long life. At Zhu Manor today, I even uncovered the longevity secret of their Old Ancestor. Want to hear it?"
Certain her grandson was spinning another yarn, Old Madam Xia played along. "Of course I want to hear it."
Chen Haoyu leaned in, lowering his voice to a conspiratorial whisper. "I wandered around the Zhu Buddhist shrine for ages, and all I heard inside was the clack of a wooden fish drum and chanting. I figured their Old Ancestor must have lived so long from tapping a wooden fish and reciting sutras every day. How about I buy you a wooden fish and a string of prayer beads tomorrow so you can try it too?"
"Enough of that. I'm not cut out for chanting sutras. Just come visit me every day and keep me company. When I'm happy, health and longevity follow on their own."
"Deal! I'll visit you every day." Looking for a way to sic his grandmother on Zhu Shuzhen, Chen Haoyu put on a pitiful face. "Grandmother, you have to help me."
"Help you with what?"
"Zhu Shuzhen bullies me."
"How does she bully you?"
"She dismissed all my maids, took over my courtyard, seized my properties, and put me on a twenty-tael monthly allowance. I'm practically a pauper now! Nobody outside even believes I belong to the Chen family."
Old Madam Xia chuckled. "Your bride has a razor-sharp tongue. Even I can't outtalk her. Besides, who told you to drop to your knees and beg her to marry you, or sign some prenuptial contract? Blame yourself for gambling with Master Jiang and nearly throwing away the entire Chen fortune."
Chen Haoyu blinked. "How did you find out about that?"
"Your new bride told me. Your father and mother know too."
Zhu Shuzhen! Chen Haoyu cursed under his breath. Not a shred of face left for me in front of my own family! Just you wait!
"Grandmother, the gambling was my fault, and I swear I'll never touch it again. Still, you have to put Zhu Shuzhen in her place. She's walking all over me."
"How am I supposed to do that?"
"Find excuses to give her trouble whenever you're free."
"She's your wife now. Giving her trouble means giving you trouble."
"Grandmother, we're husband and wife in name only."
A crease of sorrow crossed Old Madam Xia's brow. "My grandson finally takes a wife, only to live like this. It breaks my heart just thinking about it."
Chen Haoyu hurried to soothe her. "Don't be sad, Grandmother. Sooner or later, I'll marry someone you actually approve of."
"I'm quite satisfied with the bride you have right now," Old Madam Xia said from the heart.
Seeing his plot to stir up his grandmother flop, Chen Haoyu cut straight to the silver. "Grandmother, Zhu Shuzhen won't give me a single coin, and I don't dare ask Father or Mother. Could you spare me a little spending money?"
When it came to her favorite grandson, Old Madam Xia never held back. She had a maid bring a wooden coffer and drew out a five-hundred-tael banknote. "Spend it with care. Not a copper on courtesans or gamblers, understand?"
Overjoyed, Chen Haoyu snatched the note and planted kisses all over his grandmother's cheeks until she beamed from ear to ear. After a few more minutes of banter, he tucked the banknote into his robe, hurried out the front gates, and swept Scarface and his crew off for another round of revelry.
#CH 216
#TITLE: Winning Over Old Madam Xia
Two days later, the manor gardeners finished dredging the silt and weeds from the Moxiang Courtyard pond, laid down rich soil, and planted fresh lotus roots to sprout into leaves and blossoms.
With every corner arranged to her taste, personal assets in her name, kind in-laws, and nobody knocking on her door looking for a fight, Qianli Xun was in high spirits.
She had not laid eyes on Chen Haoyu since the evening of their return visit to Zhu Manor, nor did she care where he gallivanted. What did occupy her mind was winning over Old Madam Xia.
Setting her huaben aside for the moment, she racked her brains for a way to win the old lady over. Having grown up without a grandmother before her transmigration, she had zero experience coaxing elders. She thought of the shameless sweet-talking act Chen Haoyu put on to make Old Madam Xia beam, and shuddered. She had never talked like that to anyone in her life; fawning was out of the question.
Kuqiao walked in to find her mistress not writing, just propping her chin on one hand in deep thought. "What's on your mind, Miss?"
Qianli Xun lowered her hand. "Just in time. Help me figure out how to cheer up Old Madam Xia."
Kuqiao pondered a second. "My father used to say the older folks get, the more they love a lively crowd. Why not come up with something lively for her?"
Qianli Xun brightened at once. "Didn't realize you were this sharp! That's a great idea, and we have the perfect fix ready to go. Why don't we invite the storyteller over to perform for Old Madam Xia?"
"Sounds wonderful, except he might recognize me," Kuqiao worried. "I'm the one who delivers your manuscripts to him every time."
"Send Hongdou instead! When the storyteller comes, you stay here in Moxiang Courtyard and I'll bring Hongdou along to attend me."
"Smart plan, Miss. I'll go find Hongdou right now." Turning to go, Kuqiao paused. "Oh, one more thing I need to ask you about, Miss."
"What is it?"
"Matron Wei told me to teach Hongdou how to prepare whisked tea, so you'll have another maid trained to attend you."
"If Matron Wei suggested it, don't bother asking me. Go ahead and arrange it."
"Yes, Miss."
Heading to the West Courtyard, Kuqiao found Hongdou drilling the household servants in Qianli Fist, with Qinggua trailing off to the side mimicking every stance with hilariously sloppy form. Kuqiao nearly burst out laughing.
Spotting her, Hongdou told the others to keep practicing and walked over. "Sister Kuqiao? Does Miss have an errand for me?"
"Miss wants you to head to West Third Alley, find the storyteller, and invite him to Chen Manor to tell stories for Old Madam Xia."
"Got it. I'll go right away." Hongdou turned back to the group. "Take a break, everyone! Back to your chores, and we'll train again tomorrow."
The servants dispersed and Hongdou hurried off. Left with nobody to copy, Qinggua jogged after Kuqiao.
"Kuqiao, could you put in a word with Young Madam and ask her to teach me martial arts too?"
"Why do you want to learn?" Kuqiao asked as she walked.
Qinggua put on a mournful look. "Young Master ordered me to learn. If I can't pull it off, he'll banish me to the West Courtyard."
Kuqiao halted. "If the young master told you to learn, have him come ask my mistress himself. Don't drag me into your mess."
She walked on, and Qinggua trailed right behind, piling on the pity. "How is it a mess? Help me out, please? You've seen how Young Master kicks me every chance he gets. If Young Madam teaches me a few moves, at least I can dodge his boots."
Half amused and half sympathetic, Kuqiao relented. "Fine, I'll mention it to Miss. Whether she says yes is up to your luck."
Qinggua escorted her back to Suxin Thatched Hall and waited outside full of hope.
Inside, Kuqiao found her mistress seated at the tea table whisking tea with stiff, clumsy strokes.
"If you want tea, Miss, let me make it. No need to tire yourself."
"I've been mulling over Old Madam Xia's business, and my mind isn't settled enough to write or read. Figured making tea would pass the time. After watching you do it every day, I thought I could pull off at least a decent imitation, yet it still came out a total wreck." For the first time, Qianli Xun felt disappointed in herself.
Her words handed Kuqiao the perfect opening. "If you want something fun to pass the time, Miss, I have just the thing."
Qianli Xun set down the tea whisk. "What is it? Spill."
"I just came from the West Courtyard. Guess what I saw?"
"Quit stalling and tell me."
"Qinggua was trying to follow along with the martial arts drills, flailing every which way like a clown. On the way back, he begged me to ask if you'd teach him. I meant to turn him down, yet he looked so pitiful I agreed to ask."
Qianli Xun's eyes lit up and she stood. "He actually wants to learn martial arts? That's hilarious. He doesn't have a single bone made for fighting—he'd have to wait for his next life to master Qianli Fist. Still, I've got time to kill. Might as well use him for entertainment."
Having done her favor, Kuqiao stayed behind to clean up the botched tea while her mistress stepped outside.
Sure enough, Qinggua stood right beyond the threshold, craning his neck indoors. The instant she appeared, he broke into a wide grin. "Young Madam, did you agree to teach me martial arts?"
Qianli Xun looked him up and down. "Why do you want to learn?"
Knowing full well the bad blood between his master and mistress, Qinggua made sure not to mention Chen Haoyu's order. "I see how impressive you are, Young Madam—unafraid of Old Madam Xia above, Master and Madam in the middle, or Young Master below. I just want to pick up a few tricks so I can dodge when Young Master kicks me."
An idea clicked in Qianli Xun's head. "You don't have the build or aptitude for my fist style. Even if you memorized the forms, you'd only scratch the surface. Lucky for you, I know one standalone move that needs zero martial talent. Learn it, and no matter how Chen Haoyu kicks at you, you'll slip right past—and even kick him back if you feel bold."
Disappointment flickered over Qinggua's face; he had dreamed of mastering real kung fu and swaggering down the street behind his master. No such luck. Settling for second best, one self-defense move beat getting bruised black and blue. "I'll learn it, Young Madam! Teach me!"
"Kick me," Qianli Xun ordered.
Qinggua paled. "How could I dare kick you, Young Madam?"
"I told you to kick, so kick! How else can I demonstrate?"
Steeling his nerve, Qinggua aimed a kick straight at her.
In a flash, Qianli Xun slid sideways, angling her torso so his boot sliced empty air right past her hip. The momentum pulled him off balance for a split second—all she needed to step inside, brush his leg aside with one hand, and lock his arm behind his back with the other.
"This move is called Phantom Sidestep," she said, releasing him. "Catch all that?"
Qinggua shook his head blankly. "Moved too fast for me."
She had him kick at her seven or eight more times until he grasped the footwork, then called Asheng over to drill him in the West Courtyard.
No sooner had she settled that than Hongdou returned. "Miss, I've brought the storyteller from West Third Alley. He's waiting outside the courtyard."
"Bring him along. We're going to Old Madam Xia's."
Glad for the chance to accompany her mistress—a duty that usually went to Kuqiao—Hongdou asked, "Isn't Sister Kuqiao coming?"
"I gave her another task. You're with me today." Qianli Xun led the way out, with Hongdou trailing behind.
Spotting the young mistress emerge, the storyteller bowed low. "Young Madam, where would you like me to perform?"
"Just follow me."
Qianli Xun walked ahead while Hongdou guided the storyteller behind her. At the gate of Old Madam Xia's courtyard, Qianli Xun told them to wait outside and entered alone.
Maids were helping Old Madam Xia stroll around the yard. Barely a few steps in, the old lady spotted her granddaughter-in-law and barked, "What utter lack of manners, barging in without being announced! And they call you the Poetess of Jiangnan!"
Qianli Xun had hyped herself up to try a softer tone like Chen Haoyu's, only for that sharp rebuke to puncture her resolve on the spot. "Since I am the Poetess of Jiangnan, my tolerance runs wider than most. Even if you find me an eyesore, Grandmother, I won't hold it against you. I brought someone today to liven up this quiet courtyard of yours."
Ignoring the old lady's scowl, Qianli Xun directed the maids on the spot. "You two, carry a table out here. You, fetch a chair."
Seeing Old Madam Xia glare without stopping her, the maids realized she did not mind and hopped to work.
Once the table and chair stood ready, Qianli Xun stepped up to take the old lady's arm. "No matter how upset you are with me, Grandmother, set your temper aside for today and sit."
Watching her granddaughter-in-law bustle around ordering things set up made the courtyard feel alive, warming Old Madam Xia's heart even as her mouth stayed sharp. "Fine, I'll sit and see what trick you're pulling."
Once the old madam was settled, Qianli Xun called toward the gate, "Hongdou, bring the storyteller in!"
At the word "storyteller," Old Madam Xia's stern mask cracked, and the corners of her mouth curved up.
Catching that flicker out of the corner of her eye, Qianli Xun grinned inside. That easy to win over? Smooth sailing in Chen Manor from here on out.
Hongdou ushered the storyteller inside. Sighting the table, he looked right at home, pulling his wooden clapper and folding fan from his cloth satchel, followed by a purple clay teapot. He handed the pot to a nearby maid. "Trouble you for a fresh brew of tea, miss."
Old Madam Xia's smile widened. "Brew the master our finest tea."
The maid hurried off with the pot. Old Madam Xia glanced at Qianli Xun standing beside her. "You sit down too."
Another maid scrambled off for a second chair.
Taking her seat, Qianli Xun said, "Grandmother, the tale he's telling today was written by Master Qianli Xun. You're guaranteed to love it."
"Thoughtful of you," Old Madam Xia said, her voice much softer.
When the tea came back, the storyteller took a sip—fragrant and just the right temperature. Satisfied, he set the pot down and launched into his opener. "Who doesn't know that Chen Manor brims with splendor, wealth, and honor overflowing the halls, and that Old Madam Xia enjoys both fortune and longevity, revered by all—"
Old Madam Xia cut him off with a laugh. "Skip the flattery and get to the story. Tell it well, and I'll reward you handsomely."
"Your wish is my command, Old Madam!" He slapped his wooden clapper sharp against the tabletop.
"Now, at the southern edge of heaven stood a Glazed Pagoda, trapping a jade-faced fox spirit for a full thousand years. That fox didn't sit idle—she made the pagoda her home, fed on pagoda mist, dressed in pagoda dust, until every bit of her reeked of pure pagoda-ishness..."
Old Madam Xia burst out laughing. "Heh! First time I've heard 'pagoda-ishness'! What a hoot!"
The storyteller rolled right on.
"At the southern edge of heaven also dwelt a true deity named Xuanzhi. Every day after his cultivation, he gazed upon the Glazed Pagoda. Because Xuanzhi was a supreme god, his piercing gaze carried immense divine power. Day after day, the pagoda soaked up that energy until it could hold no more. With a thunderous boom across the southern skies, the Glazed Pagoda shattered to pieces! Free at last, the jade-faced fox touched a wisp of immortal aura and took human shape as a young woman—though after a millennium indoors, she stood coated in dust, unkempt and disheveled.
"Right then, Xuanzhi appeared. Startled out of her wits, the girl demanded, 'Who are you? Decent-looking face, just way too grim and uncheerful!'
"Xuanzhi swept one immortal sleeve, whisking every speck of dust off her and turning her into a stunning beauty on the spot. 'I saved you,' Xuanzhi announced. 'From now on, you belong to me.'
"Delighted to look gorgeous yet furious at his nerve, the girl snapped back, 'Says who you saved me? I didn't see a thing!' With that, she turned to bolt, only for Xuanzhi to reel her straight back with his divine power..."
Old Madam Xia laughed nonstop. Not since her sons had grown up and started their own families had anyone made her this happy.
