Word soon spread that a storyteller was performing inside Chen Manor. Chen Haofei hurried over to listen, followed by Second Aunt Madam Bao, Third Aunt Madam Cui, and Ye Anya. The courtyard filled with chatter and laughter, and Old Madam Xia could not have been more thrilled.
#CH 217
#TITLE: Guessing an Elder's Mind
After the storyteller wrapped up and left, Old Madam Xia leaned back in pure contentment and laid down an order. "One session isn't nearly enough. Have him come every day from now on."
"Don't worry, Grandmother," Qianli Xun said. "I already arranged for him to come at this same hour every day."
Old Madam Xia beamed from ear to ear.
Second Aunt Madam Bao chimed in with a tart edge. "Who would have guessed Haoyu's bride is even better at buttering up Old Madam Xia than Haoyu himself? I do envy Eldest Sister-in-law for landing such a clever daughter-in-law, unlike the one in my house..." She shot a glare at Ye Anya beside her. "Take a lesson from Shuzhen! Otherwise what use was Haodong marrying you?"
Ye Anya kept her head lowered and said nothing. Old Madam Xia frowned at the jab, yet held her tongue.
Qianli Xun stayed quiet and watched. Having binged enough household-intrigue dramas in her previous life, she knew inner-courtyard politics required brains, not fists.
Madam Cui and her son Chen Haofei treated the scolding as routine background noise; they had watched Madam Bao pick on Ye Anya too many times to blink.
Madam Bao pressed on. "The trouble is, my Haodong slaves from dawn till dusk across the Chen silk house, tea house, salt shop, porcelain warehouse, docks, inns, and taverns—unlike Haoyu and his wife, who have all the time in the world to hang around amusing Old Madam Xia. Don't favor one at the expense of the other, Mother, or Haodong will be heartbroken."
Catching the barbed hint, Old Madam Xia let her smile drop. "Save your reminders. I keep a clear scale in my head. Who has character, who has ability, and who contributes most to the Chen household—I see every bit of it crystal clear."
Not wanting Second Sister-in-law to claim all the credit for her son, Madam Cui jumped right in. "My Haofei can't compare with either of his older cousins—one schemes for business, the other knows how to play and win Grandmother's favor. He puts his whole heart into his books instead. Next year he sits the metropolitan examination, and once he passes, he'll ride to Bianliang for the palace exam! Haofei, recite a passage from the *Erya* for your grandmother."
"No need," Old Madam Xia waved it off. "Even if he recites it, I won't understand a word. Haofei is a good boy. Study hard and bring an official title back to the Chen family—that will be the greatest contribution of all."
"Yes, Grandmother," Chen Haofei answered.
Old Madam Xia dismissed the crowd. "All of you head back now. I want a private word with Haoyu's wife."
Watching them file out, Qianli Xun weighed the veiled competition from Second and Third Aunts. Suddenly it made total sense why Old Madam Xia doted on Chen Haoyu despite his good-for-nothing streak: everyone else came with an agenda, while he just showed up to make her laugh.
Once the yard cleared, Old Madam Xia held out her hand. "Shuzhen, help me inside."
Supporting her across the threshold, Qianli Xun noticed how stiff and unsteady the old woman's legs felt. Compared with the Old Ancestor back at Zhu Manor, who moved sprightly at a hundred and twenty, Old Madam Xia seemed frail indeed, stirring a sudden rush of pity and fondness in Qianli Xun's chest. "Grandmother, Chen Haoyu mentioned a physician who treats leg ailments. I'll track him down for you one of these days."
Old Madam Xia chuckled. "You believe anything Haoyu says? He only says that to cheer me up. With the Chen fortune, what physician couldn't we hire? We've tried them all; my legs are beyond curing."
Puzzled, Qianli Xun settled Old Madam Xia onto the kang couch, kicked off her own shoes, and sat cross-legged opposite her. "So you aren't angry with me anymore, Grandmother?"
Old Madam Xia laughed. "When was I ever angry with you?"
Qianli Xun froze a second before the penny dropped. "You were pretending to be mad the whole time? Had I known that, I wouldn't have worked so hard trying to win you over!"
"In this entire manor, I've at last found one person with sense," Old Madam Xia said. "You realized I picked fights and fumed just to get you to fuss over me. None of my sons or daughters-in-law ever figured out what I wanted."
Everything clicked at once. Every time Old Madam Xia snapped at Chen Haoyu's parents, she was only fishing for attention and affection—yet it had driven them away instead. Qianli Xun had stumbled onto the answer by sheer accident.
"You ought to just tell the elders and us younger ones straight out what you like, Grandmother."
Old Madam Xia sighed. "Where's the joy in spelling it out? Anyone who truly cares finds out on their own—just like you, putting real thought into cheering me up. From now on, I have your back in Chen Manor. Nobody dares bully you."
Qianli Xun grinned wickedly. "Since marrying in, Grandmother, nobody except you has even tried to bully me. Not that I'd care—my fists do plenty of talking."
"Oh, listen to you brag!" Old Madam Xia laughed, her eyes crinkling. "Still, I like a girl like you—fearless of heaven and earth, just like me when I was young. Pity I'm old now."
"You're teasing me, Grandmother," Qianli Xun said, half pleased and half skeptical. "You looked furious with me when I came raising a storm before the wedding, and at the tea ceremony too. How could you possibly have liked me?"
"You made it obvious that day you didn't want to marry Haoyu, so why would I beam and welcome you? Putting on a smiling face while you raged wouldn't have played along with your act! Besides, I worried you'd actually beat my grandson black and blue once you married in, so I figured I might as well help you blow up the engagement."
Qianli Xun marveled at how deep Old Madam Xia's waters ran—one face outside, another inside. No wonder her own sons and daughters-in-law stayed baffled.
Thinking back to that pre-wedding showdown reminded Qianli Xun of one slip Old Madam Xia had let drop. "Grandmother, that day you mentioned my father forced Chen Haoyu to marry me. Why did he do that?"
Old Madam Xia stiffened at once. Her granddaughter-in-law still did not know—meaning Prefect Zhu had kept it under wraps. She cursed her own loose tongue from that day. With this girl's temper and fists, spilling the truth now would doom Haoyu for good. She scrambled to cover it up. "Nothing of the sort! You had me so flustered that day I just blabbed nonsense. Don't give it another thought."
Seeing no crack in the old lady's poker face, Qianli Xun let it drop. "Grandmother, how did you like Master Qianli Xun's story?"
"Marvelous! Best tale I've heard in years—never heard anything so fresh!" Old Madam Xia beamed, then tilted her head. "You called the author a male scholar, yet why does it sound to me like a young woman wrote it?"
Delighted by the praise—every last trace of her pre-transmigration cyberbullying blues gone—Qianli Xun also marveled at the old woman's sharp nose. "What makes you think that?"
"A story all about a young lady's feelings? Had to be written by a woman," Old Madam Xia declared with conviction.
Qianli Xun had a comeback ready in a heartbeat. "Not necessarily! The Tang Poet Immortal Li Bai wrote 'Qingping Yue,' the Poet Sage Du Fu wrote 'The Fair Lady,' and the Poet Buddha Wang Wei wrote 'Song of the Daughter of Luoyang'—every one about women, yet all three were red-blooded men!"
Impressed by her granddaughter-in-law's learning, Old Madam Xia beamed all the brighter, wondering what karma her book-hating grandson had piled up in a past life to land such a gifted bride. "Worthy of the title Poetess of Jiangnan! You know everything."
Flushed with pleasure, Qianli Xun thought of another treat. "Grandmother, I know a blind drummer who sings ballads. I'll invite him over one of these days to perform the tale of Cai Bojie for you."
Old Madam Xia bobbed her head like a pellet drum. "Yes, yes, yes!"
The two talked right up until noon, when Qianli Xun excused herself so the old lady could eat.
Barely a few paces beyond Old Madam Xia's gate with Hongdou in tow, Chen Haofei stepped into her path. "Greetings, Second Sister-in-law!"
Qianli Xun recognized him from the courtyard. "Third Brother-in-law. Need something?"
"I have a favor to beg of you, Second Sister-in-law. Please don't turn me down!"
"Tell me what it is first, and I'll weigh whether to say yes." Hardly knowing him, she would not sign any blank checks.
"I passed the provincial exams, yet my poetry is weak and nobody in Lin'an knows my name. Whenever I'm invited to poetry gatherings, I just tag along to fill a seat because I never have fresh verses to present. Now that the renowned Youqi Jushi is my second sister-in-law, could you spare me a poem or two so I can shine at the next salon? Please?"
Promoting Zhu Shuzhen's poetry was right up her alley. "I thought it was something tough! Writing a couple of lyrics is nothing. Come ask anytime you need more."
"I'd love them right now!"
"Follow me, then."
Thrilled, Chen Haofei trailed her straight toward Moxiang Courtyard.
Approaching the gate, they spotted Ye Anya waiting outside.
"Eldest Sister-in-law, why are you standing out here without even a maid?" Qianli Xun asked.
"I was waiting for you, hoping we could chat," Ye Anya said. "What brings Haofei along?"
"Greetings, Eldest Sister-in-law," Chen Haofei said. "Second Sister-in-law promised to gift me two lyrics, so I came to fetch them."
"So that's it," Ye Anya smiled. "Having the celebrated Youqi Jushi marry into the Chen household certainly smooths your path—no more showing up empty-handed to poetry banquets."
"Of course! We're family now," Chen Haofei laughed.
Chatting and laughing, they stepped into Moxiang Courtyard, where Kuqiao had already laid out lunch. Setting aside the meal for a moment, Qianli Xun sat at her desk and penned two spring lyrics for Chen Haofei: "Xijiang Yue: Mid-Spring" and "Dielianhua: Bidding Farewell to Spring."
Accepting the crisp sheets with effusive thanks, Chen Haofei hurried off on cloud nine.
Since lunch sat steaming on the table, Qianli Xun invited Ye Anya to dine with her. Having waited right at mealtime, Ye Anya could not refuse and took a seat.
The spread was modest—four dishes, one soup, and rice. By now Qianli Xun had grown used to Song meals and found far more interest in her sister-in-law, wondering what had brought Ye Anya to her door.
Kuqiao ladled soup and served rice for both women before stepping back to attend them.
"You're the only woman near my age in Chen Manor, Eldest Sister-in-law," Qianli Xun said. "We should spend time together often."
Ye Anya raised her spoon for a sip of soup without standing on ceremony. "My thoughts exact—that's why I came over to talk today."
Qianli Xun placed a morsel of food into Ye Anya's bowl. "I just married in and don't know half the ins and outs of this household. Fill me in whenever you have time, so I don't put my foot in my mouth and upset the elders."
"Word is you never wanted to marry into the Chen family," Ye Anya remarked, "didn't consummate the marriage on your wedding night, and don't care where Haoyu wanders off to. Yet you still put real effort into pleasing the elders. I can't quite figure you out."
"I didn't want to marry Chen Haoyu, true, yet fate twisted my arm and here I am. Since I married in, I treat myself as part of the Chen family and mean to live here in peace."
Those words—"fate twisted my arm"—reminded Ye Anya of Prince Yi raiding the wedding procession. Prince Yi himself had wanted the Zhu daughter; why had the Zhu family turned down a prince to hand her to a rake like Haoyu?
She let out a quiet sigh. "Outsiders think you and I hit the jackpot marrying into the Chen family, yet only we know the bitter along with the sweet. Haoyu stays out day and night, heaven knows where, and while my husband looks respectable on the surface, in truth..."
Ye Anya bit off the rest of the sentence. Hearing the ache behind her words, Qianli Xun placed another dish in her bowl. "Try this. It's pretty good."
After a bite, Ye Anya went on. "I envy you, Sister—tough, independent, holding Haoyu's properties in your hands, remaking this courtyard just the way you please. Whereas I..."
"I only got away with that because Father-in-law and Mother-in-law are decent people, the elders indulge me, and I have a sister-in-law like you willing to speak from the heart," Qianli Xun said.
Ye Anya burst into a laugh. "What a honeyed tongue—no wonder you won Old Madam Xia over so fast! I heard that when Old Madam Xia marched in with my mother-in-law and Third Aunt to pick a fight, you argued them into a corner with solid logic until none of the three had a comeback left. That mouth of yours doesn't just compose fine verses—it shoots out airtight arguments too. Impressive!"
"You flatter me, Eldest Sister-in-law."
Laughing over lunch, Qianli Xun felt the warmth of a large, lively family—spoiled only by the wrong husband. If only Chen Haoyu were the man she actually loved.
That thought drifted straight to Shen Jianchen. He and Mengrong had to be on the road to Bianliang right now. When they returned to Lin'an, everyone would address Mengrong as Princess Consort Yi.
In silence, Qianli Xun sent them her blessing, wishing Prince Yi and his princess consort a happy life together.
#CH 218
#TITLE: Poor, Pitiful Ye Anya
After lunch in Moxiang Courtyard and another long chat with Shuzhen, Ye Anya took her leave with reluctance.
Beyond being close in age, her new sister-in-law's life fascinated and inspired her. Going through a mutual divorce would have crushed most women's pride, and enduring an entire city's slander last year would have driven ordinary gentlewomen to throw themselves into the Qiantang River ten times over—yet Shuzhen had come through thriving. Betrothed to Haoyu, she had thrown ladylike decorum to the wind and stormed Chen Manor to break off the match; even in failure, her nerve commanded awe.
Then on her wedding day, Prince Yi had raided the bridal procession and Princess Jiahe had taken a blade over it, making Shuzhen even more remarkable in Ye Anya's eyes—a woman who could inspire that kind of mad devotion even while wed to another.
During this first real talk, Ye Anya had not brought up the question burning inside her. She had watched and listened instead, studying what set Shuzhen apart, hoping some of that courage would rub off on her and push her to take the step she had dreamed of yet feared to attempt. She resolved to visit Moxiang Courtyard often.
The moment Ye Anya stepped back into her own courtyard, however, her mother-in-law Madam Bao stood waiting with a thunderous face.
Ye Anya curtsied. "Mother-in-law, what brings you here?"
"High noon, and instead of eating in your own courtyard, you come strolling back past mealtime! Where have you been gallivanting?"
Stung by the word "gallivanting," Ye Anya swallowed her resentment and forced a smile. "That's a bit harsh, Mother-in-law. I only went to chat with Shuzhen. She pressed me to stay for lunch, and it felt rude to refuse."
"Hmph!" Madam Bao snapped. "Look at Shuzhen—barely married a few days and she already has Old Madam Xia eating out of her hand! And you? Over two years in this house, and how many times have you visited Old Madam Xia, let alone won her over for Haodong? Not to mention that useless belly of yours—still not a sign of an heir! No wonder Haodong has no use for you and hates setting foot in this courtyard."
Unwilling to stand there taking another tongue-lashing, Ye Anya said flatly, "If you have nothing else, Mother-in-law, I'm going inside." Without waiting for dismissal, she walked straight into her room.
Fuming, Madam Bao turned to her maid. "Look at that attitude! Does she act like a meek daughter-in-law? Everyone outside praises her as gentle and dutiful, making me look like some wicked mother-in-law!" With a huff, she stormed away.
Inside, Ye Anya lay down on her bed, simmering in silent misery.
Her maid Xiaocui hurried over. "Miss, I checked our usual spot first thing this morning. Young Master Cao sent a note."
Life sparked back into Ye Anya's eyes. Sitting up, she unfolded the slip and read:
"By Heaven above! I would know you as my own, / Our bond unbroken, undecaying through all our years. / Only when mountains have no peaks, when rivers run dry, / When thunder rumbles in winter, snow falls in summer, / And heaven and earth fuse as one— / Only then would I dare part from you!" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Ye Anya let out a long sigh. "He loves me with all his heart, yet I am another man's wife. How can I ever answer such devotion?"
"You and Young Master Cao loved each other first, Miss," Xiaocui whispered. "You only married the young master because you had no choice. He beats you and curses you—even if you're willing to endure this life, I can't bear watching it anymore!"
"I don't want to live with him either, yet the marriage is done. What can I do?"
"Didn't you want a mutual divorce from him, Miss?"
"I do, yet walking that road isn't easy."
"The new Zhu bride went through a mutual divorce before! Why not ask her how she did it?"
"That's why I went to see her today, yet every time the words reached my lips, I couldn't get them out. I'll find another chance to ask her."
Before Xiaocui could answer, Chen Haodong strode through the doorway. Xiaocui snatched the note from her mistress's fingers, jammed it up her sleeve, and backed to the wall.
Chen Haodong scowled at her. "Getting bolder by the day, aren't you? Don't even bother greeting your master?"
"Greetings, Young Master," Xiaocui stammered.
Ye Anya rose to her feet. "Back early today, Husband?"
"What, annoyed I came back early? Hiding someone in here who doesn't belong? Don't think I don't know about your little affair with that Cao boy before our wedding."
Beneath her fragile frame flared quiet defiance. "I did have feelings for him—that was in the past, and you knew before we married. Why dredge it up now? And since you're lecturing me, let's talk about you. Don't think I haven't heard about Chun Jinchan and the filthy business you carry on with her outside."
Certain a beating was coming, Xiaocui went rigid with terror for her mistress.
"Get out!" Chen Haodong roared at Xiaocui.
Xiaocui dropped to her knees. "Young Master, please don't hit Miss! The bruises from last time haven't even healed!"
"Servants!" Chen Haodong bellowed toward the door. Two men hurried in. "Drag Xiaocui out and shut the door!"
They obeyed at once. Once the door clicked shut behind them, Chen Haodong stalked toward Ye Anya. "Getting mouthier every day. Guess I went too easy on you last time."
Ye Anya edged away. "Think beating me will make me submit? You're wrong. Every blow only makes me despise you more!"
Chen Haodong lunged and grabbed her collar. "Mother just told me you went cozying up to Haoyu's wife. I'm warning you: stay clear of her bad habits! Her name is mud across Lin'an, and you'd better keep yours spotless. One year from now the clan chooses the heir to the Chen fortune—don't you dare ruin my chances!"
Ye Anya spat back without flinching. "You put on a grand act for outsiders, yet I know what kind of rot you are underneath. Someone like you expecting to inherit the Chen estate—"
A stinging slap cut her off mid-sentence. "Keep running that mouth!"
A barrage of blows followed until Ye Anya had no strength left to fight back. He hurled her onto the bed and tore open her robes. "Beating you first and then making you do your wifely duty—nothing feels better!"
Swallowing her humiliation, powerless to resist, Ye Anya endured in silence.
When he finished, Chen Haodong straightened his clothes and sneered down at his bruised, tear-streaked wife in her rumpled garments. "Lying there cold as a slab of stone—no fun at all. No wonder you can't get pregnant! You should take lessons from the courtesans at Chunxiang Brothel on how to please a man."
Only after the door slammed behind him did Ye Anya let her tears spill over.
Xiaocui rushed back in and burst out crying at the sight of her mistress. "Miss, next time just humor him and don't talk back! You're the only one who gets hurt!"
"I can't humor a man I loathe. Bending to him tortures my heart. Better to carry bruises on my skin than rot inside. I'm leaving him—even if he has to write a letter of repudiation to cast me out, I'm ending this marriage."
Seeing firmer resolve in her mistress's eyes than ever before, Xiaocui felt a swell of relief. Her mistress had not known one happy day in Chen Manor; walking out—even alone, even scorned—beat living in this cage.
Over the next few days, the storyteller came to Chen Manor every afternoon, turning Old Madam Xia's once-quiet courtyard into the liveliest spot in the estate.
Ye Anya, however, never showed up. Still new to the household, Qianli Xun refrained from prying or barging into her cousin-in-law's quarters, deciding to wait and see.
She noticed too that neither Yao Qingyan nor Chen Jianglin ever stepped inside. One afternoon, while Old Madam Xia and everyone else sat absorbed in the tale, Qianli Xun slipped out on her own—and found Yao Qingyan pacing beneath a tree just outside the gate with her maid Yingxue.
Qianli Xun walked right up. "Mother-in-law, you're right at the gate. Why aren't you going in?"
"I'm afraid seeing me will only upset Old Madam Xia," Yao Qingyan murmured.
Both her father-in-law and mother-in-law were wonderful people, Qianli Xun thought, just clueless about reading the old lady's heart, trapped in years of misunderstanding. Time to lend a hand.
"You're overthinking it, Mother-in-law. Grandmother loves lively company. If you fill her courtyard with warmth and chatter every day, she'll be thrilled—why would she get mad at you?"
Yao Qingyan looked doubtful. "Does that actually work?"
"Of course it works! That's the exact trick I used to get Grandmother grinning ear to ear, and look how her attitude toward me flipped overnight."
"How did you figure out her mind right after arriving, when I've been her daughter-in-law for years and never noticed she craved company?"
"I'm not that clever—Matron Wei pointed it out to me."
"No wonder every well-bred lady in Lin'an envies you for having Matron Wei by your side."
Qianli Xun grinned. "What everyone in Lin'an envies most is how Father-in-law dotes on you, Mother-in-law."
"That silver tongue of yours—no mystery how you won Old Madam Xia over!" After a chuckle, sorrow crept back onto Yao Qingyan's face. "Outsiders envy our bond, yet Old Madam Xia can't stand seeing it."
"Did you ever consider, Mother-in-law, that Grandmother isn't upset because you and Father-in-law are close, just for another reason?"
"Another reason? I can't imagine what."
"I only figured it out after cheering her up. Whenever she acts cross, she isn't really angry—she's just putting on a frown to get attention, hoping we'll fuss over her so she doesn't feel so lonely."
Light dawned across Yao Qingyan's face. "So that's it! No wonder everything your father-in-law and I did backfired and earned us a scolding—and the more she scolded, the more we steered clear! Honestly, why didn't she just say so instead of leaving us misunderstanding her all these years?"
"Grandmother says spelling it out ruins the feeling," Qianli Xun explained. "She wants to feel people making an effort on their own. Elders are just like children—they want to feel noticed and cherished. From now on, if you and Father-in-law visit her every day and keep her courtyard lively, she's bound to beam."
"Now I understand why she spoils Haoyu—not just because he looks like his late grandfather, but because he visits her quarters and sweet-talks her!" Yao Qingyan squeezed Shuzhen's hand. "Marrying you into the Chen family was the best decision we ever made. Barely through the door, and you unlocked Old Madam Xia's heart for me. Thank you."
Good decision for the Chens, maybe—I'm the one who got stuck with the wrong groom! Qianli Xun thought, yet said aloud, "Don't just stand out here talking. Come inside with me. Grandmother's in a great mood; she won't get mad."
Yao Qingyan nodded and followed Shuzhen into the yard, where the storyteller was in full swing:
"Xuanzhi gave the jade-faced fox a name: Luoying. From that day on, Luoying served as a maid in his celestial palace, tending to his meals and daily needs. Every immortal praised Xuanzhi, supreme in power, deferred to even by the Heavenly Sovereign—yet Luoying couldn't stand him because he stayed icy and stone-faced, dull as dishwater.
"Each day after her chores, Luoying liked sitting by the lotus pond chatting to a single lotus flower. One afternoon she sighed to the bloom, 'Oh, lotus, serving that supreme deity is a bore. I want to slip out of this palace and explore the world.'
"Out of nowhere, a voice answered, 'I'll take you out.'
"Startled, Luoying gasped, 'Who said that?' She looked all around and saw nobody. Rising from her rock to leave, she spun around—straight into a handsome young man.
"Stepping back, Luoying asked, 'Who are you?'
"'I'm the lotus who listens to you every day,' the man said. 'Just call me Flower Hunk.'
"Luoying burst out laughing. 'What a tacky name!'"
Absorbed in the story, Old Madam Xia did not notice her eldest daughter-in-law enter. Refusing to disturb her, Yao Qingyan stepped up beside Shuzhen. When Qianli Xun offered her own chair, Yao Qingyan shook her head and gestured for her to sit. Even when a maid brought another chair, Yao Qingyan stayed standing behind Old Madam Xia, resting her hands on the old woman's shoulders to knead them as the story unfolded.
Feeling the touch, Old Madam Xia turned her head just enough to see Yao Qingyan. She spoke no word, yet the smile on her face deepened with quiet contentment.
A boulder lifted from Yao Qingyan's chest. At last she knew the key to her mother-in-law's heart, and she could not wait to tell Chen Jianglin tonight.
Watching from the side, Madam Bao sneered inside: Hmph! Eldest Sister-in-law sure knows how to suck up!
Madam Cui felt a twinge of sourness too, yet shrugged it off and turned back to the story.
#CH 219
#TITLE: Eldest Sister-in-Law's Secret Romance
Once the storyteller finished and left, the younger clan members drifted away as well. Chen Haofei had skipped today's session, so Madam Cui excused herself to check on him. Reluctant to let Yao Qingyan hog Old Madam Xia's ear and outshine her, Madam Bao stayed behind. Seeing Grandmother in good company—and guessing Yao Qingyan had heart-to-heart words to say—Qianli Xun bowed and left.
Together, the two daughters-in-law helped Old Madam Xia indoors. The old lady glowed inside; her courtyard hummed with life, her eldest daughter-in-law had caught on at last, her eldest son would soon follow, and she would never lack company again.
The moment Old Madam Xia sat down, Yao Qingyan dropped to her knees. "Mother, I was wrong all these years to misread your heart. Beat me or scold me however you wish."
Before Old Madam Xia could speak, Madam Bao cut in tartly, "Sister kneeling like that leaves me nowhere to sit."
Ignoring Madam Bao, Old Madam Xia told Yao Qingyan, "Get up. From now on, drop by whenever you have spare time to chat and keep me from getting bored. Don't leave me stranded in this yard forcing me to pick fights just to get noticed."
"Yes, Mother. I'll come keep you company every day." Helped to her feet by Yingxue, Yao Qingyan took a chair in peace.
Madam Bao pouted. "Honestly, Mother, if you wanted company you only had to say so. I'll have Haodong and his father visit every day, and I'll come too."
"Don't stand there—sit," Old Madam Xia said. "So long as you keep me in your hearts, I'm happy."
Madam Bao sat down. Surrounded by two daughters-in-law chatting away, Old Madam Xia felt a rare surge of joy and beamed nonstop.
Having won Old Madam Xia over for good, Qianli Xun felt light on her feet. On paper, everything in Chen Manor was falling into place: she held her own courtyard and assets, had neutralized the manor's biggest boss, enjoyed warm in-laws, and had a friendly sister-in-law in Ye Anya. Life was running smooth. So why did an ache still sit hollow in her chest?
In a subdued mood, she jotted an address on a slip of paper and handed it to Hongdou. "This is Xiaguo's hometown. Go find the old blind ballad singer and ask him to come to Chen Manor whenever he's free—tell him Old Madam Xia would love to hear him sing."
Hongdou took the note and set off, leaving the room hushed. Kuqiao was off somewhere, and Qianli Xun did not care to wonder where. Sitting alone with her own thoughts, the source of her gloom surfaced: she had nobody beside her whom she loved.
Her thoughts drifted to Shen Jianchen, who must be nearing Bianliang by now. Remembering everything between them, she sighed at how crooked her path of love had turned, cursing this era where no one got to choose their own heart.
Kuqiao walked in and spotted her mistress propping her chin on her hand at the desk, staring off in low spirits. "What's on your mind, Miss?"
Skipping the question, Qianli Xun asked, "Where were you?"
"With the storyteller here, I didn't dare show my face, so I went to the West Courtyard to watch Qinggua practice martial arts."
Qianli Xun lowered her hand. "Hardly martial arts—just one self-defense trick I taught him out of pity so Chen Haoyu would quit kicking him. How's he doing?"
"With Asheng sparring against him, he's looking pretty sharp—much cleaner and faster than when you first showed him."
Qianli Xun had no interest in Qinggua's drills. "I want to go see the Divine Willow."
Recognizing her mistress's dark mood, Kuqiao asked no questions, accompanying her out of Moxiang Courtyard and into a carriage bound for the hills. Kuqiao had never quite grasped why her mistress and Prince Yi stayed so attached to that tree. Bai Feng had told her that since Youlin Retreat sat close by, Prince Yi went there all the time, sometimes even talking to himself as if the willow could hear him.
Lost in thought, Kuqiao glanced out the carriage window and spotted Huayu walking alongside Nan Shuang, both headed toward Chen Manor.
"Miss, I just saw Huayu and Nan Shuang! Must be on their way to Chen Manor to ask for your poems."
Qianli Xun told the driver to stop. "Call them over."
Kuqiao leaned out the window. "Nan Shuang! Huayu! Come over here!"
Hearing her call, both servants hurried up to the carriage.
"How did you two bump into each other?" Kuqiao asked.
"My master ordered me to visit Chen Manor every few days from now on to ask for Youqi Jushi's new verses," Nan Shuang explained. "Ran right into Huayu on the road, and it turns out he's on the same errand."
"Come to think of it, you haven't come asking once since Young Master Qiao got back from Bianliang," Kuqiao noted. "Miss is heading out right now, so come back tomorrow. Don't worry—she already wrote plenty, all set for you."
Huayu jumped in at once. "Those are for my master, right? Don't cut our share just because Nan Shuang showed up!"
"My mistress writes fast and well," Kuqiao laughed. "Seven or eight new sheets are already sitting on her desk. You won't lose a single one, and there'll still be extra for Nan Shuang."
Thrilled, both servants watched the Chen carriage roll on.
Having listened in silence the whole time, Qianli Xun now asked, "Nan Shuang used to come asking for my poems all the time?"
"Hardly asking—he used to scavenge the drafts you threw away and try his luck."
"Then why did he stop after Wenyuan returned from Bianliang?"
Kuqiao studied her mistress with care. "Think about it, Miss, and you'll know."
The hint made it clear in an instant. After returning from Bianliang, Wenyuan and she had fallen hard for each other; with love in hand, why cling to scrap poems? Then their romance hit rocks, leaving him in no mood for verses, and after their breakup beneath the Divine Willow, even less so. Now he had sent Nan Shuang back for poetry—proof that Wenyuan had healed at last. That final talk at Zuiyun Tower had given their story a clean, proper closing period.
A smile touched Qianli Xun's lips. Funny how love worked: stepping onto the road together felt ecstatic, yet walking off it with peace could feel light and freeing too.
Then her smile faded. She had wandered onto a secret road with Shen Jianchen and never once felt happy—or rather, never dared feel happy, because even a flicker of joy felt like betrayal. When would this chapter close with a clean period everyone could smile at? She kept trying to draw that period inside her heart, yet stalled halfway every time. Why torture herself? Was she not even allowed to keep the man she loved locked away inside her own heart? Torn between guilt and longing, she left Shen Jianchen right where he was.
Beyond the city gates, the carriage halted at the foot of the hills. Qianli Xun stepped down and walked toward the Divine Willow. A warm spring breeze brushed her cheeks with drifting willow catkins, while more lay scattered underfoot like white down feathers.
Drawing near the great tree, she took in the fresh contrast: if the crimson silk ribbons dressed the Divine Willow in bridal red, the swirling white catkins draped a wedding veil over the bride. The mashup of ancient and modern imagery amused her; a tree a thousand years old straddled both eras anyway and would hardly mind.
Across the pond, young lotus pads floated on the surface, dusted in a thin white coat of catkins, just like the open water between them. Such a scene begged for Zhu Shuzhen's voice, and Qianli Xun recited aloud:
"Whirling through the bright sky like drifting snow, / Floating aloft, refusing to touch the mud and dust. / Soft and clinging by the flowers, snagging a bee's wing, / Wild and reckless on the lane, chasing a horse's hooves. / Pressed to water, turning to duckweed drifting far on waves, / Riding the breeze, shadowless, slipping over low walls. / Gathering in clusters, grieving spring's departure, / Trying to confuse the Lord of Spring on his homeward road." —"Willow Catkins" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Kuqiao clapped the instant she finished. "What a poem, Miss! Especially 'Soft and clinging by the flowers, snagging a bee's wing'—so true to life! I literally just watched a honeybee dive into a wildflower, get tangled in catkins on the petals, and buzz like crazy before shaking free!"
Qianli Xun smiled. Kuqiao's praise only proved how keenly Zhu Shuzhen observed real life. A sudden insight struck her: whether composing Song poetry or writing modern web fiction, staying rooted in real life made all the difference. Back in her old world, the cyberbullying had snowballed because her books drifted further and further from reality. Adapting huaben in this era, she would weave the living texture of Song daily life into every line—even tales of gods and fox spirits should make listeners hear their own lives echoing back.
Still, she had not come here for writing advice; she had come for her heart. Spotting the boulder beneath the willow, she walked over and blew a breath across its top, scattering the coat of catkins clean away. Worried the stone would chill her mistress, Kuqiao spread a handkerchief over it and stood quiet beside her.
Qianli Xun sat with her back against the trunk. Though she had never seen him here, she could picture Shen Jianchen sitting on this exact stone, leaning against this exact bark, wrestling with his own feelings. Remembering Bai Feng trying to train a piglet to climb the tree beneath these very branches brought a lump to her throat—half moved by his refusal to accept the impossible, half amused.
After a pause, she opened her eyes. "Kuqiao, did you know Bai Feng was out here trying to train a piglet to climb the Divine Willow?"
Kuqiao had never seen it firsthand, yet knew the tale. "Bai Feng told me Prince Yi had him buy a piglet and teach it to climb trees. Heaven knows what Prince Yi was thinking—pigs climbing trees! Sounds hilarious."
"It is hilarious. He just wanted to turn the impossible into the possible. Doesn't get that impossible means impossible, yet throws himself into hopeless effort anyway." Qianli Xun closed her eyes again, heavy-hearted.
Knowing everything between her mistress and Prince Yi, Kuqiao caught every layer beneath that sigh.
Qianli Xun sat quiet, daring not pray to the Divine Willow for any romantic miracle—any prayer of hers meant hurting Mengrong. Better to lock her feelings deep down and live out her days as a dutiful Chen daughter-in-law.
At length, stiff from sitting, she stood with Kuqiao to head back.
Barely a few steps down the path, a man and woman walked around from the back hill. She recognized the man first—Cao Ban. About to step out and say hello, she froze at the sight of the woman beside him: Eldest Sister-in-law Ye Anya, clutching a red silk ribbon with a face radiant with joy.
Shocked, Qianli Xun pulled Kuqiao behind a tree trunk and peered out. Cao Ban held Ye Anya's hand as they walked up to the Divine Willow, knotted their red ribbon together onto a branch, and embraced—looking every inch a devoted pair.
"Miss," Kuqiao breathed, "if Eldest Young Master Chen finds out, Eldest Young Madam is doomed!"
"There's bound to be a story behind this," Qianli Xun warned in a whisper. "Remember: take what we saw today to your grave. Never breathe a word to a soul."
"Yes, Miss. My lips are sealed."
Before the couple noticed them, Qianli Xun and Kuqiao slipped back down the path.
Her own romantic woes faded behind a wave of sorrow for Ye Anya. She was far from the only woman in this era barred from being with the man she loved; countless others like Zhu Shuzhen and Ye Anya lived trapped in the same cage. Never had Qianli Xun missed her modern world more—taken for granted when free, priceless once lost. Returning was impossible, so she would treasure whatever was truly hers to hold here.
First came family: loving parents, a brother and sister-in-law, a hundred-and-twenty-year-old Old Ancestor (another year older now), plus a Chen grandmother and kind in-laws. Second came friendship: the three scholars of the poetry salon, Wenyuan as a confidant, Xiangrong, and her bosom friend Mengrong.
Thinking of Mengrong brought back the slap on her wedding day and those words of hatred. Even so, Qianli Xun held no grudge. No matter how Mengrong treated her from here on out, she would remain her one true confidante in this era.
#CH 220
#TITLE: Three Elders Plot Who Holds the Reins
When Chen Jianglin returned from the silk house, Yao Qingyan told him everything that had happened. Enlightenment hit him like a thunderbolt: he had misread his mother for decades, talking about filial duty without grasping what it really meant.
Together, he and Yao Qingyan went straight to call on Old Madam Xia. Though Yao Qingyan had just left her courtyard a short while ago, she came gladly now that the old lady greeted her with warmth.
Expecting her eldest son, Old Madam Xia sat on her couch flipping through a volume of poetry—picking out her granddaughter-in-law's verses and frowning in puzzlement. "Shuzhen's poems read so mournful and brokenhearted. Doesn't match her spirited temper at all."
"You know what Young Madam went through before marrying into our house, Old Madam," a maid beside her observed. "After all that, how could anyone feel cheerful?"
"True enough," Old Madam Xia nodded. "Anyone in her shoes would hurt. Now that she's in the Chen family, things will turn around."
Just then Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan walked in. Old Madam Xia laid the book aside. "Back already? Tiring day?"
Overjoyed—this was the first warm welcome his mother had given him at first sight since Wenxin's birth—Chen Jianglin drew Yao Qingyan forward to bow. "Working for our own family is no hardship, Mother."
As Yao Qingyan bent to curtsy, Old Madam Xia waved her upright. "You already bowed to me today, Qingyan. Sit right here across from me and let him do the bowing."
Yao Qingyan straightened without hesitation and settled opposite her mother-in-law, while Chen Jianglin completed his bow and took a side chair.
"You two got yourselves a gem of a daughter-in-law, and I got a gem of a granddaughter-in-law," Old Madam Xia declared. "If she hadn't bothered reading my mind, you two might never have figured out what I wanted."
"Forgive your unfilial son, Mother," Chen Jianglin said. "I was too thickheaded to understand you and misread you for years. Shuzhen solved it the moment she arrived—which only shames me more for overlooking something so simple as head of the household."
"Stop beating yourself up. Just drop by often to keep my courtyard lively, and I'll be content enough to live a few extra years."
"Whenever business allows, Qingyan and I will come keep you company every day so this yard stays full of cheer—and you'll live to be a hundred!"
Laughter filled the room.
When the chuckles died down, Old Madam Xia sighed. "We've patched things up, yet my grandson is still out gallivanting, never home, and hasn't even consummated his marriage. This can't go on."
Emboldened by the truce, Yao Qingyan spoke up. "If neither you nor I step in to shield him, Mother, the moment Haoyu comes home Jianglin can give him a proper thrashing and lock him inside. Let's see him run wild then!"
Far from taking offense, Old Madam Xia blamed herself. "I spoiled him because he looks just like his late grandfather, and back then he was the only one who knew how to cheer me up. Because you two didn't get me, I flew off the handle whenever Jianglin tried to discipline him. Looking back, I only hurt the boy."
"Not all on you, Mother," Yao Qingyan admitted. "I worried Jianglin would beat him too hard and shielded him too."
Chen Jianglin grinned. "Sounds like you both approve of me cracking down on that rascal?"
"I proposed it, of course I approve!" Yao Qingyan declared. "Discipline him however you like—I won't step in."
Old Madam Xia smiled mysteriously. "I doubt it will even fall to the three of us to whip Haoyu into shape."
Understanding flashed across Yao Qingyan's face. "You mean Shuzhen, Mother?"
"Precisely. She's the ideal candidate."
"Earlier, Qingyan and I asked Shuzhen to handle him only because she wasn't afraid of you blocking her, Mother," Chen Jianglin said. "Now that we're all on the same page and you'll let me discipline him, why dump the burden on Shuzhen?"
"Fair point, yet letting Shuzhen handle him brings three big gains," Old Madam Xia counted off. "First, we already handed her the job, and taking it back looks bad. Second, it saves the three of us the headache. Third, and most important—while she disciplines Haoyu, they'll be rubbing elbows nonstop. Bang two rocks together enough times and sparks are bound to fly!"
That third point won Chen Jianglin over on the spot. "Brilliant as always, Mother. Let's do it."
"One snag," Yao Qingyan fretted. "They signed that prenuptial contract agreeing not to meddle in each other's lives. We told Shuzhen to rein him in, yet she hasn't lifted a finger. Haoyu's been gone for days and she acts like he doesn't exist, focused solely on entertaining you, Mother."
Guilt washed over Old Madam Xia. "My fault. The day of the return visit, he came whining he was broke, sweet-talked me a bit, and I caved and handed him a five-hundred-tael banknote. That's why he vanished again."
Chen Jianglin smiled knowingly. "Don't blame yourself, Mother. That actually proves Shuzhen already has him on a leash without lifting a finger. Steward Fang told me Haoyu tried to draw silver from the ledger that day, and when Fang demanded Shuzhen's written order, Haoyu didn't even dare ask her—so he ran straight to you."
"Transferring his properties to Shuzhen really was the right call," Yao Qingyan agreed.
"Controlling his purse strings is step one," Old Madam Xia chuckled. "Give her time and she'll have him trained."
"No more slipping him silver from now on, Mother!" Yao Qingyan cautioned.
"Not a copper more, I promise."
Just then Chen Jiangshu and Chen Jiangmu walked in together. Briefed by their wives on the old lady's secret craving for attention, neither wanted their eldest brother hogging all the filial credit; bumping into each other at the gate, they came in side by side, bowed to Old Madam Xia, greeted their eldest brother and sister-in-law, and took seats.
"Mother, whenever you want something from now on, don't bottle it up and make us guess," Chen Jiangshu spoke first. "Just say the word—would any of us three sons dare refuse?"
"Exactly, Mother," Chen Jiangmu chimed in. "Eldest Brother, Second Brother, and I spend all day buried in family business and overlook things. We're men, not subtle enough to guess what's turning over in your head."
Any other day, those complaints would have set Old Madam Xia's temper flaring; today she felt too happy to care. "When you three were babies unable to talk, I spent over a year guessing every single grunt and cry from each of you! Now I ask you to guess my mind a little, and you complain?"
All three brothers chorused, "We're glad to guess, Mother!"
Old Madam Xia turned to her second son first. "You raised Haodong into a capable young man, which pleases me—yet you need to teach him how to treat his wife. When each of you married, I told you to cherish your wives, and all three of you listened. Why didn't you pass that lesson to your own son? I hear Haodong beats Anya regularly."
"Rest assured, Mother," Chen Jiangshu promised. "I'll give Haodong a stern talking-to tonight and make him treat Anya better."
She turned next to her third son. "Haofei is a fine lad, devoted to his studies. If he wins an official degree, our ancestors will smile down on us. Still, don't let him bury his nose in books alone—he needs to mingle and learn to handle people. Real learning isn't just on paper; knowing how to converse and deal with people isn't taught in textbooks."
"Wise advice, Mother," Chen Jiangmu agreed. "Actually, he's improved lately. Haoyu's bride gifted him a few poems, and he's been thrilled, heading out to poetry salons every couple of days and mixing with scholars and fellow exam candidates."
Old Madam Xia nodded, even firmer in her belief that they had picked the right bride.
The family chatted until Old Madam Xia grew drowsy, then dispersed to their separate courtyards.
Walking home, Yao Qingyan asked, "Should we send men to fetch Haoyu back?"
"He always vanishes for days at a stretch," Chen Jianglin said. "We never chased him before, and we won't start now. Once his silver runs dry, he'll crawl home on his own, and we'll back Shuzhen up in teaching him a lesson."
Seven or eight days slipped by. Young lotus leaves broke the surface of the Moxiang Courtyard pond, small yet breathing fresh green life into the yard.
All week, Qianli Xun split her time between keeping Old Madam Xia company and writing her huaben, forgetting Chen Haoyu existed—as if she had moved into Chen Manor just to play daughter and granddaughter. Only when Yao Qingyan or Old Madam Xia casually asked whether Haoyu had come home did she remember she was married to the man she detested. Whenever they hinted she should send men to haul him back and straighten him out, she nodded on the outside while rolling her eyes inside. The Chens were great folks, yet they overestimated her willingness to reform a playboy she could not stand. Still, watching the quiet disappointment cross their faces when she did nothing pricked her conscience.
Meanwhile, after seven or eight days of recovery, Master Jiang's wounds had healed enough to get moving. Settling a few gang matters, he figured Chen Haoyu would still be hanging around Chun Jinmo and marched straight into Chunxiang Brothel.
He brought only five men this time; making too big a scene last time had drawn Commandant Lin's troops with nothing to show for it. Keeping the crew small avoided attracting official eyes.
Even with five men, Master Jiang swaggered in with enough menace to turn every head in the room.
The brothel madam hurried over. "Welcome, Master Jiang! Right this way!"
Settling into a table, Master Jiang leered up at Chun Jinmo dancing on stage. "When she finishes, send her down to keep me company."
The madam pulled an apologetic face. "I'm afraid I can't, Master Jiang. Young Master Chen has booked her exclusively."
Master Jiang stood and strode straight over to Chen Haoyu's table.
Chen Haoyu had spotted him walking in—with just five thugs. Backed by his three hired bodyguards, he did not bat an eye, lounging in his chair watching Chun Jinmo dance.
Master Jiang dropped into a seat across from him. "Still coming after Chun Jinmo, Young Master Chen?"
With his promissory note reduced to ash, Chen Haoyu had zero fear left. "Heh! All that fancy gambling trap you laid went up in smoke, huh, Master Jiang? Sure, I married a tigress—too bad for you I have a knack for taming tigresses! She's sweet as a kitten now, lets me roam free, purrs at me every day, gentle as silk. I go wherever I please. Jealous?"
Rage flashed across Master Jiang's face. "Hand Chun Jinmo over right now, Chen Haoyu, or I'll knock your teeth down your throat!"
At his sharp glance, his five thugs closed in. Paid to earn their keep, Chen Haoyu's three hired fighters stepped between them.
Every patron in the hall swiveled toward the showdown, nearby tables scrambling back. On stage, Chun Jinmo froze mid-step, staring down in alarm.
Upstairs, Scarface and his cronies broke off flirting with courtesans and leaned over the balcony rail.
"Another brawl's kicking off," one lackey muttered.
"We should talk Young Master Chen into dropping Chun Jinmo," said another. "Plenty of prettier girls right here in Chunxiang Brothel."
"What do you idiots know?" Scarface scoffed. "Young Master Chen is loyal in love—doesn't switch women on a whim."
"That counts as loyal in love?" the two cronies echoed. "First time hearing that!"
"Shut up and watch the fight!"
Downstairs, fists flew. Chen Haoyu and Master Jiang stayed seated, glaring daggers at each other across the table.
"Level with me," Chen Haoyu demanded. "Did Prefect Zhu grease your palm to set me up and force me to marry his leftover daughter?"
Stunned by that wild guess, Master Jiang decided to roll with it—both to mess with his head and scare him away from Chun Jinmo. "Sharp as a tack, Young Master Chen! Think just because I run in the underworld I don't have friends in office? Prefect Zhu is my backer—who else would I help? He ordered me to straighten you out and told me to warn you: stay out of Chunxiang Brothel and play dutiful husband at home, or you'll regret it!"
Infuriated, Chen Haoyu jumped to his feet. "Big deal, he's the prefect! Who gives him the right to meddle in my private life? I'll come here every single day just to drive him mad—"
He cut himself off as his three fighters sent Master Jiang's men reeling. His temper flipped straight into glee. "Look at your pathetic thugs! Wiped out by my three bodyguards! Hahaha!"
Master Jiang rose too. Seeing his five men battered backward, unwilling to lose more face in public, he barked, "Hold it!"
Both sides broke off. Master Jiang jabbed a finger at Chen Haoyu. "I'll let you off today. Next time your luck runs out!" Turning toward the stage, he roared, "Chun Jinmo! Sooner or later you're mine!"
Before leaving, he stepped up to the three hired fighters and gave them a thumbs-up. "Nice skills, boys. The Qiantang Gang's doors are always open to you. See you around!"
#CH 221
#TITLE: Eldest Cousin Plays His Trump Card
Smarting from the humiliation, Master Jiang needed someone to pay for it, and his mind went straight to Chen Haodong.
Eldest Young Master Chen had hired him to run that elaborate scheme, only for none of it to pan out: Chen Haoyu was not locked down by his fierce bride, leaving Master Jiang looking like a fool. The silver Chen Haodong had paid did not come close to covering his bruised pride.
That afternoon, Chen Haodong was tied up at the silk house, where the Chen family's booming southwest trade demanded his daily attention.
Without warning, a pack of roughnecks barged through the door, terrifying the manager and clerks. Seeing Master Jiang's stormy face at the head of them, Chen Haodong hurried forward. "Master Jiang! What brings you here?"
"If you don't want everyone hearing what you've been up to, Eldest Young Master, find us a quiet spot to talk," Master Jiang growled.
Guessing why he had come, Chen Haodong led him straight back to the rear storeroom. Part of Master Jiang's crew guarded the door while the rest filed inside.
Between tall stacks of silk bolts smelling faintly of sour dye, the door clicked shut—and without a single word, Master Jiang signaled his men to jump Chen Haodong. With no martial skill, Chen Haodong could only curl up and take the beating.
Once he had vented enough, Master Jiang called, "Stop!"
Wincing through the ache, Chen Haodong gasped, "Barging in and beating me without a word—where's the justice in that?"
"Beating you is getting off light!" Master Jiang fumed. "You talked me into rigging that scheme to marry Chen Haoyu to a tigress, swearing she'd cage him so he'd leave Chun Jinmo alone. Instead she doesn't lift a finger, he's lounging in Chunxiang Brothel with Chun Jinmo all day, and I took a beating over it today!"
"Even so, you got paid every tael promised. You didn't lose out."
"Your silver doesn't scratch the surface of my lost face and grief!"
"I want Haoyu caged by that wife just as bad as you do, Master Jiang," Chen Haodong wheezed. "I didn't see them signing a non-interference prenuptial contract coming. Still, I have one trump card left. Give it a few days, and Chun Jinmo will be all yours."
Master Jiang squatted beside him. "Not jerking me around again?"
Chen Haodong beckoned him closer and whispered a few lines into his ear. A wicked grin spread across Master Jiang's face. "So Chen Haoyu was behind that! All right, I'll trust you one more time."
Tossing a bottle of bruise ointment into Chen Haodong's lap, Master Jiang led his men out.
Pocketing the ointment, Chen Haodong hauled himself up, staggered out front to warn the manager and clerks not to breathe a word to the Chen elders, and rode home by carriage.
His bruises were surface-deep; two days of rest saw him mostly mended, and he spent both days figuring out how to slip word of Haoyu's rumor campaign into Zhu Shuzhen's ears.
When a household servant stepped in announcing, "Eldest Young Master, Second Master is back and asks for you," an idea clicked. Chen Haodong beckoned the man closer. "Come here. Got an errand for you."
"At your command, Eldest Young Master."
Chen Haodong pressed a tael of silver into his palm. "Take this. More waiting once it's done."
Grinning, the servant pocketed the silver, listened close as Chen Haodong whispered instructions, and nodded. "Get word to the Moxiang Courtyard servants today," Chen Haodong finished.
"Yes, Eldest Young Master."
After the servant left, Chen Haodong stood to go see his father—just as Ye Anya walked in with Xiaocui behind her.
One look at Ye Anya's cool, unsmiling face set his temper flaring. "Where have you been?"
"Out on the street buying rouge and face powder."
"Hmph! Since when do you wear rouge for me? Sneaking out to doll yourself up for some secret lover?"
Hit right on the mark, Ye Anya felt her heart jolt, yet kept her mask ice-calm. "If you need nothing, I'm going inside."
Ever since their wedding night, she had worn that same frozen detachment, and Chen Haodong hated it with a passion. He had assumed claiming her as his wife would melt her into smiles and sweet compliance; instead, even after consummating the marriage and forcing himself on her countless times, she never showed him an ounce of warmth.
Blocking her path, her very coldness ignited his urge to dominate her. He seized her wrist and barked at Xiaocui, "Get out!"
"Young Master, please don't!" Xiaocui cried.
"Get out!" he snarled.
Too drained to fight, Ye Anya murmured, "Go outside, Xiaocui, and close the door."
Xiaocui retreated and pulled the door shut.
Strangely, her numb obedience killed Chen Haodong's appetite on the spot. He flung her hand away. "Cold as a block of stone in bed. I won't touch you again."
Ye Anya breathed out in relief—only for him to spin back at the threshold, seize her in his arms, and crush his mouth down on hers in a rough, frantic kiss. Gasping for air, unable to shove him off, she stood trapped until he finally pulled back, still holding her tight.
"Chen men always treat their wives well," he muttered against her hair. "Father lectured me to treat you better, yet heaven knows why—every time I look at you, all I want is to possess you. Can't you quit acting like a block of ice? Maybe then I'd treat you better."
Ye Anya answered with dead silence.
He dropped his arms. "Father needs me. I have to go."
The instant the door shut behind him, Ye Anya wiped her lips hard with a silk handkerchief, disgust twisting her face.
Meanwhile, the bribed servant loitered outside Moxiang Courtyard until Asheng walked out, then called him over and pulled him aside. "Got something huge to tell you."
"Huge about what?" Asheng asked.
"About your young madam and Second Young Master."
Asheng turned guarded. "They're doing fine. What about them?"
"Remember all those nasty rumors tearing your young madam apart across Lin'an last year?"
"Pure slander made up by scum! My mistress isn't anything like that."
"Too bad she's still kept in the dark, clueless who spread them."
"You know something?"
"I've worked in Chen Manor for years—of course I know who started it."
"Spill it!" Asheng urged. "I'll help my mistress thrash the blind bastard!"
Glancing both ways to ensure nobody listened, the servant dropped his voice. "It was your own young master—Second Young Master Chen! Shocking, right? The man your young madam married is the very bastard who spread those lies about her."
Asheng stared in disbelief. "Where did you hear that?"
"Overheard Second Master and Eldest Young Master talking. Kept eating at my conscience, so I figured I'd tell someone from Moxiang Courtyard. Lucky I ran into you—feels better getting it off my chest. Got chores to run; see you around."
Frozen in his tracks, Asheng reeled. Even he felt outraged—how would his mistress take it? Should he tell her or keep quiet? Deciding to finish his errand first, he hurried off with a heavy head.
Outside Chen Manor's gates, a Qiao family carriage pulled up. Xiaguo stepped out first, followed by Aimo helping down the blind ballad singer.
Xiaguo took her grandfather's arm. "Grandfather, Miss is married into the Chen family now. Remember to call her Young Madam when we go in."
"You've drilled that into my ears ten times already!" the old man chuckled. "Are the Chens as rich as the Qiaos?"
"In sheer wealth, the Chens are the richest family in Lin'an—the Qiaos can't touch them," Xiaguo explained. "In noble rank, though, the Qiaos are imperial in-laws, so the Chens can't match that."
"Each has its own glory—worlds beyond country folk like us. Mind your place in the Qiao household and live well."
"You aren't ashamed anymore that I married as a concubine?"
"I've made peace with it. What good is grumbling now that you're wed? So long as you're treated well, your parents and I are happy."
"Let's go in. Miss must be waiting."
Having given the storyteller the day off so the blind ballad singer could perform his drum song, Qianli Xun was writing her huaben in Suxin Thatched Hall when a maid announced, "Concubine Xia of the Qiao household has arrived."
Setting down her brush, she had Kuqiao stow her manuscript and stepped out with her to greet the guests.
Meeting them in the courtyard, Qianli Xun stepped forward to take the old man's arm. "Thank you for making the trip, Elder. Few performers still master drum ballads like yours, so I invited you to sing a set for our Old Madam Xia."
"You showed our Xiaguo great kindness, Young Madam. One word from you and this old man comes running."
"Would you like to rest first, or head straight to Grandmother's courtyard?"
"Sat plenty in the carriage already. Let's go sing for Old Madam right away."
"Then I'll walk you over."
"Miss," Xiaguo spoke up, "I'll wait here while you settle him in, then we can chat when you get back."
Qianli Xun nodded and called Hongdou. "Take Concubine Xia inside for tea. I'll be right back."
Hongdou ushered Xiaguo into Suxin Thatched Hall. Instead of sitting right away, Xiaguo wandered around eyeing the furnishings. "Why do the furniture and layout look identical to Wangyue Pavilion? Felt like I stepped right back into Miss's old room!"
Seated at the tea table scooping tea powder, Hongdou explained, "It didn't look like this at first. After the wedding, Miss had everything redone to her taste and hauled all her furniture over from Wangyue Pavilion."
"No wonder." Watching Hongdou work, Xiaguo raised an eyebrow. "You learned to whisk tea too?"
"Matron Wei told Sister Kuqiao to teach me. She said Sister Kuqiao will marry sooner or later, so once she weds Guard Bai, I can step up to attend Miss."
Xiaguo settled onto the cushion opposite the tea table. "Passable form, though you need more practice."
"I'm just starting out, still clumsy, yet Miss says it tastes pretty good."
"Serving Miss brings good fortune," Xiaguo mused with pride. "Look at me—half a mistress in Qiao Manor, married to the man I love. Even Kuqiao will marry Prince Yi's personal guard, way better than some rich family's male servant. Do you know Youfan?"
"Never met her."
"How about Nan Shuang?"
"Him I know. He came yesterday to collect Miss's poems for Young Master Qiao."
Xiaguo curled her lip in scorn. "Youfan is Miao Zhiyu's maid—nowhere near as lucky as us. One Nan Shuang has her spinning in circles, and she's thrilled about it."
Finishing the whisking, Hongdou presented the bowl. "Your tea, Concubine Xia."
Xiaguo took a sip. "Not bad at all." She turned to Aimo behind her. "You learn whisked tea too when we get back."
"Yes, Concubine Xia—I'll need you to teach me!" Aimo beamed.
"Naturally. I may not rival Yongtai Teahouse's tea master, yet I outshine Kuqiao—the student surpassing the teacher! Stay loyal to me and I'll arrange a far better match for you than that Youfan."
"Thank you, Concubine Xia!"
Just then Qianli Xun walked back in. "What's got you all grinning?"
Setting down her tea bowl, Xiaguo rose eagerly. "You're back, Miss! I have so much to tell you!"
Without even hearing a word, Qianli Xun already knew she was in for a pile of Qiao Manor gossip. Uninterested, yet out of respect for the blind grandfather, she braced herself to sit through Xiaguo's chatter.
#CH 222
#TITLE: A Tedious Chat with Xiaguo
Chatting with Xiaguo promised to be dull enough; doing it indoors without scenery would make it unbearable. "Let's talk outside under the hehuan tree."
Matron Wei had already placed a rattan chair and wooden table beneath the silk tree. Hongdou brought out an extra chair for Xiaguo, Kuqiao set down a platter of seasonal fruit and stood by to attend, and Hongdou slipped off to the West Courtyard to watch Qinggua drill—by now he had the move down well enough to dodge his master's kicks with ease.
Though summer had not officially started, the midday sun already carried heat. Sitting in the dappled shade beneath the leaves spared a woman's delicate skin while letting gentle shafts of sunlight through, pleasing Xiaguo immensely.
She began by praising the yard. "I noticed the courtyard on your wedding day, Miss—not half as lovely as this, without a single tree if I recall right. Now look at it: your favorite chrysanthemums everywhere, lotus sprouting in the pond, and this lush hehuan tree. Yard and hall alike scream that you rule the roost here."
"Chen Haoyu promised me this courtyard before the wedding. Since it's my turf, I fix it up how I like—why stare at an eyesore every day?"
Xiaguo sighed. "Nothing beats being a primary wife. A concubine like me in Qiao Manor could never dream of treatment like this."
"At least you married the man you love," Qianli Xun offered.
That hit the sweet spot. Formidable and admired as her former mistress was, she was stuck married to someone she couldn't stand, making Xiaguo feel far luckier by comparison. A pleased smile curved her lips. "There's one thing you ought to be happy for me about."
Leaning back in the rattan chair with eyes closed, soaking in the fresh scent of leaves and warm patches of sun, Qianli Xun asked, "What's that?"
"Wenyuan has consummated our marriage," Xiaguo confessed with a shy flush.
Without opening her eyes, Qianli Xun let a small smile tug at her mouth. "Sharing bedroom secrets right in front of me? Have you no shame?"
"I treat you like family, Miss, or I wouldn't say it. Mind you, Wenyuan also consummated his marriage with Miao Zhiyu—fair as a level bowl of water. Miao Zhiyu acts a bit nicer toward me now, though her words still pack hidden thorns."
With zero interest in another household's drama, Qianli Xun stayed silent.
"You wouldn't believe it, Miss," Xiaguo pressed on. "Miao Zhiyu plays the poised gentlewoman outside, yet her heart isn't half so pretty. Every few days she finds an excuse to pick on me. Lucky for me I'm no fool, and Wenyuan mostly sides with me, so she can't touch me."
Feeling obliged to say something, Qianli Xun spoke with her eyes still shut. "Good or bad in a person is hard to judge—depends where you stand. At poetry salons, Miao Zhiyu struck me as gracious and cultured; during last year's rumors, she spoke up for me behind closed doors, so I thought well of her. As for now, I don't see her daily like you do, so I won't jump to conclusions."
Taking that as validation, Xiaguo warmed up even more. "She may share salon memories with you, Miss, yet she doesn't truly like you. When Wenyuan sent Nan Shuang every few days to ask for your poems, Miao Zhiyu fumed—even grilled Wenyuan yesterday asking if he still wasn't over Youqi Jushi!"
Dismayed that a handful of Zhu Shuzhen's lyrics were stirring trouble in Wenyuan's home, Qianli Xun opened her eyes and gazed across the garden, trying to tune out the noise.
Xiaguo rolled right on. "Wenyuan told her he's moved on, and I believe him, yet Miao Zhiyu doesn't. She demanded he stop sending Nan Shuang for poems, and he refused. Given our bond, of course I backed Wenyuan, so lately he's been spending most nights in my room! Miao Zhiyu is livid—and the madder she gets, the happier I am."
Staring up at the new spring shoots on the hehuan branches, Qianli Xun tried to picture them in bloom, yet every word of Xiaguo's chatter drilled straight through her ears. She wanted this visit over. "Xiaguo, pass word to Wenyuan for me: tell him to stop sending Nan Shuang for poems."
"No way!" Xiaguo refused on the spot. "That's his one hobby—I won't spoil his mood!"
"Then next time Nan Shuang shows up, I just won't give him any." Qianli Xun shut her eyes again.
"No way!" Xiaguo protested again. "You've always lived free and unbothered, Miss—why let Miao Zhiyu dictate what you do? Just proves she was faking her love of your poems all along! Guess who she's chummy with these days?"
On second thought, Qianli Xun realized Wenyuan was hardly the only person requesting her verses, yet no other household fought over them. The fault lay in human jealousy, not Zhu Shuzhen's poetry. Someone looking for offense could twist anything; her own conscience was clear—she was only passing along Zhu Shuzhen's literary legacy, not secret love notes. She dropped the idea of cutting Nan Shuang off and tossed out one word: "Who?"
"Lin Jue's wife, Bai Meiruo!" Xiaguo wrinkled her nose at Lin Jue's name, still remembering how he had groped and tied her up when she was a maid. "She visits Qiao Manor constantly, and Miao Zhiyu even drags her around to meet all her close lady friends. Don't ask me why, I can't stand Bai Meiruo—whereas Lin Jue's new concubine actually seems decent, even from one meeting."
Qianli Xun's eyes popped open. Barely ten days had passed since she bumped into Bai Meiruo, and Lin Jue already had a concubine? "Don't tell me it's Chun Jinqiao from Chunxiang Brothel?"
Xiaguo gasped. "You heard already? Word travels fast!"
Fast? I'm the one who handed her the playbook! Qianli Xun thought.
"Bai Meiruo may be awful, yet she dotes on Lin Jue," Xiaguo went on. "Used her own dowry money three days ago to buy Chun Jinqiao's freedom and install her as his concubine. Yesterday Miao Zhiyu dragged me to a banquet where every household was told to bring their concubines. Thought she was being nice—turned out to be a pack of primary wives ganging up to mock and lecture the concubines! That's where I met Chun Jinqiao."
Qianli Xun closed her eyes a third time, breathing in the spring air. "People actually throw banquets like that? Stay clear next time."
"Caught me off guard once—next time Miao Zhiyu tries dragging me along, I'll say no flat out. Still, silver lining: I met the concubines from all the big families in Lin'an and found out they hold private gatherings of their own! I chipped in to join their circle, so now I'll never run short of invitations." A genuine, settled smile lit Xiaguo's face—she had found her tribe, women she could chat with and vent about primary wives to.
With eyes still shut, Qianli Xun murmured, "Old Master Laozi got it right: 'Good fortune leans against misfortune, and misfortune lurks within good fortune.'" [TN: Translator's rendering]
Baffled by the quote, Xiaguo was casting about for a reply when a maid from Old Madam Xia's courtyard arrived.
"Young Madam, the old gentleman finished his ballad, and Old Madam Xia wants to chat with him and keep him for lunch. She sent me to invite you and Concubine Xia to join them."
Grateful for an escape from Lin'an's wife-versus-concubine soap opera, Qianli Xun stood right up. "Come on, let's go eat with Grandmother."
Over the lively meal in Old Madam Xia's courtyard, Xiaguo had zero chance to launch back into her gossip.
After lunch, Qianli Xun personally saw the blind ballad singer and Xiaguo to the front gate. Loaded down with enough silver from the generous Chen household to cover a whole year's living without trudging village to village in wind and sun, the old man climbed into the carriage sighing in gratitude.
"No need to sigh, Grandfather," Xiaguo chided. "Even without Chen silver, what I send home covers the family. Stop wandering the roads singing."
"Your own path in Qiao Manor isn't easy," her grandfather warned. "Quit sending us so much and stash silver away for yourself so you can stand tall in that house. Remember to stay on good terms with your in-laws and the primary wife—harmony brings prosperity."
"You've told me a dozen times, Grandfather. I remember."
Returning to Moxiang Courtyard, Qianli Xun spotted Asheng pacing uneasily in the yard. "Something on your mind, Asheng?"
He spun around, mouth opening as if to spill something, then hesitated. "Nothing, Miss. Heading to the West Courtyard to help Qinggua drill." He hurried away looking weighed down.
"What's gotten into Asheng?" Qianli Xun wondered.
"Word is he likes Hongdou," Kuqiao replied. "Probably wanted to ask you to betroth her to him and lost his nerve."
Qianli Xun laughed. "He likes Hongdou? If he's too shy to ask, let's pretend we didn't notice." Putting it out of her mind, she went back inside to write.
Halfway to the West Courtyard, Asheng ran straight into Hongdou. Unable to hold the secret in another second, he grabbed her sleeve and pulled her into a quiet corner.
"What are you dragging me over here for?"
"I'm bursting!"
Hongdou teased, "Go use the latrine then—you'll feel right as rain!"
"Not that kind of bursting! I'm sitting on a secret that's killing me!"
Having noticed Asheng's crush ages ago, Hongdou felt her cheeks flush, convinced a confession was coming. "Go ahead, I'm listening."
Asheng dropped his voice. "Remember those awful rumors about Miss last year?"
Disappointed it was not a confession, Hongdou deflated. "Of course. Why bring up ancient history?"
Glancing around to ensure the coast was clear, Asheng whispered, "A servant from Eldest Young Master's courtyard told me today—our own young master was the one who spread those rumors!"
Disappointment flipped to shock. "What? Second Young Master spread them?"
"Keep your voice down!"
"What do we do?" Hongdou fretted. "My heart aches for Miss already. She never wanted to marry him, and if she finds out he's the one who ruined her match with Young Master Qiao with slander, she'll be crushed! Yet leaving her in the dark like a fool feels cruel too."
"That's why I'm stuck. Tell Miss or not? I'll go with whatever you think."
"I don't know either..."
"What are you two whispering about over here?" a voice cut in.
Both spun around to see Matron Wei, just back from checking the shops. Trading a look, they knew at once: nobody was wiser than Matron Wei. Bowing, Hongdou said, "Please come inside first, Matron. Asheng and I have something urgent to tell you."
Inside Matron Wei's room, before she even sat down, the two spilled every word.
Matron Wei sank onto her couch in stunned silence. Clever as she was, she had never dreamed the groom himself stood behind last year's slander.
Yet the pieces clicked fast. Back during the betrothal, the Chen family overlooking a divorce made sense, yet shrugging off citywide scandal had puzzled her. Now it all made sense. Prefect Zhu cared fiercely about face and would never let rumors slide—Madam Xiang had even let slip once at Wangyue Pavilion that he was investigating the culprit. Then the probe went dead silent, followed out of nowhere by a sudden match with the Chen family. Prefect Zhu had clearly uncovered the truth and forced a marriage as a neat fix: saving Zhu face while landing his daughter a wealthy husband.
Only his daughter had been kept completely in the dark. Poor girl.
"Go back to your duties, both of you," Matron Wei said. "Let me think this through."
"Yes, Matron." Together they stepped outside.
With the weight off his shoulders, Asheng breathed out in relief. "Handed the headache to Matron Wei—feel light as a feather now!"
Still stinging from her dashed hope of a confession, Hongdou snapped, "Enjoy feeling light, then! I've got work to do!" and marched off.
Asheng stared after her, scratching his head. "Weird—she was fine a second ago. What set her off?"
#CH 223
#TITLE: The Culprit Is the Groom Himself
Seated on the edge of her couch, Matron Wei weighed the dilemma. Keep quiet, and since even servants knew, her young mistress would find out sooner or later anyway; tell her now, and her grudge against the groom would explode, making their married life unbearable.
Her mind drifted back to running into Yao Qingyan in the garden just before coming home.
Walking back through Chen Manor after her errands, she had heard Yingxue calling from a pavilion where Eldest Madam sat resting and waving her over with a smile. Detouring into the pavilion, Matron Wei bowed. "Madam."
"You're such a busy woman, Matron—hard to catch you out in the garden," Yao Qingyan greeted her warmly. "Lucky I bumped into you today. Sit and chat a moment."
Guessing Eldest Madam had waited there on purpose, Matron Wei sat at the stone table. "Busy as I am, I can't compare with the Chen masters."
"I owe you thanks for helping Shuzhen—and helping me in the bargain."
Having forgotten her offhand remark, Matron Wei blinked. "Which matter do you mean, Madam?"
"You help Shuzhen so often it's no wonder you lose track. Winning over Old Madam Xia!"
Understanding dawned. "Ah, that. I only dropped a passing hint, and Miss took it to heart and acted on it. Credit belongs to her."
"You have sharp judgment, Matron, and Shuzhen has a good heart." Then Yao Qingyan's brow furrowed. "There's another matter I need your brains on."
"What troubles you, Madam?"
"Haoyu and Shuzhen, of course. Married, yet unconsummated, living separate lives—him roaming wild outside, her going about her days acting like he doesn't exist. At this rate, will I ever live to hold a plump grandson?"
Matron Wei had expected this sooner or later; a household might humor some clauses of a prenuptial contract, never all of them. "No rushing these things, Madam. It takes time."
"True enough. Set grandchildren aside for now—the urgent thing is getting Haoyu reined in. Old Madam Xia, Jianglin, and I all agreed to leave disciplining Haoyu to Shuzhen. We asked her, she nodded politely, yet hasn't cared one bit. We feel awkward bringing it up again, so I'm begging you, Matron—figure out a way to make Shuzhen willing to take Haoyu in hand."
At last Matron Wei saw why Yao Qingyan had staked out the pavilion. "Normally parents raise their own children. Having a new bride take on educating her husband—folks outside would hardly believe it."
"You're right, and as his mother I'm ashamed to dump our failure on my daughter-in-law," Yao Qingyan confessed. "Yet Old Madam Xia, Jianglin, and I talked it over and saw huge benefits—above all, knocking heads constantly might just strike sparks between them! Bang two boulders together enough and even stone sparks, let alone two living people. Don't you agree, Matron?"
Matron Wei found the whole scheme half laughable, yet understandable. Old Madam Xia spoiled him too much to be harsh; Yao Qingyan lacked the heart; and Chen Jianglin could not stay stern with both women softening every blow. Handing the baton to Shuzhen let all three stay on good terms while potentially turning Haoyu around and sparking romance in the process. Neat math—and honestly, molding a decent husband with her own hands was not a bad deal for her mistress either. The only catch was that Shuzhen respected her contract to the letter and had zero urge to meddle with him.
"A clever plan, Madam, except Miss won't agree."
"That's why I'm coming to you, Matron! Find a way to make her willing."
"I never force Miss to do anything she dislikes—"
Yao Qingyan rose and stepped in front of her. "If you don't say yes, Matron, I'll kneel right here until you do!"
Horrified—a mistress kneeling to a servant broke every law of propriety—Matron Wei jumped up to catch her arms. "You'll shorten my life, Madam! I'll try my best, though I can't promise Miss will agree."
Overjoyed, Yao Qingyan gripped her hands. "With you stepping in, you're worth two of us!"
Replaying that scene now, Matron Wei saw the whole board click into place. Tell her young mistress the truth, and she would fly into a rage and never let Chen Haoyu off the hook—ending his carefree gallivanting overnight. Even without wanting to "discipline" him, her wrath would accomplish the exact same thing. And Matron Wei knew her mistress's character: fierce fists aside, she would never cripple or kill him, only make him sweat—which the reckless playboy had long overdue. As for hope of him turning good, one only had to look at his father Chen Jianglin's devotion to Yao Qingyan; raised in a home like that, the boy had decent roots buried underneath. If Chen Haoyu ever treated her mistress half as well as Chen Jianglin treated his wife, Matron Wei would wake up smiling in her sleep.
Mind made up, she stepped out of her room and glanced across the back courtyard at Boya Study. "If Miss can kick the groom out here to live across from me, what can't she handle? Helping her whip herself up a decent husband—nothing left to hesitate over."
Inside Suxin Thatched Hall, Qianli Xun was bent over her desk writing.
