Having wrapped up her previous serial, she had decided to quit adapting modern web novels and write an original tale—inspired by her own heartache and seeing Ye Anya and Cao Ban tying red silk beneath the Divine Willow, a story about star-crossed lovers breaking free of miserable marriages to chase happiness. Words flowed off her brush across three or four pages before Matron Wei stepped inside.
Spotting her, Qianli Xun set down her brush and rose to welcome her. "Same courtyard, yet I haven't seen you in days, Matron! Missed you."
Matron Wei eyed the desk with a teasing smile. "Not telling Kuqiao to hide your manuscript today, Miss?"
Qianli Xun pulled her into a chair. "You knew all along and never said a peep, making me put on an act for nothing!"
Weighing how to break the news, Matron Wei stayed silent for a beat, letting her young mistress ask first.
"Why so quiet, Matron?"
Right on cue. Matron Wei spoke with measured care. "There is something I turned over for a long while, and decided you have a right to know."
"What is it?" Qianli Xun leaned forward in curiosity.
"Last year's rumors about you."
"Those? Ancient history—don't tell me people started gossiping again?"
"The culprit behind those rumors has surfaced."
Before Qianli Xun could open her mouth, Kuqiao bristled. "You know who did it, Matron? Name him right now so I can march over and curse him out until his ears bleed!"
"Who is it, Matron? And how did you find out?" Qianli Xun asked.
Matron Wei grew even more cautious. "How I found out doesn't matter. What matters is: once I tell you the name, what will you do?"
"Need you ask?" Qianli Xun declared. "That bastard put me through hell. One word: payback!"
"Yes! Payback!" Kuqiao echoed.
"Spit it out, Matron. Who?"
Steeling herself, Matron Wei uttered two words: "Young Master."
Qianli Xun stared. "Chen Haoyu?"
Matron Wei nodded.
Kuqiao froze in silence, watching shock darken into fury on her mistress's face. Qianli Xun's fingers clamped onto the wooden armrest—crack!—snapping the solid camphorwood clean in half.
Young Master is dead meat! Kuqiao winced inside.
Qianli Xun shot to her feet, paced two tight steps, and whirled around. "If even you know, Matron, with my father's reach he must have known ages ago—and still shoved me into this marriage blind! I'm marching back to Zhu Manor to have it out with him!"
Matron Wei rose to block her. "What good does arguing with Prefect Zhu do now, Miss? What's done is done. Better figure out how to deal with Young Master."
That cooled her head a fraction. Raising a storm at Zhu Manor would change nothing. "Fine, my father gets a pass for now. Anyone else I can forgive, never Chen Haoyu! I'm collecting every ounce of payback for making me the laughingstock of Lin'an! Kuqiao!"
"Here!"
"Fetch Hongdou!"
Moments later, Hongdou rushed in.
"Send men through every brothel and gambling den in Lin'an to track down Chen Haoyu," Qianli Xun snapped. "Report straight back—I'm going myself to beat him black and blue and haul him home in ropes!"
"No need to search, Miss," Hongdou answered. "I already know where he is."
"Chunxiang Brothel?"
Hongdou nodded.
"Then grab the men and follow me to Chunxiang Brothel!" Qianli Xun stormed straight out of Suxin Thatched Hall.
Matron Wei let out a sigh. "Well, things are about to get lively."
"Why do you look like you're enjoying the show, Matron?" Kuqiao blinked.
"Nonsense! Gather those manuscript pages off the desk before anyone sees them, and swap out that broken chair."
Moving to clean up, Kuqiao caught Matron Wei's sleeve as she turned to leave. "Matron, I'm scared—Miss already went through one mutual divorce. You don't think she's headed for a second one?"
"Can't you wish your mistress well instead of harping on divorce?" Matron Wei chided. "Young Master may be a mess right now, yet this second marriage is worlds apart from the first because the Chens are nothing like the Lins. Just sit back and watch."
Matron Wei walked out, leaving Kuqiao scratching her head. "Matron always talks in riddles—still, she doesn't look worried, so Miss must be fine. Right, he's the one in the wrong, and Miss's fists will beat his backside into ribbons!" Relieved, she tidied the manuscript and fetched a spare chair.
Leading ten servants—three maids and seven men—Qianli Xun marched out of Moxiang Courtyard toward the front gates, drawing curious stares and whispers from every passing servant.
Near the main gate, she ran straight into Chen Haodong returning from outside. Sighting her war party, Chen Haodong knew his bait had landed and stepped up with a bright smile. "Off somewhere with such a crowd, Sister-in-law?"
With zero reason to hide it from the Chens, Qianli Xun said straight, "Going to fetch Chen Haoyu."
"Why go yourself with a small army when you can send servants?" he baited her.
A sudden question hit Qianli Xun: did the entire Chen family know about Chen Haoyu spreading those rumors, leaving her the lone fool kept in the dark? "Eldest Cousin-in-law, do you know about Chen Haoyu spreading slander about me last year?"
Bullseye! Concealing his glee, Chen Haodong poured oil on the fire. "Since you already know, I won't hide it—everyone in Chen Manor knows. That's the exact reason the Chens never minded your damaged reputation or past divorce. Haoyu's a man; he made the mess, so he had to own up to it. Besides, last year Prefect Zhu showed up at our gate with a horsewhip, hauling Haoyu tied like a pig, and forced Eldest Uncle and Aunt to seal the betrothal on the spot. They had no choice."
Whatever Chen Haodong's angle, he had laid bare the full truth: Zhu Xiyan had known all along and hidden it just to offload a "damaged" daughter fast—caring solely about Zhu Manor's face, never once about her happiness.
Too furious to waste another syllable on Chen Haodong, Qianli Xun brushed right past him out the gate.
Grinning wider, Chen Haodong called after her, "Haoyu's at Chunxiang Brothel, Sister-in-law! Don't go to the wrong place!"
Job done, he strolled back to his courtyard in high spirits to await the fireworks.
#CH 224
#TITLE: Hog-Tied and Locked Up
Too furious to care a fig for ladylike appearances, Qianli Xun hopped down from the carriage with ten servants behind her and stormed straight into Chunxiang Brothel. Seeing their mistress march in without blinking, Hongdou and the other two maids cast aside their shyness and followed right behind.
Inside, business hummed as usual—patrons watching dancers, sipping wine, flirting with courtesans. Every jaw dropped at the sight of a respectable young lady striding in flanked by servants, three of them young maids. Never before had a gentlewoman raided the hall; every head swiveled to stare.
Recognizing her on sight, the madam whispered, "Mercy me! Young Master Chen's tigress tracked him all the way here!" Putting on a nervous smile, she hurried over. "Looking for Young Master Chen, Young Madam?"
"Where is he?" Qianli Xun's face promised immediate bruised noses and black eyes.
Not daring to hide a husband from his wife, the madam pointed up. "Upstairs with Chun Jinmo."
"Lead the way." Qianli Xun's tone dripped ice over fire.
Trying to give her patron a heads-up, the madam spotted Scarface on the landing and yelled, "Scarface! Tell Young Master Chen his wife's here!"
Already spotting the raid, Scarface bolted into the private room to sound the alarm.
Only then did the madam gesture toward the stairs. "Right this way, Young Madam."
Before they reached the foot of the staircase, Chen Haoyu's shout boomed down. "Zhu Shuzhen! What the hell are you doing here? Go home!"
Looking up, Qianli Xun saw him descending the steps adjusting his rumpled robes, clearly fresh from a tryst, with the same three hired bodyguards trailing behind.
Thinking of last year's slander and staring at his smug, punchable face snapped her last nerve. Launching upward with light-foot skill, she soared straight up the staircase, seized him by the collar, dropped back to the main floor, and shoved him sprawling onto the floorboards.
Humiliated before a packed house, Chen Haoyu blustered from the floor, "Who do you think you're shoving? I won't stoop to fighting you in public—wait till we get home and see how I handle you!"
Having already tasted the seven servants' fists—and seeing from that leap that their mistress outclassed all three of them combined—the three bodyguards traded one glance and bailed. Stepping up to Chen Haoyu, they bowed quick. "We don't meddle in family matters, Young Master Chen. See you another day!"
Left high and dry as they bolted out the door, Chen Haoyu scrambled to his feet—just as Qianli Xun barked, "Tie him up!"
Asheng whipped out a length of rope, and with two men helping, trussed Chen Haoyu tight in three seconds flat.
"Zhu Shuzhen, you've gone too far!" he roared. "I'm your husband! Bullying your own husband in public—everyone's going to laugh at you!"
Sure enough, half the patrons craned their necks grinning at the spectacle.
Unwilling to linger another second in a brothel, Qianli Xun ordered, "Haul him out!"
Just as she spun toward the door, a woman's voice floated down behind her. "Please wait, Young Madam!"
Qianli Xun turned back. Descending the stairs came a slender, pretty woman swaying like a willow branch in the breeze—Chun Jinmo, no doubt.
Reaching her, Chun Jinmo curtsied deep. "Greetings, Young Madam."
"What do you want?"
"I have read your poems, Young Madam—gentle, poignant, full of tender feeling. Meeting you in person today, you look nothing like your verses."
"I'm in no mood to critique poetry. Goodbye." Qianli Xun turned to leave again.
Chun Jinmo stepped across her path. "Wait, Young Madam, I didn't come to talk poetry. I heard you and Young Master Chen signed a prenuptial contract promising not to meddle in each other's lives. Why barge in here today and treat your husband like this?"
Learning Chen Haoyu had blabbed their private contract to a courtesan made Qianli Xun's blood boil hotter. "Who the hell are you to stick your nose in my affairs? What prenuptial contract? Never heard of it! Quit wasting my breath!"
She turned a third time—and Chun Jinmo hurried forward, dropping to her knees in front of the whole crowd.
"I didn't lay a finger on you! What are you kneeling for?"
"Young Madam, ever since I was sold into Chunxiang Brothel, Young Master Chen has been my only man, looking out for me all along. If he stops coming, my life here will turn to hell. Have mercy and let Young Master Chen go—Jinmo will be forever grateful!"
Seeing her kneel melted Chen Haoyu's heart into mush. "Look how pitiful she is, Zhu Shuzhen! You hate my guts—you're happier without me, and the Chen family has plenty of other sons, yet Jinmo can't survive without me! I mean the world to her! Untie me right now and quit making a scene!"
With her grudge still scorching hot, Qianli Xun stepped right up to his face. "Starting today, the more you want something, the harder I'll make sure you don't get it. Want to look after her? I'll make sure you never lay eyes on her again!"
Turning back to Chun Jinmo, she spoke cold and clear. "Sure, you have a rough life, and I'm not heartless—I just don't have the bandwidth to care right now. Besides, how do you know I never needed pity? Where were you when I was drowning in misery, and what was your precious Chen Haoyu doing? I'm no saint and no savior. Kneeling to me buys you nothing!"
With that, she marched her crew out the door.
Dragged past the threshold, Chen Haoyu craned his neck back shouting, "Hang tight! I'll be back soon!"
Left crumpled on the floorboards, Chun Jinmo wept. With a wife that fierce, clinging to Young Master Chen was over. Would she really end up serving someone like Master Jiang?
The madam stepped over and helped her up. "Jinmo, you're a courtesan—face the truth. Any lord who calls your name and puts down silver gets served, unless you get lucky like Jinqiao and someone buys you out as a concubine. I hear you hinted to Young Master Chen plenty of times to redeem you, and he never gave a straight answer. Never meant to—just came here to play. With a tigress like that at home, forget him buying you out. Pick a new patron who might actually redeem you! Master Jiang isn't a bad choice—older and rough-looking, sure, yet dead serious about you. Look how many brawls he fought with Young Master Chen over you! Word is he doesn't even have a primary wife yet. Plenty of opportunity there."
Some of the madam's words sank in, and Chun Jinmo went upstairs dabbing her eyes.
Back in Moxiang Courtyard, Qianli Xun sat behind her desk glaring at Chen Haoyu trussed in a chair before her.
Playing cool, he smirked. "Why the staring? Never seen a handsome man before? Don't tell me you missed me these past days!"
"Hongdou! Slap him twice for me!"
Chen Haoyu bristled. "Zhu Shuzhen, are you out of your mind?"
Hongdou faltered. "Miss, I dare not—he's the young master."
"Touch me and I'll sell you straight to Chunxiang Brothel!" Chen Haoyu barked.
Hongdou froze.
"What's gotten into you today, Zhu Shuzhen?" Chen Haoyu demanded. "We had a prenuptial contract—no meddling! Did a dog eat your word?"
Qianli Xun lifted a sheet of paper from her desk. "Here's your contract. Watch close." Popping the lid off her bronze incense burner, she rolled the sheet tight and dropped it inside. Flames licked up and reduced it to gray ash in seconds.
Chen Haoyu's jaw dropped. Only when she clapped the lid back on did he find his voice. "Since you burned it, the deal's void! Hand back my properties, restore this courtyard and hall the way they were, and pack off to the back courtyard—I'm moving back in here!"
"Don't you feel ridiculous, Chen Haoyu? If I dared burn the contract, think I'm handing back one copper of the properties or one tile of this courtyard? Dream on!"
"Aren't you scared I'll tell Grandmother and my parents so they come teach you a lesson?"
"You have to make it out of this courtyard first!"
Sensing something had shifted—before, she had ignored him and preached peaceful coexistence; why go nuclear today?—Chen Haoyu damped his temper and asked, "Did you swallow gunpowder this morning? I haven't wronged you once since our wedding. Why pull this?"
That question lit the fuse all over again. "Forgotten the rotten filth you pulled, Chen Haoyu?"
A cold jolt hit his gut. Burning the contract, foaming at the mouth—had she found out about the rumors? He asked warily, "Did you hear something?"
Itching to smash his nose in, Qianli Xun rose, fists clenched, and stalked toward him. "Last year, you spread lies that wrecked my engagement and made me the laughingstock of Lin'an! Time to settle that tab!"
Terror iced his veins—she knew! I'm a dead man! Seeing her fist whistle straight toward his face, he squeezed his eyes shut. Crash! Splinters exploded beneath him as he hit the floorboards—she had punched the wooden chair into kindling instead.
Exhaling in relief, he cracked his eyes open and still tried to wriggle out. "Your own fault for bullying me nonstop! If you hadn't pushed me around, I never would've spread those rumors! Besides, people didn't just gossip because of me—you wrote 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed' yourself—"
"Hongdou! Slap him right now!" Qianli Xun barked.
Given a second direct order, Hongdou hesitated no longer. Stepping up, she declared, "Even if you sell me to a brothel, Young Master, I'm slapping you for Miss!"
Smack! Smack! Two ringing blows landed square on Chen Haoyu's cheeks.
Watching from the side, Kuqiao felt furious on her mistress's behalf yet winced at the sting on his face.
Hands tied behind him, unable to rub his throbbing cheeks, Chen Haoyu yelped, "You little wench! Why hit so hard?"
Qianli Xun crouched down and patted his reddened cheek. "I meant to beat you half to death, and downgraded it to two slaps. That's mercy—still complaining?"
Unmoved, Chen Haoyu spat, "Zhu Shuzhen, I'm going to divorce you!"
"Divorce me? Not a chance! From today on, I have two goals in Chen Manor: live a great life, and make yours miserable."
Fear flickered in his eyes. "W-what are you going to do to me?"
"Everything you enjoy, I'll forbid," Qianli Xun enunciated word by cold word. "Everything you hate, I'll force you to do."
Chen Haoyu sputtered speechless. "You... you..."
Qianli Xun stood. "Guards! Haul Chen Haoyu to Boya Study in the back courtyard and lock him up!"
Asheng and another servant hoisted him up, marched him to Boya Study, and untied his ropes. The instant his arms came free, Chen Haoyu swung at Asheng hoping to bolt—only for Asheng to block him solid. "Behave yourself, Young Master. Crossing Miss won't end well for you."
Outside, servants slammed the shutters and began nailing wooden planks across the windows.
Chen Haoyu stormed to the sill roaring, "Stop that! I own this courtyard!"
Behind him, Asheng stepped out and snapped a heavy padlock onto the door.
Chen Haoyu pounded the wood shouting, "Zhu Shuzhen, this isn't over! Just wait! This is Chen Manor, not Zhu Manor, and not your Wangyue Pavilion!"
A hushed voice drifted through a crack in the boards. "Young Master? Young Master, are you all right?"
For the first time in his life, Qinggua's voice sounded like heaven. Chen Haoyu pressed his face to the shutters. "Qinggua, thank goodness! The tigress is torturing me! Run straight to Old Madam Xia, Father, and Mother—tell them I'm repudiating her! Go!"
"Right away, Young Master!"
Qinggua sprinted toward the front yard to sound the alarm, yet had not even cleared the lotus pond before Qianli Xun's voice froze him in his tracks. "Qinggua! Where are you rushing? Playing messenger for your master?"
Skidding to a halt, Qinggua scrambled for a fib. "Not at all, Young Madam! I was just looking for an open space to practice Phantom Sidestep!"
"You already know it inside out—no need to practice. I worried you didn't have a bed, so from today on, you're rooming with Chen Haoyu in Boya Study." Taking zero chances on him running his mouth, she marched over, grabbed him by the collar, and hauled him toward the back courtyard.
When the padlock rattled open, Chen Haoyu beamed, sure Qinggua had summoned Grandmother to spring him—only for Qinggua to fly stumbling through the doorway before the door slammed shut again.
"Didn't want you lonely, Chen Haoyu," Zhu Shuzhen's voice drifted in, "so I sent you a roommate!"
Chen Haoyu hurled himself against the door, hammering the panels. "Zhu Shuzhen! The second I get out of here, I swear I'll divorce you!"
#CH 225
#TITLE: Matron Wei's Clever Reassurance
After pounding the door and shouting himself hoarse to zero effect, Chen Haoyu needed a punching bag. Spinning around, he laid eyes on Qinggua standing there blinking innocently, and saw red.
"I sent you to fetch Old Madam Xia and my parents! How the hell did you end up locked in here too?"
"Don't blame me, Young Master! Young Madam's too sharp—caught me in the front courtyard, saw straight through me, and tossed me in here."
"You idiot, couldn't you make up a lie?"
"I did! She didn't buy it, said I should keep you company." Qinggua edged closer. "Young Master, why not just play along from now on? If she says east, don't go west. Fighting her gets you nowhere and drags me down with you—"
Already boiling, Chen Haoyu snapped and threw a fierce kick. "Daring to lecture me now? I'll kick you silly!"
Qinggua glided sideways with effortless ease; Chen Haoyu missed empty air and nearly toppled over. Another kick—missed. Three, four more kicks—Qinggua slipped past every single one.
Furious, Chen Haoyu ditched kicking and locked Qinggua in a basic grappling hold. "Keep dodging! Where'd you pick up that trick?"
"Mercy, Young Master! Mercy! Young Madam taught me Phantom Sidestep!"
"Zhu Shuzhen again!" Releasing him, Chen Haoyu waited half a second before booting Qinggua square in the backside. "Screw her!"
Stumbling forward onto his knees, unhurt, Qinggua sat back on the floor. "Stop taking it out on me, Young Master. Beating me won't get you out of here—better figure out how to escape."
Mad as he was, Chen Haoyu knew none of this was Qinggua's fault. The real trick was getting word to his parents and grandmother; once the elders intervened, Zhu Shuzhen would have to unlock the door.
Dropping into a cushioned chair, he asked, "Which servant in this courtyard are you closest with?"
Qinggua puffed up with pride. "All of them! While you were gone, I drilled in the West Courtyard with them every day—just Phantom Sidestep, mind you, since Young Madam said I lack the bone structure for fist forms. Hanging out daily made us tight. Actually, Young Master... between us, I fell for Hongdou, and I think she likes me back!" His ears turned pink.
Chen Haoyu snorted. "Hongdou falling for a mug like yours? Quit daydreaming! Tell me which of the ten servants you're tightest with so we can get him to sneak out a message."
Wounded by the dig, Qinggua pouted. "You always look down on me! First maid I ever like and you mock me. Look how well Young Madam treats her servants—you ought to take notes from her!"
"Since when does a lackey lecture his master? Talk business or I'll smack you!"
Qinggua pursed his lips. "I get along fine with all ten, problem is they answer to Young Madam. None of them take orders from me."
Chen Haoyu jumped up and grabbed the front of Qinggua's tunic. "All that breath wasted for nothing? Fine—your punishment is to stand at the door and yell, 'Zhu Shuzhen, I'm divorcing you!' Don't stop until I say!" Shoving him against the door, he barked, "Yell!"
Sprawled by the threshold, Qinggua whimpered, "Young Master, she married you, not me! How can I shout that? And a servant calling his mistress by her full name—"
Chen Haoyu lifted a boot over him. "Yell, or I stomp you into meat pie!"
"I'll yell! I'll yell! Hold your temper!" Scrambling up, Qinggua leaned against the wood and muttered toward the crack, "Zhu Shuzhen, I'm divorcing you."
"Louder!"
"Zhu Shuzhen, I'm divorcing you!"
"Louder!"
Squeezing his eyes shut, Qinggua bawled at the top of his lungs, "Zhu Shuzhen! I'm divorcing you!" Over and over he shouted.
Satisfied, Chen Haoyu strolled into the inner bedroom and flopped down for a nap.
Right across the back yard in Jingyi Hall, Matron Wei sat calm and alert, listening to every sound—including Qinggua's howling. Rising to her feet, she murmured, "Time to pay Old Madam Xia a visit."
Over in Old Madam Xia's rooms, her three daughters-in-law sat chatting with her when a maid burst in gasping, "Old Madam! Old Madam! Terrible news!"
"Mind your tongue," Old Madam Xia scolded. "What's got you flustered? Slow down and speak plain."
Catching her breath, the maid reported, "I just saw Second Young Madam march Second Young Master through the gate tied up in ropes! Looks like he's in for rough treatment!"
Both Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan felt their hearts clench in worry.
Thrilled that her son's scheme had worked, Madam Bao feigned shock. "Outrageous! A bride barely through the door tying up her own husband! You have to step in, Mother, or folks will think Chen men let women walk all over them!"
Madam Cui piled on. "Eldest Sister-in-law, you need to put your foot down and teach that Zhu girl how a daughter-in-law behaves! Wild as Haoyu is, disciplining him is up to you and Eldest Brother, not her!"
Old Madam Xia tamped down her own unease. "Quit fanning the flames, you two. I gave Shuzhen authority to discipline Haoyu, so I trust her. However she handles him, neither Qingyan nor Jianglin is to interfere—and neither will I."
Itching to rush over and check, Yao Qingyan bit her lip and held still. "I'll obey you, Mother."
With both women too distracted to chat, Madam Bao and Madam Cui saw the fun was over and took their leave together.
Once outside the gate, Madam Bao sniffed, "Heaven knows what Eldest Brother and Sister-in-law saw in that Zhu girl—poetess or not, foul temper and stained name."
"Drop the act, Second Sister-in-law," Madam Cui retorted. "Haoyu marrying poorly only helps Haodong. Go laugh behind closed doors."
"What talk is that? Even if Haoyu landed a saint, he couldn't hold a candle to my Haodong, who learned trade from his father and uncles since childhood. Not our fault Haoyu only chases pleasure and your Haofei ignores business—leaving all the heavy lifting to my Haodong."
Stung by the swipe at her son, Madam Cui stiffened. "I have chores to attend to. Excuse me."
Madam Bao sneered after her retreating back. "Generations of Chen merchants, yet instead of teaching her son trade, she daydreams of him winning an official post in the capital. Good luck with that!"
With news this juicy, she hurried off to report to her son—barely glancing at Matron Wei walking toward Old Madam Xia's courtyard as they crossed paths.
Inside, Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan sat in taut silence, each wrestling with her heart while the maids stood mute in the heavy air.
At last a maid announced, "Old Madam, Eldest Madam, Matron Wei from Second Young Madam's courtyard requests an audience."
Color rushed back to both women's faces. "Show her in at once!" Old Madam Xia ordered.
Matron Wei stepped in and bowed.
Old Madam Xia waved for a chair. "Since Shuzhen married in, this is your first visit to my quarters."
Reading the anxiety etched across both faces—bold enough to delegate discipline, yet softhearted the moment it began—Matron Wei replied smoothly, "My oversight, Old Madam. I beg your forbearance."
"Understandable," Old Madam Xia said with a forced smile. "Running Shuzhen's entire courtyard keeps you busy enough."
Deciding to set their hearts at ease right away, Matron Wei said, "Thank you for understanding, Old Madam. Now that I have a free moment, I came to pay respects and speak with you about Miss and Young Master."
Both women blurted in unison, "Is Haoyu all right?"
"Look how anxious you both are—worried Miss is abusing him?"
"Not abusing," Yao Qingyan hedged, "I just hate seeing him suffer."
Old Madam Xia sighed. "I spoiled him from the cradle and can't bear seeing him take the smallest hardship. I know it hurt him, yet I can't stop fretting."
"All the more reason you were wise to leave his discipline to Miss," Matron Wei said. "Right now she has him locked in the back courtyard—windows boarded, door padlocked, servants bringing him meals and water..."
Both women gasped together, "How can she do that?"
"Disciplining him is fine, yet isn't locking him up too harsh?" Yao Qingyan fretted. "How can Haoyu stand that?"
Seeing their panic, Matron Wei played her real card. "Earlier today, Madam, you stopped me to persuade Miss to discipline Young Master—which she had zero intention of doing. Why did she suddenly change her mind? Not because I talked her into it, but because she just learned something."
"Learned what?" Old Madam Xia asked.
"That Young Master was the one who spread all those rumors about her last year."
Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan traded a dismayed look. "You can't wrap fire in paper forever," Old Madam Xia sighed. "Just as well she found out—though poor Haoyu is in for rough days now. Please go back and coax Shuzhen: discipline is fine, just beg her not to go too heavy-handed."
"Haoyu acted like a scoundrel ruining her name," Yao Qingyan fretted. "I'm terrified she'll lose control and injure him!"
"Since you entrusted Young Master to Miss, trust her completely," Matron Wei reassured them. "I watched Miss grow up. Her heart is upright and deeply kind—"
"Wenxin told me she turned fierce after losing her memory, nothing like before!" Yao Qingyan cut in.
"Fierce on the surface, yes, yet her core kindness hasn't changed one bit. Haven't you both felt that since she married in?"
Both women nodded.
"Even furious at him for ruining her match with Qiao Manor, Miss would never genuinely hurt him with her fists—just teach him a sharp lesson. Put your minds at rest and pretend you know nothing. Think of it as him off gallivanting again! Heaven knows how many scrapes he got into outside without you knowing, and he still came home bouncing on both feet."
Remembering the bruises he had dragged home and Master Jiang's rigged wager—who knew how many other close calls they had never heard of—both women exhaled.
Yao Qingyan steeled herself. "Matron Wei is right. We'll pretend he's out roaming."
Still uneasy, Old Madam Xia asked, "Wait—from what you said, Shuzhen locked him up out of anger over the rumors, not to reform him. Won't that miss the whole point?"
"Rest easy, Old Madam," Matron Wei smiled. "Whatever her motive, her methods are identical, so the result will be the same. Just wait for Miss to whip a fine grandson into shape for you—and who knows, just as Eldest Madam said, bang two stones together enough and sparks might really fly!"
At that, both women chuckled, their gloom lifting away.
Before leaving, Matron Wei gave one final warning. "While Miss disciplines Young Master, neither of you must step in. If he sends pleas for help, harden your hearts and ignore them. Soften even once, and all Miss's effort goes down the drain."
Bracing themselves internally, both women nodded in agreement.
#CH 226
#TITLE: Hongdou Delivers Dinner to Boya Study
By dusk, Qinggua's voice had shrunk to a hoarse croak, yet he dared not stop whispering, "Zhu Shuzhen, I'm divorcing you."
Then a loud snore drifted from the inner bedroom. Qinggua lit up. "Young Master's asleep! Break time—my throat's dry as parchment. Need tea."
Padding to the wooden table, he poured a cup and tested it with his lips. "Cold already, and nobody brought a fresh pot? Oh well, thirsty enough to drink anything." Gulping it down, he glanced at the dim cracks between the boarded shutters. "Dark already and my stomach's growling. Where's dinner?"
Right then the padlock rattled outside. Qinggua darted to the door as it swung open and Hongdou walked in carrying a food carrier in one hand and a steaming teapot in the other.
"Hongdou! You're here!" Taking the teapot to set on the table, Qinggua eyed the open doorway thinking of bolting—just as the door clicked shut behind her.
Hongdou set down the food carrier and lifted out two plates of plain vegetables and two bowls of rice. "Miss sent dinner. Where's Young Master? Call him out to eat."
Woken by the clack of the padlock, Chen Haoyu strolled into the front room and glared at Hongdou. "The second I get out, I'm selling you off to pay you back for those two slaps!"
Having already swung on him, Hongdou had zero fear left with her mistress backing her. "Miss holds my deed of sale, Young Master. Nothing you can do to me."
Snapped at by a maid, Chen Haoyu bristled. "Go tell Zhu Shuzhen I'm divorcing her!"
"You had Qinggua shouting it all afternoon—every soul in Moxiang Courtyard heard, Miss included. No need for me to repeat it! Miss says whatever you want, she'll do the exact opposite. Divorcing her? Dream on!"
Chen Haoyu cocked a fist toward Hongdou—and Qinggua jumped right between them. "Don't take it out on Hongdou, Young Master! We depend on her to bring us food!"
Seeing his own servant jump in front of a maid made Chen Haoyu snap. Forgetting Qinggua's training, he swung a boot out of pure reflex.
Qinggua glided right past it. "Quit kicking me, Young Master—doesn't work anymore! Only reason I didn't shove you flat is because I care about you; anyone else would've hit the floor!"
Chen Haoyu knew he could not beat Hongdou, and now Zhu Shuzhen had ruined even his lone punching bag. Planting his feet, he clenched both fists and hissed, "Zhu Shuzhen, this isn't over!"
Amused, Hongdou said, "Better eat up, Young Master. I'm waiting to take the bowls back."
Too hungry to waste more steam on a losing battle, Chen Haoyu dropped onto a stool—and scowled at the table. "Two measly dishes? I never eat less than five dishes and a soup! Tell Zhu Shuzhen to send more!"
"Miss says every time you make a demand, we cut the portion further."
"Fine, forget I spoke!" Swallowing his rage, he picked up his chopsticks—and froze. "All vegetables? Not even a scrap of minced pork?"
"Miss says while you're locked up, it's vegetarian only. No meat."
Too livid to waste another word, Chen Haoyu dug in.
Starving himself, Qinggua eyed the second bowl of rice set out for him, took a bold seat across the table, and reached for his chopsticks.
"Who gave you permission to sit at my table?" Chen Haoyu barked.
Qinggua set the chopsticks down, stood up, and swallowed hard staring at the rice. "I yelled my lungs out for you all afternoon, Young Master—if not merit, at least pity! My belly's howling; let me have a couple bites?"
"Let Qinggua eat at the table with you, Young Master," Hongdou interposed. "Miss's orders."
"Hear that, Young Master? Young Madam's orders!" Qinggua chimed in.
Chen Haoyu had almost softened at Qinggua's puppy-dog eyes, yet invoking Zhu Shuzhen hardened him right back. "You're my servant, you obey me—Zhu Shuzhen doesn't call the shots! Sit down with me and see if I don't skin you!"
Backing off, Qinggua stood hovering at his elbow, heart twisting with every chopstickful his master lifted, terrified nothing would be left.
At last Chen Haoyu finished—leaving half of each vegetable dish intact. The instant his master shot Hongdou a glare and stomped back into the bedroom, Qinggua dropped onto the stool and wolfed everything down.
Once full, he helped Hongdou stack the dishes and seized the moment. "Hongdou, what do you think of me?"
Setting the empty bowls into the food carrier, Hongdou replied, "You're all right."
"What does 'all right' mean?" Qinggua pressed.
Stacking the plates inside, she said, "Not great, not terrible."
Unsatisfied, Qinggua pushed on. "So... do you like someone who's not great and not terrible?"
With the question laid bare, Hongdou felt zero embarrassment since she had zero interest. "What's rolling around in that head of yours, Qinggua? Plenty of maids in Chen Manor—bound to be someone who likes you."
Handing her the cold teapot, Qinggua said, "I don't care about anyone else. I only care if you like me."
Lifting the carrier in one hand and the teapot in the other, Hongdou drawled, "Do I like you..."
Qinggua's face lit up.
"...or not like you..."
His heart dropped.
"...truth is, I don't know."
Disappointed yet undeterred, Qinggua trailed her to the door. "I'm a really good guy! Spend more time with me and you'll see—marry me and I'll spoil my wife rotten!"
Hongdou laughed. "Since you're such a catch, I'll spread the word around Moxiang Courtyard—and all of Chen Manor if need be. Guaranteed we find you a bride!"
Before Qinggua could blink, the door opened and clicked padlocked from outside.
Qinggua called through the wood, "Hongdou! Will you be my wife?!"
No reply came from outside—only a mocking voice right behind him. "Have you no pride? Hongdou just shot you down cold and you're still howling shameless nonsense through the door! Even I'm embarrassed for you!"
Qinggua spun around. "Can you stop creeping up behind me like a ghost, Young Master? Nearly stopped my heart!"
"Daring to complain at me now? Sick of living?" Bored stiff in lockup and realizing anger bought him nothing, Chen Haoyu dropped onto a chair. "Come sit and chat."
Flattered, Qinggua hopped onto a stool. "What do you want to talk about, Young Master?"
"Chasing Hongdou like that won't work in a million years."
"Teach me how, Young Master!"
"Know why girls always fall over themselves for me?"
Stumped, Qinggua ransacked his memory. "Young Master, I've never once seen a girl actually like you! All I ever saw was you grabbing village maidens to serve in your courtyard—that's where every maid in Moxiang Courtyard came from before, plus other Chen properties..."
Chen Haoyu raised a fist. "Keep blabbing and eat knuckles!"
Qinggua leaned clear. "Not blabbing—just facts!"
Chen Haoyu lowered his fist. "Fine, I acted wild back then, yet I haven't touched a village girl in over half a year. Aren't I allowed to improve?"
"True enough—since last year you switched to haunting Chunxiang Brothel. Guess brothel girls suit you better. Do you have a favorite there, Young Master?"
Chun Jinmo crossed Chen Haoyu's mind. "Sort of. Wait—we were talking about you! How'd it turn onto me?"
"You asked why girls like you!" Qinggua protested. "If you ask me, they only flock to you for your silver."
"Bingo! You nailed it!" Chen Haoyu snapped his fingers. "That's why you need silver. Get rich, and you won't have to chase Hongdou—she'll throw herself at you!"
Qinggua shook his head. "Wrong, Young Master! You're loaded, yet Young Madam doesn't want you—even locked you up! Anyone chasing silver isn't sincere. Even if I had silver, I'd pretend to be broke so I'd know Hongdou likes me for real."
Those words left a bitter ache in Chen Haoyu's chest. Maybe that was why—knowing every coerced village girl and every smiling courtesan bent only for his silver—he had never truly fallen for anyone. Bought affection held zero real joy; bored with life, he had merely numbed himself with money.
Seeing his master fall quiet, Qinggua winced. "Put my foot in it again? Mad at me, Young Master?"
Instead, Chen Haoyu answered calm and low. "Looks like you know more about the heart than I do. Forget my advice—chase Hongdou your own way. Still, take it from me: she's not into you. Don't waste your breath."
"Thanks for the warning, Young Master, yet I haven't even tried for real yet—today was just a warm-up! Once we get out, I'll pull out all the stops. Sooner or later, Hongdou will be mine!" Qinggua declared with glowing confidence.
Not having the heart to burst his bubble further, Chen Haoyu turned back to escape. Night had fallen, yet neither Grandmother nor his parents had shown up. Could Zhu Shuzhen have kept him hidden? Impossible—he had spotted a maid from Old Madam Xia's own courtyard staring when he was dragged through the gates in ropes! Why hadn't Grandmother come storming to his rescue?
"Qinggua," he frowned, "why hasn't Grandmother come to spring me?"
"Maybe Old Madam doesn't know you're locked up?"
"One of her own maids saw Zhu Shuzhen march me in tied up! Normally Grandmother would've flown over here in a heartbeat. Did something big happen while I was away?"
Qinggua thought back. "Actually, yes—huge stuff!"
"Like what?"
"While you were gone, Young Madam hired a storyteller to come every single day and tell stories to Old Madam Xia—had her laughing nonstop! Today she even brought an old blind ballad singer, and Old Madam Xia loved it so much she ate extra bowls of rice! And the biggest thing of all—"
Unease prickled up Chen Haoyu's spine. "What's the biggest thing?"
"Young Madam smoothed things over between Old Madam Xia and Eldest Master and Madam! Now Second Master, Third Master, and their wives visit Old Madam Xia's courtyard every single day too! The maids say her courtyard is packed with laughter daily, and Old Madam Xia praises Second Young Madam to the skies—Eldest Master and Madam too!"
Chen Haoyu slammed his fist down onto the table. "Sneaky! Pure sneaky! Bought off my whole family the second my back was turned! No wonder nobody came for me! One clever bride walks in and suddenly Grandmother stops spoiling me and Mother forgets I exist?!"
Qinggua stifled a snicker.
"I'm fuming and you're laughing?" Chen Haoyu glared. "Asking for a beating?"
Qinggua jumped clear, making Chen Haoyu chuckle despite himself. "Relax, just messing with you. Sit back down and keep me from dying of boredom."
Settling back on his stool, Qinggua grinned. "Didn't mean to laugh, Young Master—you just called Young Madam a 'clever bride' for the first time ever!"
"Learn to listen! Was that a compliment? Quit stalling and help me get out of here. Wait—I've got a plan! Put your looks to work and seduce one of Zhu Shuzhen's maids into unlocking the door so I can slip out! Forget Kuqiao and Hongdou—she's got two other maids with plain looks who'll fall hook, line, and sinker!"
Qinggua pulled a sour face. "My looks don't hold a candle to yours, Young Master. Besides, I have zero interest in those two maids—and every maid under Young Madam is a tough cookie!"
"What, expecting me to step up and seduce them myself?"
"With your charm, Young Master, guaranteed success!"
Chen Haoyu raised a palm. "Want a smack?"
Qinggua ducked with a sheepish grin. "Hehe... my looks are really nothing special."
Chen Haoyu lowered his hand. "Sacrifice a sliver of your charm for me, and once we're out, I swear I'll help you win Hongdou over!"
Qinggua's eyes lit up like lanterns. "Really? Don't lie to me, Young Master!"
"A gentleman's word stands firm as four galloping horses!"
"Deal! I'll bite the bullet and do it for you!"
"Now you're talking!"
#CH 227
#TITLE: Payback Under the Cover of Discipline
Dragging Chen Haoyu back from Chunxiang Brothel in ropes, Qianli Xun had not feared criticism from the Chen elders—in fact, she had welcomed a confrontation so she could demand why they had pushed this marriage knowing full well he had smeared her name across Lin'an.
Yet night fell without a single elder darkening her doorway. She had seen a maid from Old Madam Xia's courtyard watch the whole procession; with how the old woman doted on her grandson, she should have charged over first. Puzzled yet unwilling to overthink it, Qianli Xun let it go.
With the culprit locked away, her hot fury ebbed, replaced by a rare wave of raw grief. Unable to focus on her huaben, she sat at her desk remembering how those rumors had torn her life apart—her darkest months, drowned in tears and bleak despair.
All of it Chen Haoyu's doing—and she had married him. Thank goodness they had never consummated the marriage, or she would have regretted it to her dying day.
Thinking back to that bleak winter brought up images of her family, Mengrong, and Shen Jianchen. Family warmth had kept her standing; even though Zhu Xiyan had never visited Wangyue Pavilion, her mother, brother, and sister-in-law could never have come without his nod. Stern as her father was, he protected her in his own rigid way—matching her with the Chens included. Matron Wei had been right to stop her from storming Zhu Manor; raising a fuss now could not rewind the clock.
During those dark months, Mengrong had come to Wangyue Pavilion two or three times—few visits, yet enough to prove the world had not abandoned her, someone willing to sit beside the brazier and share heart-to-hearts. That was when Qianli Xun had vowed to treat Mengrong as her lifelong confidante.
And once she learned Prince Yi was Shen Jianchen, everything else snapped into focus: the gifts, the treats, waiting outside Wangyue Pavilion in his carriage while Mengrong visited—kindness she could never repay in one lifetime. For Mengrong's sake she had turned him down cold, yet she could not deny that having an imperial prince see her worth while an entire city spat on her had restored her backbone. Real white-knight stuff.
"Kuqiao, bring out that gift Prince Yi sent to Wangyue Pavilion last year."
Kuqiao racked her brains. "Miss, I don't recall Prince Yi sending a gift."
"The peeing clay boy."
Light dawned. "Oh, right! That bare-bottomed clay figurine! What do you want it for, Miss?"
"Stop asking and go fetch it."
Kuqiao frowned trying to remember where she had tucked it—her mistress had looked once and told her to put it away, never asking for it again. "I'll check the storage room; that's where I stash things you rarely look at." She hurried out.
Left alone with melancholy pressing on her chest, Qianli Xun spread a sheet of paper and wrote:
"How many springs have come and gone, / Yet never a year with resentment so deep as this. / On quiet crabapple branches shines the moon— / How shall I endure this clear, lone night?" —"Ten Spring Reflections, No. 1" [TN: Translator's rendering]
As she lifted her brush, Kuqiao returned holding a small wooden box and set it beside her. "Had to dig for ages to find it."
Setting her brush aside, Qianli Xun watched Kuqiao lift the poem sheet and blow across the wet ink. "I'll save this for the next time Nan Shuang or Huayu comes asking."
Catching the word "resentment" in the damp strokes, Kuqiao paused. "Miss, do you hate Young Master?"
Qianli Xun slid the box closer, lifted the lid, and took out the bare-bottomed clay figurine. A genuine smile broke across her face—the mischievous little doll made it impossible not to laugh. "Hate him? Of course I hate him. He put me through hell."
Blowing once more on the ink, Kuqiao relaxed at seeing her mistress smile. She did not worry for the groom; she worried for her mistress spending a second marriage poisoned by hatred. "So long as you smile, Miss, I'm happy."
Once dry, Kuqiao tucked the poem away and noticed her mistress still gazing down at the clay doll. Remembering the figurine Bai Feng had bought her, Kuqiao murmured, "Wonder when Bai Feng and Prince Yi are coming back to Lin'an."
That echoed Qianli Xun's own tangled wish—wanting Shen Jianchen back, yet dreading facing Mengrong. "Honestly, I hope they stay in Bianliang and never come back."
Understanding her heart, Kuqiao steered away from reminders of the wedding raid. "Miss, do you really plan to keep Young Master locked up forever?"
Setting the doll back inside its box and closing the lid, Qianli Xun said, "Locked up for now. Haven't decided for how long."
Suddenly Kuqiao began to sniffle.
Qianli Xun turned. "Why on earth are you crying?"
"I feel so awful for you, Miss!" Kuqiao sobbed. "Your first marriage was misery, you fought so hard for a mutual divorce, and now you're wed to the man who spread slander about you! How are you supposed to live like this? Boo-hoo!"
Her maid's tears tugged at Qianli Xun's own heart. Without weeping, she answered grimly, "I'm going to make Chen Haoyu pay."
"He's your husband now, Miss!" Kuqiao wept on. "In outsiders' eyes you're one household—rising together, sinking together! If he pays a steep price, you'll get dragged down with him! I hate seeing you trapped like this!"
"Quit crying, you're giving me a headache." Handing her the wooden box, Qianli Xun said, "Put this on the shelf where I can reach it whenever I want."
Wiping her tears lest she upset her mistress further, Kuqiao placed the box on the shelf.
"Go get some rest. I want to sit alone for a while."
Obeying, Kuqiao retreated to Yuening Room.
Finding the room stifling, Qianli Xun stepped outside to the edge of the lotus pond. Small green pads floated over dark water, bathed in moonlight—pure silver reflection against deep shadow.
Kuqiao's warning about rising and sinking together rang true. Even though she did not love Chen Haoyu and the elders had forced the match, walking into that wedding had been her own choice. Whether out of exhaustion, bowing to fate, or giving Mengrong back her brother, she meant this manor to be her final stop and had never considered a second divorce. Hearing Chen Haoyu howl about repudiating her today had not scared her, yet it had stung.
How could she vent her grudge while still building a stable life here?
A sharp idea flashed: Old Madam Xia and her in-laws had begged her to discipline Chen Haoyu. A dry smile curved her lips. "Fine—I'll take my revenge under the banner of 'disciplining' him! Pray you never change, Chen Haoyu, so I get to discipline you for life! And if you actually reform, I become the Chen family's hero with an unshakable seat in this house."
Picturing scatterbrained Chen Haoyu turning refined like Wenyuan or steady like Shen Jianchen made her laugh out loud. With her mind clear, her spirits rose. Tomorrow she would call on the elders upfront and lay out her plan to "discipline" him, reassuring them so nobody meddled in her payback—sorry, her education program.
From the window of Yuening Room, Kuqiao watched her mistress's silhouette by the pond, sighed, shut the casement, and turned to Hongdou. "I'm terrified Miss will end up going through another divorce."
Lying awake on one of the two maids' beds, Hongdou replied, "Stop stressing, Sister Kuqiao. Miss gets along great with the Chen elders—they'd never let her go."
Kuqiao sat on the edge of Hongdou's bed. "After finding out Young Master spread those rumors, Miss won't go easy on him. He's Old Madam Xia's precious darling and Eldest Madam's own son—how long will they sit back and watch? Fond as they are of Miss, blood runs thicker than water."
Hongdou rolled over. "Quit borrowing trouble. Matron Wei isn't worried one bit, so why are you?"
True enough—since Young Master had been marched home, Matron Wei had stepped out once and stayed totally unruffled. Kuqiao relaxed. "When you delivered dinner, was Young Master still cursing Miss?"
Hongdou turned back. "Nonstop! Swear they must've been bitter rivals in a past life forced under one roof in this one. Wonder who'll win?"
"Need you ask? Miss will wipe the floor with him!" Kuqiao crowed, all worry forgotten.
Hongdou frowned softly. "Still, they're husband and wife—what's the fun in fighting forever? I want Miss happy with a decent husband. Eldest Master and Eldest Madam are such wonderful people—how didn't Young Master take after his father? If he ever treated Miss the way Eldest Master treats Eldest Madam, I'd be over the moon for her."
Stifling a yawn, Kuqiao snuffed the candle, slipped out of her outer jacket, and climbed into bed. "Worrying won't fix him. All we can do is hope Young Master turns over a new leaf soon so Miss can have peace."
Late that night, when Chen Jianglin returned from the tea house, Yao Qingyan recounted the whole day's drama.
Chen Jianglin grinned. "Locking him up is just what he needed! I wanted to do it years ago—only held back because of you and Mother."
Helping him slip off his outer robe, Yao Qingyan said, "When I first heard, Mother and I both ached for him. Thank goodness Matron Wei came over to reassure us—turns out Shuzhen found out Haoyu spread those rumors last year, and that's why she locked him up."
Sitting on the edge of the bed, Chen Jianglin sighed. "Fire never stays wrapped in paper. Just as well she knows now. After pulling a stunt that foul, locking him up is mercy—he deserves a good horsewhipping."
When Yao Qingyan bent to remove his boots, he waved her off. "I'll handle my own boots. Get into bed and rest."
Taking off her hairpins at the dressing table, Yao Qingyan shed her outer robe, snuffed the lamp, and slipped under the covers. "I'm less worried about whips than her fists! What if Shuzhen breaks his bones?"
"Put your mind at rest. Tough as her fists are, has she thrown one punch since marrying in? Quite the opposite—courteous, sharp, and warmhearted. Barely days under our roof and the whole atmosphere of the manor brightened."
"Sounds like she brought good luck into Chen Manor."
"No question! Now I'm convinced her divorce from Lin Jue and the broken Qiao match were Heaven saving her for our Chen household! Hahaha!"
"Hardly Heaven—our two families only sealed this match because Haoyu spread those rumors."
"Which proves fate forced Haoyu to earn himself a fine wife! Hahaha!"
Half amused despite disagreeing, Yao Qingyan smiled in the dark, silently wishing for her son and daughter-in-law to find their way to peace.
#CH 228
#TITLE: One Branch Worries While Another Laughs
While one wing fretted, inside Chen Jiangshu's quarters, his family of three laughed up a storm.
"Hahaha! Locked up tight!" Chen Haodong crowed. "No more roaming wild for Haoyu! Feels so good—why should I slave away earning silver for this house while he does nothing and spends like water? That tigress came through big-time!"
"Satisfying, yes," Chen Jiangshu mused, "yet it puzzles me why Old Madam Xia and First Sister-in-law haven't budged an inch. Haven't they heard?"
"Of course they heard!" Madam Bao said. "I was sitting right there in Old Madam's room when a maid ran in reporting it. You should've seen the looks on their faces!"
"Then why didn't they charge over to stop her? Nothing like them."
"My guess? They're terrified of the Zhu girl's fists and don't dare step in."
Chen Jiangshu shook his head. "Old Madam Xia has seen real storms in her life—no youngster scares her. Besides, didn't she march right over to make trouble when you and Third Sister-in-law egged her on last time? Wasn't scared of fists then."
"Put like that, I'm stumped too," Madam Bao admitted.
"I bet I know," Chen Haodong speculated. "The Zhu girl just won big points smoothing over relations between Grandmother and the elders, so Grandmother and First Aunt held back to save her face. Better for us if they don't step in—otherwise she might buckle under their pressure and let Haoyu out, blowing my plan."
"Makes sense," Chen Jiangshu nodded. "Only worry is they hold off today and cave tomorrow."
"Even if they try, they won't stop her," Madam Bao scoffed. "The Zhu girl's tongue is just as lethal as her fists. When Grandmother confronted her last time, dead in the wrong, she spun out argument after argument until she sounded spotless and left us speechless! Fearless as they come. Now that she knows Haoyu ruined her name, even Grandmother and First Sister-in-law combined won't talk her down!"
Father and son cracked up at Madam Bao's dead-on summary.
When the laughter eased, Chen Jiangshu praised his wife. "Spot on, my dear. Never again will I claim women are shortsighted!"
"Mother came from a scholarly family and read plenty of books," Chen Haodong chimed in. "No wonder she cuts straight to the bone."
Praised by the two men who mattered most to her, Madam Bao glowed with pride.
With the hour growing late, Chen Haodong bade his parents goodnight and returned to his own courtyard. Riding high on triumph, desire stirred in his gut; too late to head out to Chun Jinchan, he looked up at Ye Anya's darkened room. Asleep without waiting up for her husband—instant irritation flared.
He marched up and rapped hard. Knock, knock, knock!
Awake under the covers after sending Xiaocui off to bed, Ye Anya knew who stood outside and pulled the quilt over her head without stirring.
Knock, knock, knock! Still silence. Snapping, Chen Haodong kicked the door wide open, stormed past the screen in the dark, and yanked Ye Anya upright by the shoulder. "Why didn't you open the door?"
Numbed by endless beatings, Ye Anya showed zero fear. "Don't you know? Why ask?"
Smack! A hard slap cracked across her cheek. "You're my wife—supposed to wait up every night and attend me to bed! Instead you crawl under the covers early without a shred of respect! Behave half decent and I wouldn't lay a finger on you!"
Ye Anya gave a cold laugh. "Before our wedding I already heard about you cavorting with courtesans at Chunxiang Brothel. Since marrying you, the sight of you makes my stomach turn. Why would I wait on you?"
Chen Haodong shoved her flat onto the mattress and pinned her beneath him. She offered no struggle, just turned her head away, every line of her face frozen in moonlight with a chill that radiated straight through his ribs.
"I'm a man—what's wrong with playing around with a few women outside? Learn from Lin Jue's wife: gentle, dutiful, even used her own silver to buy Chun Jinqiao out—"
Spit hit his cheek square on. "Want me like that? Wait for your next life—no, not even then!"
Chen Haodong wiped the saliva off his face. "The colder you act, the more I want to claim you. I'm in a good mood tonight, so I'll go easy."
Closing her eyes, Ye Anya went numb while he forced himself on her. Not one happy night since her wedding—when would this nightmare end, and when could she be with the man she loved? Thoughts of Shuzhen rose in the dark, bold enough to haul Haoyu out of a brothel and lock him behind bars. Tomorrow she would go talk to her.
Over in Third Brother Chen Jiangmu's room, husband and wife chatted as they got ready for bed.
Removing her hairpins at the mirror, Madam Cui sighed. "Eldest Brother and Sister-in-law never should have sealed this match for Haoyu. Look at the poor boy suffering now."
Soaking his feet in a hot basin filled by a maid, Chen Jiangmu shrugged. "You know why—Prefect Zhu marched right to the gate. Eldest Brother had no choice. Married now; accept it."
Setting her ornaments in her box, Madam Cui crouched beside him and dipped her hands into the basin to wash his feet. "Still can't believe Haoyu started those rumors last year. Why on earth did he do that? Never heard of them crossing paths before."
Closing his eyes as his wife kneaded his arches, Chen Jiangmu murmured, "Haoyu roams outside day and night—who knows what trouble he gets into?"
"Thank goodness our Haofei is steady—never gave me a day of grief, nose always buried in his books."
"I'd rather he learned family trade, yet his heart's glued to scholarship."
Finishing up, Madam Cui stood, dried her hands on a towel handed by a maid, while another maid handed Chen Jiangmu a cloth to dry his feet. Once the maids withdrew and the lamp went out, both lay down.
"Haofei's two cousins each hold strong cards," Madam Cui reflected in the dark. "One charms Old Madam Xia, the other is a shark in trade. Neither you nor your son has the stomach to fight them for the family business. Studying for official exams is a smart separate path—merchants always rank beneath scholar-officials anyway. Once Haofei wins a degree, we become the parents of a court official! Talk about honor!"
Rolling over with his back to her, Chen Jiangmu murmured, "True enough. That's why I back him even if I'd prefer trade."
"Who do you think ends up running the Chen fortune?" she asked.
Chen Jiangmu yawned. "Count Haofei out."
"I mean between Haodong and Haoyu—who do you want?"
"Isn't it obvious?" he mumbled.
"You mean Haodong?" Hearing only a steady snore in answer, Madam Cui whispered to the ceiling, "As things stand, Haodong looks like a sure bet."
At dawn, Chen Haodong hurried off to the silk house, leaving Ye Anya breathing free air—wishing deep down he would roam outside forever like Haoyu. With heavy thoughts on her mind, she skipped sleeping in, ate an early breakfast, and walked to Moxiang Courtyard with Xiaocui.
Qianli Xun had just stepped to her courtyard gate, bound for Old Madam Xia's rooms to lay out her "discipline" plan, when she spotted Ye Anya approaching.
"Early visit, Eldest Sister-in-law! Must have something to talk over. My errand can wait—come chat!"
Ye Anya felt a rush of admiration at Shuzhen's resilience: furious enough yesterday to hog-tie her husband, yet bright and unclouded this morning. How did a spirit this sunny write such heartbreaking poetry? Perhaps pouring sorrow onto paper left her heart light in daily life.
"Am I interrupting anything?" Ye Anya asked hesitantly.
Taking her hand to lead her inside, Qianli Xun smiled. "Never! You're welcome anytime."
"Then let me sit in your room a moment and speak from the heart."
Sensing bedroom confidences coming, Qianli Xun led her straight into the inner parlor of Suxin Thatched Hall, where a woven rug lay under a low square tea table flanked by short stools that felt almost like sitting on the floor. Without being told, Kuqiao began whisking tea in the outer hall.
Seated, Ye Anya opened with yesterday's news. "I heard you locked Haoyu up! You have real nerve—aren't you worried Grandmother and Eldest Uncle and Aunt will chew you out?"
"If even you heard, they sure heard too," Qianli Xun observed. "Not a single one showed up at my door, which means either they know they're in the wrong, or they secretly approve."
Careless of the elders' motives, Ye Anya praised from the heart, "Bold, decisive, doing what you please—I envy you more than I can say."
Qianli Xun grinned at herself. "Don't you know folks call me a tigress behind my back?"
"Call it an insult if they like—to me it sounds like praise," Ye Anya smiled. "How many women in this world have the guts to march into a brothel, drag their husband home in ropes, and lock him up? Pure courage."
"You might be the only soul in Chen Manor who sees it that way."
Ye Anya paused, gathering every ounce of nerve. "Shuzhen, I wanted to ask your advice last time, yet felt too shy on our first real chat. Today I'm swallowing my pride to ask straight out—please don't hold back."
Remembering the embrace beneath the Divine Willow, Qianli Xun kept her tone careful. "No need to stand on ceremony, Eldest Sister-in-law. Speak your mind."
Ye Anya plunged right in. "You went through a mutual divorce once. Could you tell me how you managed it?"
Right then Kuqiao walked in carrying a tray with two bowls of whisked tea, catching the question mid-air. Hearing the dead seriousness behind it, the scene beneath the Divine Willow flashed through her mind. Awkward at overhearing, she set both bowls on the table without a word.
"Kuqiao, wait in the outer hall and keep everyone out," Qianli Xun ordered.
Ye Anya nodded to Xiaocui, who stepped outside behind Kuqiao.
Studying the raw hope in Ye Anya's eyes, Qianli Xun weighed her words with care—unwilling to wreck another woman's marriage carelessly without hearing the full truth. "A few days ago, I went out to the Divine Willow beyond the city... and stumbled onto an unexpected sight."
Catching the hint at once—and having already asked about divorce—Ye Anya dropped all pretense. "Since you saw, you already know why I'm asking."
"How long has it been?"
"We loved each other before I ever married into the Chen house. Because the Ye and Cao families have feuded for generations, my father forbade us to wed and betrothed me to the Chens instead. For a while I tried to accept my fate—until the very night before our wedding, when Xiaocui heard outside that Chen Haodong was spending the night with a brothel courtesan. Disgust took root in my bones, and I haven't gone one day without loathing him."
Ye Anya pushed back her sleeve, revealing angry purple-and-yellow bruises blotching her pale forearm. "Because I stay cold toward him, whenever Chen Haodong wants... his way, he uses his fists. My body is never free of bruises. I cannot live another day like this, so I thought of mutual divorce—only I have no idea where to start. Since you lived through it, I came to ask you."
Qianli Xun reached across and drew Ye Anya's sleeve back down over the bruises. "I had no idea you were going through hell, Eldest Sister-in-law. Are you certain you want to take the step of mutual divorce?"
Ye Anya nodded hard. "My mind is set, or I never would have dared ask."
Having survived the gauntlet herself and looking at fragile Ye Anya across the table, Qianli Xun feared she might shatter under the backlash. "Mutual divorce is brutal, Eldest Sister-in-law. Think long and hard before you leap."
#CH 229
#TITLE: Putting the Ugly Truth Up Front
Ye Anya drained her tea bowl in one gulp, dabbed her mouth with a silk handkerchief, and spoke steady and firm. "Don't worry for me, Sister. If you truly want what's best for me, tell me how to end this marriage."
Reflecting on her own tangled path since arriving in the Song, Qianli Xun felt a deep pang. Compared with Ye Anya, she had been lucky—neither Lin Jue nor Chen Haoyu stood a chance against her fists, whereas her sister-in-law endured Chen Haodong's violence helplessly. She owed her the honest truth.
"In this era, mutual divorce for a woman is uphill through thorns. I moved heaven and earth and still only pulled it off because Prince Yi stepped in to pressure my father and Lin Jue into signing."
"Prince Yi stepped in?" Anxiety clouded Ye Anya's face. "So it really is that hard..."
Qianli Xun took a slow sip of tea. "Yes. Sometimes I wonder if I ever would have escaped Lin Manor if Prince Yi hadn't come to Lin'an."
Her mind drifted to Shen Jianchen—helping her divorce Lin Jue the instant they crossed paths, helping win over Qiao Manor, helping stamp out last year's rumors...
Seeing Shuzhen fall quiet, Ye Anya asked in a low voice, "Did Prince Yi already have feelings for you back then?"
After the wedding raid, the question was natural enough. Wanting to stay honest without sparking fresh gossip, Qianli Xun replied, "You must have heard about the wedding raid. Whatever Prince Yi felt back then I cannot say, yet everything between us has always been clean. Princess Jiahe was my closest friend—how could I ever betray her? What happened on my wedding day blindsided me completely."
Ye Anya sighed. "We're two miserable souls in the same boat."
Steering off Prince Yi, Qianli Xun returned to the point. "If you truly want a mutual divorce, Eldest Sister-in-law, you don't just have to win over Eldest Cousin-in-law—you need both families' elders to agree. If a single side refuses, mutual divorce hits a wall."
Twisting her silk handkerchief between pale fingers, Ye Anya fell silent in distress. Looks like she's backing down, Qianli Xun thought.
Instead, Ye Anya lifted her chin. "I'll find a way to persuade them one by one. And if all else fails, I'll push Chen Haodong to repudiate me."
Remembering Kuqiao's warning from her earliest days in the Song, Qianli Xun asked sharp and fast, "Do you have sisters back at your maiden home?"
"Two younger sisters."
"Then under no circumstances let him repudiate you!"
"I know being cast out ruins my sisters' marriage prospects," Ye Anya whispered, "yet I cannot bear one more day of this. Worst comes to worst, let the Ye family disown me as a daughter."
Moved by the steel beneath her fragile exterior, Qianli Xun clasped her hands. "You're rarer than most women in this era, Eldest Sister-in-law. Just remember: whatever path you choose, plan every step in advance—and keep yourself safe."
Ye Anya squeezed her fingers back. "Thank you, Shuzhen. Watching you gave me the nerve to try."
Qianli Xun had no words, nagged by quiet dread that her influence might push Ye Anya into a fire she could not survive.
With everything said, Ye Anya took her leave. Seeing her out beyond Moxiang Courtyard, Qianli Xun sent a silent wish after her: May Eldest Sister-in-law find happiness.
Why not wish her a successful divorce? she asked herself—and saw the answer at once. Succeed, and she won happiness with the man she loved; fail, and she still had to live on, needing every blessing she could get.
Setting Ye Anya's troubles aside for her own pressing business, Qianli Xun headed straight for Old Madam Xia's courtyard. Knowing her father-in-law and mother-in-law gathered there every morning after breakfast, one trip would let her face the whole clan at once instead of repeating herself room by room.
Approaching the gate, lively family chatter drifted from indoors—a full house. When a yard maid started forward to announce her, Qianli Xun raised a hand. "No need. I'll walk straight in."
She wanted to catch the elders' unfiltered reactions before they had time to put on polite masks.
Recognizing Second Uncle's voice rising inside, Qianli Xun stepped boldly across the threshold. Conversation snapped off mid-sentence.
Old Madam Xia, Yao Qingyan, and Chen Jianglin all greeted her with warm smiles—relieving her at once, though she had come braced for a showdown over the rumors either way. Both aunts wore sour expressions laced with gleeful anticipation of drama, exposing how paper-thin their polite harmony was. Both uncles masked their feelings better, yet one quick glance and lowered eyes proved they already knew Chen Haoyu was under lock and key.
"Shuzhen, come sit!" Old Madam Xia called warmly.
Instead of sitting, Qianli Xun dropped straight to her knees, with Kuqiao kneeling beside her.
"What are you kneeling for?" Old Madam Xia asked.
"Grandmother, yesterday I marched Chen Haoyu home from a brothel in ropes and locked him in the back courtyard. In my own conscience I did nothing wrong, yet I feared the Chen elders might disagree, so I kneel first to beg pardon."
Before Old Madam Xia could speak, Chen Jiangmu burst out, "If you know you crossed the line, hurry up and let Haoyu out instead of kneeling here for show—"
Old Madam Xia raised one hand, silencing her third son on the spot. "What pardon do you need? Get up at once."
Rising alongside a relieved Kuqiao, Qianli Xun tested the waters. "Grandmother, Father-in-law, Mother-in-law—you truly aren't angry with me?"
"When were we angry?" Chen Jianglin asked. "We put Haoyu's discipline in your hands; handle him however you see fit."
"We heard yesterday and didn't lift a finger to stop you," Yao Qingyan added. "That shows we back you completely. Discipline him whichever way works."
Old Madam Xia chuckled. "You have my word: while you're straightening Haoyu out, none of the three of us will get in your way."
Chen Jianglin backing her came as no surprise; Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan not even slipping in one plea for mercy baffled her.
Finding the elders' blind faith laughable, Chen Jiangshu traded a look with Madam Bao, who caught his cue at once. "Funny—yesterday Mother and Eldest Sister-in-law looked panicked when they heard Haoyu was tied up, yet today they don't mind a bit! Oh, wait—as I left yesterday, I spotted Matron Wei heading into Mother's courtyard. Must have put in a good word for Shuzhen! That matron of yours is truly something else."
Whatever poison Madam Bao meant to stir, she just solved Qianli Xun's mystery. Good old Matron Wei had already paved the whole road for her last night!
"Shuzhen isn't your daughter-in-law—keep your nose out of it!" Old Madam Xia snapped at Madam Bao.
Madam Bao pressed her lips together, while Madam Cui swallowed the comment she had been about to offer.
Old Madam Xia beamed at Qianli Xun again. "Sit down already! Why stand?"
Qianli Xun stayed on her feet. With the lockup approved, one thorn still sat raw in her chest—useless to vent or not, she needed it off her tongue. "Let me stay standing, Grandmother. What I have to say next might displease you."
Guessing at once, Old Madam Xia beat her to it. "About Haoyu spreading those rumors?"
"Yes."
"All water under the bridge. You're married in now—we're one family. No sense dredging up the past."
Those three words—"one family"—nearly dulled Qianli Xun's edge with unexpected warmth, until Chen Haoyu's smug grin flashed back in her mind. "Granddaughter-in-law wants to know one thing: knowing full well Chen Haoyu spread slander that ruined my happiness, why did the elders still agree to this match? Tying two bitter enemies together as man and wife—if that isn't hurting me, what is?"
The blunt blast landed like a cannonball—half the elders squirming, the other half secretly licking their lips.
Chen Jianglin let out a heavy sigh. "We understand your resentment, Shuzhen. Yet when Prefect Zhu marched Haoyu in trussed up and laid out what he'd done, I was frantic and furious. When Prefect Zhu proposed this match, it seemed the right way to make amends, so I didn't think deeper. Haoyu caused the mess; he had to take full responsibility."
"Plenty of ways to take responsibility—why shove me into a marriage trap and dress it up as noble?"
Yao Qingyan rose and clasped Shuzhen's hands. "How is Chen Manor a trap? Have you suffered one ounce of mistreatment since arriving?"
Honestly, no—even Old Madam Xia's "trouble" had been an act—yet the knot in her chest refused to loosen. "The Chen family is wonderful and the elders treat me well, except Chen Haoyu... we're oil and water. He is nowhere near the husband I ever wanted."
Yao Qingyan laughed. "Then whip him into the exact husband you want! Wouldn't that be perfection?"
Old Madam Xia and Chen Jianglin joined in chuckling.
Madam Bao sneered inside: Can't discipline their own son and expect a bride to do miracles—laughable!
Madam Cui thought: Never heard of a wife reforming a rake! If the Zhu girl whips Haoyu into shape, I'll write my surname Cui upside down!
Chen Jiangmu found it absurd too, yet kept quiet seeing his eldest brother smiling.
After holding back, Chen Jiangshu finally spoke up. "Mother, Eldest Brother—if you ask me, send Haoyu to the Chen shops under my wing to learn trade while I keep an eye on him. Sounds far better to outsiders than letting a new bride discipline her husband! Shuzhen's fists are impressive, sure, yet raising a man takes more than fists."
Madam Bao knew her husband had zero interest in actually mentoring Haoyu—just taking a clean dig at Eldest Brother's failed parenting and mocking his choice of enforcer.
"Shuzhen is doing splendidly," Old Madam Xia shut it down. "Case closed. End of discussion."
Not wanting the job anyway, Chen Jiangshu bowed his head. "Whatever Mother thinks best."
Old Madam Xia turned back to Shuzhen. "I know you married in reluctantly, and learning he started those rumors makes your blood boil. Still, nobody held a knife to your throat on your wedding day. Call it tangled fate—looks like an ill-starred bond right now, yet Grandmother trusts you to turn ill fate into good fortune."
Still holding her hands, Yao Qingyan added, "Mother's right—ill fate can turn sweet. Put the rumors behind you, Shuzhen; bright days lie ahead. If anger still burns, treat disciplining him as venting your spleen—"
Finding their optimism wildly naive—expecting revenge to blossom into romance—Qianli Xun seized Yao Qingyan's slip to set firm ground rules upfront. "Since Mother-in-law put it that way, I'll take you at your word: I'll treat disciplining Chen Haoyu purely as venting my grudge. If he doesn't turn into the paragon you elders hope for, don't come regretting today's choice."
Chen Jiangshu grinned inside: Vent all you want—makes my son's victory easier!
Madam Bao gloated silently: Smooth talk, Eldest Sister-in-law—dug your own pit and jumped right in!
Third Uncle and Aunt exchanged scandalized looks at such brazen arrogance, yet held their tongues.
Dropping Shuzhen's hands in alarm, Yao Qingyan fluttered, "I only joked about venting—don't take it literally!" Retreating to her chair, she shot an anxious glance at Chen Jianglin.
Qianli Xun knew her warning sounded blunt, yet ugly truths belonged upfront: she was out for payback, not miracle-working, and had zero intention of taking blame if Chen Haoyu stayed hopeless.
#CH 230
#TITLE: Qinggua Seduces Chouya
Catching Yao Qingyan's panicked look, Chen Jianglin cleared his throat to reassure both his wife and his daughter-in-law. "Since marrying in, Shuzhen helped us three brothers finally understand Mother's heart. That proves she is thoughtful and deeply kind."
"Deeply kind" echoed Matron Wei's exact words from yesterday, easing the knot in Yao Qingyan's chest. Right—Shuzhen isn't cruel; why am I panicking?
"Nothing is impossible to a willing heart," Chen Jianglin went on. "I trust you, Shuzhen. Even if you can't reform him, I won't blame you or regret it. Either he shapes up and we all rejoice, or he doesn't and I wash my hands of him—every child walks his own fate. All I ask is that after I'm gone, whoever takes the helm of the Chen family leaves Haoyu a warm bowl of rice, and that you don't abandon him."
Fair and heartfelt words—leaving Qianli Xun unable to keep bristling. Chen Haoyu was the villain, not these elders; no sense taking her rage out on them.
Chen Jiangshu and Madam Bao perked up at Chen Jianglin's phrasing—clearly Eldest Brother had already written Haoyu off as heir, and Haofei cared only for books, leaving the Chen fortune practically handed to Haodong! Both hid pleased smiles.
Chen Jiangmu jumped in. "What talk is that, Eldest Brother? What kind of house are the Chens that Haoyu would ever be down to a plain bowl of rice? Even if he plays around for life, he'll eat fine meats and fish whenever he walks home!"
"Exact right!" Madam Cui echoed. "How could Eldest Brother, Sister-in-law, and Mother let Haoyu go hungry? Between his own share, his cousins looking out for him, and his wife—Shuzhen holds her own dowry plus all Haoyu's personal properties, richer than almost any lady in Lin'an! Haoyu won't suffer a day!"
Watching her granddaughter-in-law's cool face, Old Madam Xia sensed she still had little real interest in reforming Haoyu and felt a dull pang. Annoyed by Third Son and his wife jumping straight to dividing estates, she cut them off. "Enough! I'm not dead yet, and you're already planning Haoyu's welfare after the elders are gone! As the saying goes, 'A worthy wife outweighs ten thousand acres of fertile land.' Haoyu earned blessings in a past life to marry Shuzhen—with a wife like her, why fret over his future?"
Worthy wife, maybe—too bad I got stuck with a grudge against Chen Haoyu that turned me into everyone's favorite tigress! Qianli Xun thought.
Madam Bao scoffed silently: Worthy wife? Pure tigress!
Madam Cui snickered inside: Sweet face, lethal fists—never heard of a "worthy wife" tying her husband up and locking him in a back room! Old Madam Xia's bar for worthy wives sure dropped low!
"Shuzhen," Old Madam Xia continued, "if you think well of your father-in-law, mother-in-law, and me, leave those old rumors in the past. Good or bad, yesterday is gone—people have to look forward. Focus purely on disciplining Haoyu from now on; no need to come pay morning respects or worry about keeping me entertained. The storyteller knows the path by heart now, and this big family has plenty of people to keep me company. No need to report your methods either—handle him as you please. Go on back now."
Not wanting Shuzhen to vent further resentment and give Second and Third Sister-in-law more fodder to gloat over, Old Madam Xia closed the session. Qianli Xun curtsied and left.
Walking back, she felt the trip had been pointless: Matron Wei had already cleared the elders' approval, yet the sour lump still sat in her chest. Better to stay away from the main courtyard until her temper cooled, and focus instead on creative "discipline" for Chen Haoyu.
Earlier that morning, after Hongdou delivered breakfast to Boya Study and they ate, Chen Haoyu shot Qinggua a look.
Catching his cue, Qinggua edged up to Hongdou as she stacked bowls. "Could you do me a favor and call Chouya over here?"
"What do you want Chouya for?" Hongdou asked.
"Don't get the wrong idea—I don't like her! Just need to ask her something."
Having noticed Chouya's crush on Qinggua ages ago, Hongdou happily seized the chance to pass Qinggua off onto her and get him off her own back. "Hang on, I'll send her over. She can't come inside though—she'll have to talk through the window."
"Window works fine!" Qinggua escorted her to the door until the padlock snapped shut again.
"Why pick Chouya?" Chen Haoyu asked.
Qinggua turned back. "You handed me a brutal mission, Young Master—Chouya's my only shot! With a nickname like 'Ugly Girl,' she's way easier to charm."
"What's the other maid's name?"
"Amei."
"Amei—means pretty, right? Must be a looker."
"Pretty plain actually, nowhere near Hongdou. She's Asheng's younger sister."
"Brother and sister both slaving under Zhu Shuzhen—hopeless."
Qinggua disagreed. "You grew up rich, Young Master, so you don't know how hard poor folks have it. Poor families celebrate landing a servant post in a wealthy house, especially under a good mistress like Young Madam. Both siblings get to learn martial arts from her—half the manor envies them!"
"Shoo, shoo! Quit singing Zhu Shuzhen's praises in front of me! You're my man—don't let her buy you off!"
"Relax, Young Master. Loyal to you to the bone."
Right then a voice floated outside the boarded window. "Qinggua? Sister Hongdou said you wanted me?"
Chouya was not truly hideous—just plump with narrow, squinting eyes, less pleasing to look at than the other maids, which had saddled her with the nickname Chouya, literally "Ugly Girl."
"She's here! Go turn on the charm!" Chen Haoyu whispered, dropping into a chair to watch the show.
For his master—and for the promise of winning Hongdou later—Qinggua braced himself and leaned close to the shutters. "Chouya, Young Madam locked me up in here, and it's been sixteen hours since I last saw you. I begged Hongdou to call you just so I could hear your voice."
