Chen Haoyu's brows shot up. Who knew Qinggua could spin smooth lies without blinking? Gotta watch this kid from now on—and ugh, what syrupy cheese!
A bashful flutter answered from outside. "I thought you only had eyes for Sister Hongdou! Never dreamed you had feelings for me... truth is, I like you too."
Qinggua froze stiff—fake flirting had reeled in real feelings! Struck dumb, he stood gaping.
Thrilled, Chen Haoyu scrambled over and hissed in his ear, "Say something! She's into you—this is in the bag!"
Nudged forward, Qinggua stammered, "Then... we feel the same way about each other..."
"Get to the point!" Chen Haoyu hissed.
"I'm dying to see you, yet I'm trapped in here! Could you unlock the door for me?"
Chouya hesitated. "I want to see you too, yet Miss locked you up. What if I beg her to let you out when she gets back?"
"No use—Young Madam will never let me out! Who knows how long I'll rot in here without seeing your face? Chouya, I miss you."
"I miss you too!"
"I didn't even do anything wrong, yet she threw me in here keeping us apart..."
"Wait right there, Qinggua! I'll sneak the key and let you out!"
Hearing her footsteps hurry off, master and servant slapped palms in triumph.
"So who sneaks out first, Young Master—me or you?"
"Me, obviously! Let's see—should I run straight to Grandmother and my parents, or bolt outside? Forget the elders, I'll bolt straight out of the manor and hide somewhere Zhu Shuzhen can never find me! Deal!"
Minutes later the padlock rattled. Both sprang to the door ready to dash—only for Chouya to slip inside and slam the door shut behind her, cutting off every inch of escape.
The instant she saw Qinggua, Chouya threw her arms right around him. "Qinggua! I'm so happy you actually like me too!"
Pinned in her bear hug, Qinggua stiffened, forcing himself to endure for freedom's sake.
While Chouya was distracted, Chen Haoyu reached quietly for the door latch—only for Chouya to pivot, still hugging Qinggua, and block the door solid. "Going somewhere, Young Master?"
Impressed by Zhu Shuzhen's sharp maids, Chen Haoyu tried a bluff. "Don't let me out and I'll order Qinggua to dump you! He's my servant—he obeys my every word!"
Releasing the hug, Chouya clasped Qinggua's hand tight. "Don't try scaring me, Young Master. Qinggua and I love each other—nobody can tear us apart!"
Chen Haoyu switched to sweet-talking. "Let me walk out, and I give my blessing to you two!"
"Your blessing doesn't matter," Chouya replied flatly. "I'm only taking Qinggua out. You stay right in here, Young Master."
"Let go of my hand a second, Chouya," Qinggua said. "Let me whisper a word to Young Master."
Obediently, she let go. Leaning to Chen Haoyu's ear, Qinggua breathed, "Hang tight a bit longer, Young Master. Let me get out first and I'll figure out a way to spring you from outside!"
Chen Haoyu nodded and waved to Chouya. "Fine, take Qinggua. I'll stay right here enjoying my lockup."
Watching the door open, sunlight flash in and vanish, and the padlock snap shut again, Chen Haoyu rushed to the crack yelling, "Qinggua! Don't forget to go straight to Old Madam Xia!"
"Count on me, Young Master!" Qinggua called back.
Outside, Chouya clung tight to his arm. Helpless, Qinggua coaxed, "Hurry and put the key back before anyone notices it's gone!"
Only then did she let go. "While Miss is out, go hide in the west garden pavilion and wait for me! I'll slip the key back to Asheng in the West Courtyard first!"
The second Chouya hurried off, Qinggua sighed in relief and sprinted straight out of Moxiang Courtyard toward Old Madam Xia's rooms—nearly colliding halfway with Qianli Xun and Kuqiao walking back! Diving behind a rockery until they passed, he popped back out and bolted to Old Madam Xia's courtyard.
Inside, after Shuzhen left, the family had moved back to chatting while waiting for the storyteller; all three brothers had cleared their morning to stay and listen with their mother.
Bursting past an empty yard straight into the hall, Qinggua threw himself to the floor squeezing out loud wails. "Old Madam! Save Young Master! Young Madam locked him up! Won't let him breathe free air! Boo-hoo!"
Chen Jiangmu frowned. "Haoyu's wife went too far! Mother, since we're all here, lead us over and tell her to let him out!"
"Yes, Mother, go help Haoyu!" Madam Cui urged.
Old Madam Xia lifted one hand to halt them. "Quit wailing, Qinggua. Go tell Haoyu that from today on, neither his parents nor I will meddle in his courtyard. Sink or swim, he's on his own."
Shocked dry of tears, Qinggua gaped before stammering, "You can't abandon him, Old Madam! Young Madam's fists are lethal—Young Master's life is in danger!"
Secretly thrilled, Chen Jiangshu and Madam Bao kept smooth poker faces and stayed dead silent—let Haoyu take his beating!
Turning to Chen Jianglin, Qinggua begged, "Master, he's your own flesh and blood! You can't stand by!"
"Tell Haoyu I'm thrilled she locked him up," Chen Jianglin said coldly. "Why would I rescue him? He made his bed—let him lie in it."
Desperate, Qinggua swung toward Yao Qingyan. "Madam—"
Hardening her heart, Yao Qingyan cut him off. "Not another word! Live or die, Haoyu answers to his wife now. Don't bother me with Moxiang Courtyard's business again!"
"Servants!" Old Madam Xia snapped. Two men stepped in. "Escort Qinggua out!"
Dragged outside just as the storyteller arrived, Qinggua lingered until the clapper sounded and laughter rose within, then trudged off with a heavy sigh.
#CH 231
#TITLE: The Torture Plan Begins
Returning from Old Madam Xia's courtyard into Moxiang Courtyard, Qianli Xun ran straight into Chouya rushing past with rosy, glowing cheeks.
"Miss," Chouya curtsied nervously.
"What's the rush?"
Flustered, Chouya fibbed, "N-nothing! Got chores to do—won't keep you!" and scampered off.
"What's gotten into Chouya today?" Qianli Xun wondered aloud.
"Looked scared you'd scold her," Kuqiao guessed.
"Nah—looked more like she's rushing off to meet a secret boyfriend!" Qianli Xun joked, dismissing it.
Reaching Suxin Thatched Hall, she remembered Chen Haoyu—time to roll out her payback schedule. "Go fetch the key to Boya Study."
While Kuqiao ran to the West Courtyard, Qianli Xun strolled into the back courtyard. Glancing first at Jingyi Hall's shut door—Matron Wei out handling business as usual—gratitude warmed her chest for everything the matron quietly did behind the scenes. Then she faced Boya Study. Locking Chen Haoyu up forever solved nothing and didn't even scratch her itch for payback; better lay out her terms face to face.
Kuqiao returned with the key and clicked open the heavy padlock.
Inside, Chen Haoyu had sat waiting for Qinggua's rescue. At the click of the lock, he leaped up and hurried to the door expecting his grandmother's doting face—only to find Zhu Shuzhen and Kuqiao standing alone on the threshold, not another soul behind them.
Qianli Xun shot him a smirk. "Still waiting for the Chen elders to ride to your rescue? Hate to break it to you, they handed me full authority to discipline you—and I plan to put it to great use making your life miserable."
"Liar! Grandmother dotes on me and Mother's softhearted—no way they handed me over to you!" Seeing the door wide open, he lunged to bolt past her.
Qianli Xun stepped squarely across the doorway. "Running? Dream on. That door can stay propped open all day, and while I stand here, you aren't taking one step out."
Shaking a furious finger at her speechless, Chen Haoyu spun back and slumped into a chair. Catching his breath, he snapped, "Fine—what does it take for you to let me out?"
"I came here right now to let you out."
Suspicion narrowed his eyes. "You're being that generous?"
"Hardly generous." Qianli Xun nodded to Kuqiao. "I dictate, you write."
Kuqiao sat at the desk and spread a sheet of paper.
Facing Chen Haoyu, Qianli Xun declared, "Starting today, you may leave Boya Study—just not Chen Manor."
"Says who?!"
"Says my fists, and says the Chen elders who put me in charge of whipping you into shape."
"I don't buy one word of your story!"
"Believe it or not, be smart and listen close."
Chen Haoyu clapped both hands over his ears. "Not listening! Not listening! Do your worst!"
Fed up with his racket, Qianli Xun stepped in and tapped two acupoints. Instantly he froze mute and rigid, reduced to glaring daggers.
"Peace and quiet at last. Now listen to how I'm disciplining you. First, your entire monthly allowance is canceled. Want spending money? Earn it yourself."
Unable to speak, Chen Haoyu's fists clenched white inside his sleeves: Canceling even twenty taels?! Outrageous! Grown up with robes handed to me and meals spoon-fed—when have I ever earned silver?!
"Second, here is your wage scale. Item one: run ten laps around Chen Manor's main road daily for ten wen. Item two: memorize assigned classics and poetry daily for thirty wen. Item three: complete one daily chore for fifty wen. Item four: say 'I'm sorry' to someone you've bullied for one tael of silver."
Improvised on the spot—half torture, half boot camp, every task guaranteed to make Chen Haoyu crawl out of his skin—she beamed in satisfaction. "Four items to start; I'll add more as they occur to me. Got all that, Kuqiao?"
Kuqiao laid down her brush. "Every word."
"Good. Get his thumbprint on it."
Paralyzed, Chen Haoyu could only turn purple with rage as Qianli Xun pried his thumb open, pressed it into the red ink paste Kuqiao held out, and stamped it firm onto the contract.
Done, she tapped open his acupoints.
The instant feeling flooded back, Chen Haoyu swung a wild punch at her. "Zhu Shuzhen! I'm not doing a single one of those things!"
Catching his wrist effortlessly, Qianli Xun twisted his arm up behind his back. "Glad you reminded me—forgot the penalties! Skip one task, empty my chamber pot for one day! Skip two, two days! Skip all four, ten straight days! Resist altogether, and you empty my chamber pot for life!"
"Ow! Chen Manor has spotless latrines everywhere—nobody uses chamber pots for number two, only urinals! Joke's on you!" he yelped through the pain.
Qianli Xun grinned wickedly. "Easy fix! Just to punish you, I'll start using a chamber pot exclusively for number two—stink you right out of your boots!"
Outmatched in muscle and outmatched in wit, Chen Haoyu tried softening his tone. "Look, Zhu Shuzhen, what if I just apologize right now? Let it go! Spreading rumors was my fault—hit me, curse me, do whatever, just don't cripple me!"
Seeing him fold, Qianli Xun let go. "Kuqiao, go call Hongdou."
Jotting down the chamber-pot penalty on the thumbprinted sheet, Kuqiao hurried off to summon Hongdou.
Relieved the door stayed unlocked, Chen Haoyu sank back into his chair. "Zhu Shuzhen, my biggest regret in life is marrying you. What about you? Regret marrying me?"
Ever since choosing to walk down the aisle, Qianli Xun had forbidden herself from wasting breath on regret. "Whether I regret it is none of your damn business!"
"Bet you regret it too," Chen Haoyu probed. "Why don't we get a mutual divorce and set you free? Prince Yi's clearly crazy about you—infatuated as he is, he won't care if you've divorced twice! Why not sit down calm and hash out a mutual divorce?"
The words "mutual divorce" struck an unexpected hollow note in her chest. After her first divorce she had dreamed of a happy match, only for her second husband to bring up divorce right out of the gate. Not that she wanted one—she simply had no energy left for that war; her first divorce had drained every ounce of fight out of her.
Spotting sadness cross her face, Chen Haoyu lit up and teased, "Wait—don't tell me you fell for me and can't bear leaving? Makes sense, tall and handsome as I am, beloved by every maiden in town! Since you like me, quit torturing me and treat me sweet—maybe I'll get in a good mood and like you back! Hahaha!"
Snapping out of her gloom, disgust flared hot at his leering smirk. "Shut your filthy mouth! Want a divorce? Too bad—I'm refusing just to spite you! Maybe once I've tortured you raw and my grudge cools, I'll think about it."
Too smart to blow up, he kept needle-poking. "Heard you fought tooth and nail to divorce Lin Jue, yet now I hand you a divorce on a silver platter and you say no? If that isn't secret love, what is? I never turn down a pretty woman—go ahead, throw yourself in my arms!"
Nothing set Qianli Xun's fist flying faster than a sleazy smirk. Crack! One clean punch landed square on Chen Haoyu's left cheek.
Clutching a bruised face swelling blue on the spot, he gasped, "Are you crazy?!"
Qianli Xun shook out her knuckles. "You're the crazy one! Keep flirting like a creep—I hate that leering mug more than anything!"
Sweat stinging the fresh welt forced his hand down. "Acting like a monster like this—heaven knows what Qiao Wenxian and Prince Yi ever saw in you!"
Right then Hongdou and Kuqiao stepped in, struggling to stifle grins at the comic purple shiner blooming across his face.
Kuqiao retrieved the rule sheet while Hongdou asked, "Your orders, Miss?"
"Assign two men to shadow Chen Haoyu starting now. Make sure he completes every task on this sheet daily."
"Yes, Miss—right away."
Kuqiao handed the sheet to Hongdou. "Have the watchers memorize every line, then bring it back to me." Hongdou hurried off.
"Can I walk out now?" Chen Haoyu asked.
"Not until your shadows arrive."
So long as the padlock stayed off, Chen Haoyu figured he could run straight to Grandmother and weep his case. Wait—why hadn't Qinggua come back yet? Had Zhu Shuzhen truly bought off the entire Chen clan? "Did Grandmother and my parents really put you in charge of disciplining me?"
"Obviously!" Qianli Xun lifted her chin. "Promised not to meddle one bit, too."
"Don't believe you!"
"Suit yourself. Think I'd unlock your door if I feared you running crying to the elders?"
Hongdou returned with Asheng and another male servant. "Miss, Asheng and Sanbao will watch Young Master from here on out." Returning the paper to Kuqiao, she added, "They memorized every word."
Satisfied, Qianli Xun instructed both men. "Stick to him like glue all day. Do not let him step outside Chen Manor's gates—inside the manor, he can roam wherever he wants. Days out of Boya Study are fine, yet once he goes to bed at night, lock the door so he doesn't slip out in the dark."
"Understood, Miss!" both chorused.
Everything set, Qianli Xun gave Chen Haoyu a cool scoff. "You're free to go."
Chen Haoyu shot out the door with a whoop. "Sweet air of freedom!" Spinning back, he jabbed a finger at her. "Just wait, Zhu Shuzhen—I'm heading straight for Grandmother!"
With Asheng and Sanbao hot on his heels, he marched toward the front yard.
Her revenge campaign had launched, yet Qianli Xun felt strangely flat. Chen Haoyu mentioning divorce and Prince Yi had brought Mengrong right back into her mind—the real reason she had accepted this marriage and refused divorce. Mengrong's furious vow of hatred from the wedding day still echoed in her ears, killing any dare to hope for her own heart.
Following her back into Suxin Thatched Hall, Kuqiao put away the rule sheet and stood quietly beside her mistress as Qianli Xun stared blankly at her untouched desk.
#CH 232
#TITLE: No Rescue, Just Laps
Turned away empty-handed from Old Madam Xia's rooms, Qinggua dared not return to Moxiang Courtyard—and clean forgot his date with Chouya—instead wandering the manor gardens planning to sneak home after dark.
Strolling into the west wing, he looked up to spot Chouya waiting inside the pavilion and froze. Oh no—tried helping Young Master and got myself hooked instead! Better ghost her; once I no-show, she'll get the hint!
Creeping back toward Moxiang Courtyard to wait for an opening, he slipped behind the lush green bamboo flanking the gate—and barely seconds later watched Chen Haoyu stride out with Asheng and Sanbao trailing behind!
If Young Madam let him out, sneaking out didn't matter anymore! Qinggua jumped out from the stalks. "Young Master, Young Madam released you?"
Fuming over waiting half the day with zero rescue, Chen Haoyu cocked a boot—remembered Phantom Sidestep, pulled his foot back, and swung a fist instead. "Sneaking out to enjoy yourself while leaving me locked up? I'll clobber you!"
Qinggua hopped clear. "You're out now, Young Master—let it go!"
Chen Haoyu lowered his fist. "What good is being out with these two glued to my tail?"
"Still beats staring at four walls," Qinggua grinned.
"Quit yakking and come with me to Grandmother's!" Chen Haoyu started off.
Qinggua stepped in front of him. "Save your steps, Young Master—I already went! Old Madam Xia said she's staying out of your courtyard for good; Master said he's thrilled you're locked up and you asked for it; Madam said live or die, you're Young Madam's problem now!"
Convinced Qinggua was talking trash, Chen Haoyu shoved past him and marched straight to Old Madam Xia's gate.
Inside, after the storyteller left and Second and Third Brothers retired with their wives, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan lingered chatting with Old Madam Xia when a maid hurried in. "Old Madam, Second Young Master is out and headed this way!"
"Shut the courtyard gate," Old Madam Xia commanded. "Tell him to quit bothering me."
"Yes, Old Madam."
Seeing distress flicker across Yao Qingyan's face, Old Madam Xia added sternly, "Lock your own courtyard gate when you go back too. Don't go soft—soften one inch and everything collapses." She pressed a hand to her chest. "Hurts right here, believe me, yet we have to endure for Haoyu's own good."
Yao Qingyan bit her lip and stayed silent.
"My fault for failing to raise him right and putting you both through this," Chen Jianglin said. "Make or break right here—we all have to hold firm." Both women nodded.
Arriving to find the gate bolted shut, Chen Haoyu had Qinggua knock. A maid answered through the wood: "Go back, Second Young Master. Old Madam won't meddle in your affairs anymore."
"A lowly maid talking back to me?" Chen Haoyu roared. "Wait till I have Grandmother sell you off tomorrow!"
"This servant wouldn't dare—Old Madam ordered me to say it."
Switching tactics, Chen Haoyu put on a shrill wail toward the gate. "Grandmother! You can't abandon me! Sure the tigress unlocked my room, yet she's cruel as a demon! Assigned two thugs to tail me nonstop, piled chores on my head—and worst of all, she's forcing me to empty her chamber pot! Boo-hoo! Save me or I'm a dead man!"
At "empty her chamber pot," Yao Qingyan flinched. "How can Shuzhen make Haoyu do something that filthy? He's never even poured his own washbasin!"
Old Madam Xia surged half off her couch. "Unthinkable! Unthinkable!" Then she froze and sank back down. "Hold firm... bring me cotton to plug my ears!"
A maid stuffed cotton into the old lady's ears, yet her grandson's howling still bled through, forcing her to clench her teeth. Seeing Old Madam Xia hold the line, Yao Qingyan swallowed her plea. Only Chen Jianglin sat calm as stone. "No pain, no gain. Spoiling him turned him rotten—at this rate he'd be worth less than a night-soil collector! Emptying a chamber pot is getting off easy."
Getting zero response, Chen Haoyu bawled louder. "Grandmother! Zhu Shuzhen's just out for revenge! Had her maid Hongdou slap my face twice yesterday—still throbs! Just now she punched me right in the cheek, bruised black and blue without even giving me ointment, and canceled my allowance too! Boo-hoo!"
Still dead silence. Realizing Grandmother meant business, he dropped the sobbing and shouted, "Fine! If Grandmother won't back me, I'm going to my mother!"
The maid's voice drifted back through the gate. "Master and Madam are right inside with Old Madam, Second Young Master. They told me to say: go back and take Young Madam's discipline like a man."
Every lifeline snapped. For the first time, Chen Haoyu felt the full chill of Zhu Shuzhen's skill at winning hearts.
"Time for your laps, Young Master," Asheng reminded him. "Skip them and it's chamber-pot duty."
Chen Haoyu threw a punch at Asheng. "A servant bossing me around?!"
Asheng blocked it clean. "Save your breath, Young Master."
Swinging at Sanbao next, he hit empty air as Sanbao ducked clear. "No use, Young Master."
Abandoned by family and outmatched by servants, he vented his frustration by booting toward Qinggua—who glided sideways so fast in panic that he accidentally shoved Chen Haoyu off balance, nearly sending him sprawling.
"Qinggua! Tired of breathing?! Daring to shove me?!"
"Accident! Slipped, Young Master—don't hold it against me!"
Boiling at being pushed by his own servant, Chen Haoyu lunged to pummel him, only for Asheng to step square between them. "Don't waste time, Young Master. Start running."
Qinggua breathed silent thanks as Chen Haoyu—beaten on every front—squatted helplessly on the cobblestones.
Stepping closer in concern, Qinggua asked, "Are you all right, Young Master?"
Smack! Chen Haoyu slapped his cheek.
Clutching his face, Qinggua yelped, "Picking on me again, Young Master!"
The slap let off just enough steam for Chen Haoyu to rise. "Who else can I pick on—them?"
Qinggua swallowed his grievance.
Then a vivid whiff of a steaming chamber pot flashed through Chen Haoyu's mind, making his stomach churn. A smart man picks his battles; better run the laps. "Hey! Chen Manor's massive—where am I supposed to run?"
"I'll set the pace in front, Sanbao brings up the rear, you run right between us," Asheng said.
Refusing to suffer alone, Chen Haoyu pointed at Qinggua. "You! Run with me!"
"Come on, Young Master, I hate running—too tiring!"
"Look at them—not even my servants and they're running with me, while my own man acts like a coward! Get moving or forget my help chasing Hongdou!"
For Hongdou, Qinggua bit the bullet. "If you're running, Young Master, I'm right beside you!"
Down Chen Manor's wide main avenues they jogged: Asheng leading, Sanbao trailing, Chen Haoyu and Qinggua panting between them. Passing manor servants and Xinglong Pavilion clerks gawked and whispered, sending a burn of humiliation up Chen Haoyu's neck, though he barely had breath left to curse them.
Rounding the west road, bold clerks even called out teasingly, "Jogging, Second Young Master? Taking up fitness now?"
Wheezing hard, Chen Haoyu gasped at Asheng, "Can... can we... take a breather?"
Without looking back, Asheng kept striding. "Not even one lap down and you're winded, Young Master? Nine more to go—push through and you'll catch your second wind!"
Then an angry female voice boomed from behind: "Qinggua! Halt right there!"
Loud and furious enough to freeze all four men in their tracks—music to Chen Haoyu's ears as he bent over gasping for air.
Storming down the lane came Chouya. After waiting half the morning in the pavilion, she had realized Qinggua played her for a fool and marched out hunting answers—spotting him jogging right down the main road.
Seeing Chouya's thunderous face cracked Chen Haoyu up. "You're toast, Qinggua!"
"I only seduced her for your sake, Young Master!" Qinggua hissed. "Save me—what do I do?"
"Play smart—sweet-talk her out of it for now!" Chen Haoyu whispered back.
Plastering on a sheepish grin, Qinggua trotted over. "Sorry to stand you up, Chouya! Wasn't skipping the pavilion—Young Madam let Young Master out and ordered me to run laps with him!"
Chouya squinted in suspicion. "Really?"
"Cross my heart! Ask Young Master!"
"You two breathe out of the same nostril—why would I ask him? Sanbao! Did Miss order Qinggua to run with Young Master?"
"Miss only ordered Young Master to run," Sanbao answered straight. "Young Master dragged Qinggua along himself."
Chouya whipped back toward Qinggua, fists balling tight. "Lying to me again!"
Panicked, Qinggua stammered, "Not lying! Servant like me can't say no to Young Master or Young Madam!"
"What about saying you liked me?" Chouya demanded right in front of everybody. "Was that a lie too?"
Chen Haoyu grinned like a spectator at a street opera—far more fun than jogging. Meanwhile Asheng exhaled in secret relief, having worried Qinggua was after Hongdou.
Qinggua weighed his odds in half a second: admit lying and take a bruising right now, or keep lying and get stuck with her forever. One beating beat a lifetime sentence—about to confess, he froze at Chouya's next threat: "If you lied to me, I'll stalk you every single day so no other girl comes near you until you have to marry me! Run to the ends of the earth and I'll follow!"
Even Chen Haoyu winced for him.
"Chouya's sincere, Qinggua—don't play games with her," Sanbao chimed in.
"Relax, Chouya, Qinggua takes feelings seriously," Asheng piled on.
Recomputing on the fly—survive today, wriggle out later—Qinggua forced a bright smile. "Of course I meant every word, Chouya! Head back now—I have to finish running with Young Master, or he fails his task and has to empty Young Madam's chamber pot, which means I'll end up doing the dirty work!"
Glowing because he hadn't denied it, Chouya checked an urge to hug him in front of company. "All right, keep running with Young Master. I'll go back!" Blushing bright pink, she scampered off.
"Show's over, Young Master," Asheng announced. "Back to running."
Reluctant to see the entertainment end, Chen Haoyu swallowed his grumbling and jogged back down Chen Manor's main avenue.
#CH 233
#TITLE: Resistance Gets Him Nowhere
Chen Manor sprawled wide enough that three or four laps wrecked an untrained runner, let alone ten. By the final two laps, Chen Haoyu and Qinggua were reduced to a wheezing shuffle, forcing Asheng and Sanbao to slow to a brisk walk just to stay alongside.
"Young Master," Qinggua gasped, "Young Madam only... made you run... let me catch my breath..."
"No... way!" Chen Haoyu panted. "Too embarrassing... running solo... you suffer with me!"
Qinggua shut up and shuffled on.
Rounding a bend, Chen Haoyu looked up to find Chen Haodong standing right in the middle of the lane wearing a broad smile.
Inside, Chen Haodong was walking on air: Watching you suffer makes my day! Time to pour a little oil on the fire between you and the tigress and sit back for the show!
Spotting his cousin felt like grabbing a lifeline. Finding a fresh burst of wind, Chen Haoyu jogged up and threw an arm around him. "Brother Dong! Been running for hours and not one elder lifts a finger for me—you're the only one who cares!"
Putting on a solemn show of sympathy, Chen Haodong sighed. "The moment I heard, I hurried to Grandmother's, yet her gate was bolted tight, and Eldest Uncle and Aunt's too! Nobody else dares cross Old Madam Xia, so I waited out here to help you brainstorm."
Asheng and Sanbao halted respectfully to wait, while Qinggua collapsed gratefully onto a stone to catch his breath.
Pulling back, Chen Haoyu pleaded, "Give me a plan! How do I dodge the tigress's punishments?"
Instead of answering right away, Chen Haodong pointed at his cheek. "Where'd that bruise come from?"
"The tigress punched me!"
Serve you right—hope she beats you black and blue! Chen Haodong cheered inside, saying aloud, "Hang tight while I put these lackeys in their place."
Striding over to Asheng and Sanbao, he barked, "Blind, insolent servants—daring to humiliate Second Young Master in broad daylight! Who gave you the gall? Tomorrow I'll report to Old Madam Xia and have you sold off to the countryside for good!"
Sanbao bristled to argue, yet Asheng laid a calm hand on his arm and bowed. "Forgive us, Eldest Young Master. Our mistress received full permission from Old Madam Xia, Eldest Master, and Eldest Madam to discipline Second Young Master, and ordered us to supervise."
Feigning wrath, Chen Haodong snapped, "Step aside and stay put while my cousin and I sit in that pavilion! If Sister-in-law has a problem, send her to me!" Taking Haoyu's arm, he steered him toward the pavilion.
"Brother Dong, you're a legend!" Chen Haoyu flashed a thumbs-up.
"You're embarrassing every man in the Chen clan," Chen Haodong chided as they walked. "Sit down and let me teach you how a man keeps his wife in line."
Settling onto stone stools, Chen Haoyu sighed. "Not like I want to lose face, Brother Dong—Zhu Shuzhen is a beast, and her servants are beasts too! Can't outfight or outrun those two, so I had to play along."
Chen Haodong leaned in to fan the flames. "A real man never rolls over—fight back! Tough as Sister-in-law is, she won't dare cripple you or endanger your life. Use your head: why did Grandmother and Aunt step back without lifting a finger? Because they know for a fact she won't actually hurt you! So what are you scared of? Push right back!"
Light flared across Chen Haoyu's face. "Brilliant! How didn't I see that?! No wonder Sister-in-law Anya is so gentle and obedient under you! Forget the last lap—I'm marching straight back to Moxiang Courtyard to go head-to-head with Zhu Shuzhen!"
"That's the spirit!" Chen Haodong clapped his shoulder, grinning silently as his cousin swaggered off: Go stir up a war with your wife so I get front-row seats!
Seeing his master strut back, Qinggua hurried up. "Rested up, Young Master? Just one lap left—I'll finish it with you."
"Who said I'm running?" Chen Haoyu jabbed a finger at Asheng and Sanbao. "Listen up! I'm skipping the final lap and heading straight back to Moxiang Courtyard! Let's see what Zhu Shuzhen does about it!"
Unwilling to rough up their young master, Asheng and Sanbao fell in helplessly behind him. "Young Master," Asheng cautioned, "skipping tasks means emptying the chamber pot."
Chen Haoyu spun around barking, "Empty my foot! Not doing it! Let's see what she dares do!"
Resigned, Asheng trailed along while Qinggua whispered beside his master, "What did Eldest Young Master tell you to put all that fire in your belly?"
"Pure golden wisdom," Chen Haoyu boasted.
Stepping into Moxiang Courtyard, Chen Haoyu saw three maids standing in a neat line—familiar faces from Third Uncle's courtyard whom he had flirted with once. Wondering why they stood here yet not caring, he turned toward the back courtyard—until a maid stepped right across his path.
"Young Master, Miss says once your laps are done, first apologize to these three maids, then memorize the *Analects* and Wang Wei's poems, and after that dust Miss's room."
About to explode, Chen Haoyu remembered she dared not hurt him and relaxed into a smirk. "What's your name? Never seen you around."
"I'm Amei. Helped fight off Master Jiang's thugs for you and went with Miss to Chunxiang Brothel to haul you home—you just never noticed me. Hardly surprising since you've stayed out of the manor ever since the wedding."
"Ah, Amei!" He jabbed a thumb at Asheng behind him. "Your brother's shadow! Think you two siblings can boss me around? Tell Zhu Shuzhen I'm not scared of her—whatever she tells me to do, I'm doing the exact opposite!"
Turning toward Boya Study, he froze at Qianli Xun's voice floating from the porch. "Chen Haoyu! Eager to empty my chamber pot? Didn't peg you for that kind of hobby."
He spun to face her. "I'll say it one last time: I'm not lifting a finger for any of your chores, chamber pot included!"
In a flash Qianli Xun stepped in and pinned his arm behind his back. "Doing them or not?"
Wincing hard, he yelled, "Not doing them! You won't cripple me and you won't kill me—I'm not scared of anything you throw at me!"
Caught off guard that he had called her bluff, Qianli Xun shoved him clear. "Not a copper in your pocket—don't you want to earn silver?"
Rubbing his shoulder, Chen Haoyu sneered. "Locked in here with free food and board, what do I need silver for? Keep it!"
"Then I lock you in Moxiang Courtyard forever!"
Gleeful at reading her hand, he crowed, "Great! I'll become a homebody! Seen everything, eaten everything, played everything—only thing I haven't tried is staying home all day! Perfect chance!"
Faced with shameless immunity, Qianli Xun paused a beat—and found his true Achilles' heel.
"Break today's rules, and I'll have a wooden signboard carved reading 'I Am a Giant Scumbag,' hang it around your neck, truss you up in ropes, and have my servants parade you first through every courtyard in Chen Manor, then up and down every street in Lin'an daily! Your choice: follow the rules, or go on tour."
Chen Haoyu's hair nearly stood on end. "Y-you... how does parading me through town help you?! I'm your husband on paper!"
The madder he got, the wider Qianli Xun beamed. "Keyword: on paper! You lose face, not me—so long as I get payback for last year's slander, I'm thrilled! Hanging around Chunxiang Brothel every night since our wedding, did you spare one thought for me being your wife? Why would I spare one for you?"
Grabbing at his favorite needle, he feigned surprise. "Wait—did you fall for me? Makes sense, stellar catch like me—"
"Which ear heard me say I like you? I only like torturing you!" Cutting him off flat, she barked, "Asheng, go fetch a wooden board!"
"Zhu Shuzhen! You're pushing it!"
Stepping right into his space, she flexed her knuckles. "Got plenty of energy left—want another punch?"
Crushed across every round, Chen Haoyu cursed Brother Dong's useless advice. This woman never bluffed; let her drag him through Lin'an wearing a scumbag sign and his life was over. He threw up both hands. "Fine, fine, put the board away! I'll listen! Spreading those rumors was my fault—consider it paying my debt!"
"Saved us both time if you'd said that first." Qianli Xun nodded to Amei. "Take Chen Haoyu to apologize to the three maids."
"Right this way, Young Master," Amei gestured.
Fuming yet cornered, convinced Zhu Shuzhen was his nemesis—and recalling Master Jiang's lie at the brothel—he shot back over his shoulder, "Your father's a sly old fox, Zhu Shuzhen! Screwed me over good!"
Clueless what he meant, Qianli Xun barked, "Quit stalling and apologize!"
Stopping before the three maids, Chen Haoyu eyed them up and down. "All I did was pinch your hands and cheeks once—and I have to apologize for that? Be honest, how'd the tigress dig you three up?"
"Hongdou told us Second Young Master wanted to apologize to servants he mistreated," one maid answered. "Curiosity got the better of us, so we came to see!"
"Your curiosity is my misery! A master apologizing to maids—where's the decency?"
"Apologize already, Young Master," Amei nudged.
"Shut up! Nobody asked you!" Glancing back at Zhu Shuzhen's icy glare, he bit the bullet and muttered, "Sorry."
Never having heard a master apologize before, the three maids giggled behind their sleeves.
"One won't cut it," Amei reminded him. "Three people—two more to go."
"Sorry. Sorry!" he ground out.
Still giggling, one maid piped up, "Don't get handsy with maids anymore, Second Young Master, or you'll never run out of 'sorrys' to say!" Satisfied, the three trotted off.
Chen Haoyu spun back. "Happy now, Zhu Shuzhen?"
"Just getting warmed up—nowhere near happy yet."
Envious of the three maids, Qinggua stepped right up to Qianli Xun. "Young Madam, can I join the apology line too?"
"Of course!" Qianli Xun smiled. "He bullies you nonstop—he owes you dozens of 'sorrys'!"
Qinggua beamed—and Chen Haoyu lost his cool completely. Lunging forward with a furious kick at his own bold servant, he swung so hard on empty air as Qinggua sidestepped that his legs flew out from under him and he hit the flagstones flat on his backside.
"Still trying to kick me, Young Master?" Qinggua hurried to help him up.
Chen Haoyu snatched Qinggua's collar. "Anyone else can ask for an apology—never you!"
"Young Madam already said yes!"
"You belong to me! You listen to me alone!" he hissed, tightening his grip.
Qinggua nodded reluctantly.
Ignoring the comedy act, Qianli Xun turned and went indoors, Kuqiao signaling Asheng before following.
Shoving Qinggua off, Chen Haoyu clambered up with Qinggua's help as Asheng reminded him, "Back to Boya Study for your memorization task, Young Master."
Brushing off his knees, Chen Haoyu shook a fist at Asheng and Sanbao. "Pair of ghosts haunting my shadow—one day I'll flatten you both!" Still, beaten for now, he had to study—and needed an outlet for his bile. "Qinggua, walk in front of me."
"Why?"
"Less mouth, more walking!"
Unsuspecting, Qinggua stepped ahead. Gathering every ounce of steam in his right leg, Chen Haoyu booted him square in the rear: "Take that, Zhu Shuzhen!"
#CH 234
#TITLE: A Single Tear Reveals the Heart
With no eyes on his backside, Qinggua took the kick square on, pitching forward off balance straight toward a face-plant on the flagstones.
Out of nowhere, Chouya darted in and caught him steady. "Are you all right, Qinggua?"
"Whew—thanks, Chouya!"
"Don't mention it." Letting him go, Chouya turned and curtsied to Chen Haoyu. "Young Master, could you please stop bullying my Qinggua?"
Denied even his lone vent and scolded by a maid in his own yard—every tile altered against his wishes, every servant loyal to Zhu Shuzhen, even Qinggua backed up while he stood utterly alone—a sudden, raw sting of humiliation burned behind Chen Haoyu's eyes. Not once in his entire life had he felt this cornered. Without another word, eyes reddening, he turned and walked back toward Boya Study with Asheng and Sanbao trailing behind.
Chouya blinked after him. "Qinggua, did you see that? Did Young Master just cry?"
"What? Cry? No way! My master only makes other people cry—never shed a tear in his life!"
Amei strolled over. "I saw it too. Looked crushed, eyes red as cherries. Well, married to a force of nature like Miss, anyone would cry."
Ignoring Amei, Chouya turned shyly to Qinggua. "Come meet me at the west pavilion when you're free."
"No shame at all, Chouya, flirting right in front of me!" Amei teased.
"Then make yourself scarce!" Chouya nudged her away. "Go do your chores!" Laughing, Amei strolled off to the West Courtyard.
Worried about his master—and eager to ditch Chouya—Qinggua said quick, "Gotta go check on Young Master!" and jogged off to the back courtyard.
Letting him go since she had work anyway, Chouya muttered at his retreating back, "Young Master boots you nonstop and you still run after him like a pup! Next time I'm leaving you on the ground!"
Along the walk to Boya Study, Chen Haoyu fought back the burn, yet one lone teardrop slipped down his cheek anyway. Halting, he wiped it away with his sleeve—and froze as an old drinking boast rushed back into his head.
One night at Juxiang Tower, drinking and swapping ribald stories with his rich buddies, the talk had turned to real love. Every friend named some girl who had moved his heart—everybody except Chen Haoyu.
"All the flowers you've plucked, Brother Chen, never once caught real feelings?" one friend had asked.
With a girl draped over each arm, Chen Haoyu had smirked. "I just like playing around. Who bothers with real love?"
"Always wondered what kind of woman could actually make you fall, Brother Chen," another pressed.
Waving the girls away when the whole table clamored for an answer, Chen Haoyu had turned strangely serious. "Can't recall how much I cried as a toddler, yet since I've been old enough to remember, I've never shed a tear. If any woman ever makes me cry, that's the woman I've fallen for."
"What if she's hideous?" someone joked.
"My standards for looks are sky-high—no plain face ever catches my eye, let alone makes me cry!"
Memory fading, Chen Haoyu stared blankly ahead: Wait... did I just fall for the tigress?!
He shook his head hard. Impossible! We're sworn enemies! Then why did a tear just fall? No—overthinking it! Old drunken talk doesn't count!
Shaking off the eerie thought, he stepped into Boya Study while Asheng and Sanbao took posts outside.
Inside, Hongdou was tidying the desk. Turning to curtsy, she gestured to a tall stack of bound volumes. "Desk's all set, Young Master. Miss handpicked these for you—start with the *Analects* and Wang Wei's poetry. Five chapters of the *Analects* and five poems daily, tested by Miss in Suxin Thatched Hall once you finish."
One look at the tower of books gave Chen Haoyu an instant migraine, firing his curses back up: Rotten tigress! As if I could ever fall for you!
"Better start memorizing, Young Master," Hongdou urged.
Patting his growling belly, he countered, "Starving after running all morning! Bring lunch first, then I'll memorize."
"Start reading while I fetch your meal."
After Hongdou left, Qinggua crept in, blinking at his master seated before a mountain of classics. Grinning, he sidled up. "Chouya and Amei said you cried, Young Master—true?"
"Beat it! Quit bugging me! None of your business whether I cried!"
"Aha, so it's true! I know you're hurting, Young Master, yet with the elders staying out and Young Madam unstoppable, hard times are here—unless you hear out an idea of mine?"
Staring gloomily at the stack of books, Chen Haoyu muttered, "What bright idea could you have?"
Qinggua leaned over the desk. "Going head-to-head flopped completely. Why not try going soft?"
Chen Haoyu's eyes snapped up bright and alert. "Got a brain in that skull after all—keep talking!"
Thrilled not to be cursed at, Qinggua warmed up. "You and Young Madam are husband and wife! Treat her sweet—tough as she is, she's still a woman, and women melt for soft words and warm care! Pour on concern every day until her heart softens; how can she swing fists at a devoted husband? Turn yourself into a model husband, let her see your good side, and she'll want a real marriage instead of torturing you!"
Turning it over, Chen Haoyu mused, "Soft words I can do—never tried being a good husband though. How do you even do that?"
"Eldest Master is a model husband! Copy how he treats Eldest Madam!"
"Right! My own father's a living textbook!" Chen Haoyu slapped the desk in excitement—then sagged seeing the books staring back. "Still stuck memorizing these?"
"Of course!" Qinggua flipped the top volume open in front of him. "Think about it: has Eldest Master ever crossed Eldest Madam once?"
"Never—though Mother never tortured Father either! The tigress is flat-out bullying me, and I hate reading!"
"Cool down, Young Master." Qinggua gently rubbed his master's back. "Ask yourself why she's mad at you—heard every servant in the manor talking about you spreading those slanderous rumors about her last year! Imagine a young lady cursed by an entire city, crying her eyes out in her room, watching her match with Qiao Manor blow up! Hard to believe you pulled something that dirty."
"Not all on me—she wrote 'falling asleep in his arms, fully dressed' herself!"
Qinggua paused. "Sure, yet ordinary folks don't read scholars' poetry! Nobody on the street would've heard that line if you hadn't woven it into your gossip!"
That struck home like a cold splash: commoners never paid attention to highbrow verses; he really had lit the match that burned her name down. "All right, all right—my bad. I'll memorize."
He opened the *Analects*, muttering at ancient sages for writing so many tedious words just to torment future generations.
When Hongdou brought lunch, Chen Haoyu ate his fill, grew heavy-lidded, bobbed over his book, and soon slumped face-down on the desk snoring. Sighing, Qinggua stood beside him nodding off on his own feet—until a poke jolted him awake to find Chouya standing there.
"Come on, west pavilion," she whispered.
Not liking Chouya and worried Hongdou might get the wrong idea, Qinggua grabbed a quick scheme and drew her clear of his snoring master. "Chouya, stop coming to look for me."
"Why?!"
"While Young Master's miserable under Young Madam, I have zero mood for romance."
"He's him and you're you!"
"Nope—when he's happy I'm happy; when he hurts, I hurt."
"So you won't meet me at the pavilion until Miss and Young Master make up?"
Qinggua nodded.
"That could take forever!"
"Your choice: stop coming around, or help Young Master and Young Madam patch things up!"
"Patching them up is impossible... guess I'll stay away then." Crestfallen, Chouya slipped out.
Thrilled at shaking her off so clean, Qinggua's grin faded a second later: wait—if even Chouya thinks they'll never make up, is my soft-approach plan doomed?
Just then a mumble rose from the desk: "Zhu Shuzhen... wait till I beat you... flowers falling, water flowing..." Talking in his sleep.
Moments later, another quiet murmur drifted out: "I... like... you..."
Qinggua's ears perked up. Leaning close, he whispered, "Who do you like, Young Master?"
Through sluggish sleep, Chen Haoyu slurred three syllables: "Zhu... Shu... zhen..."
Blurry as it sounded, Qinggua caught every syllable and broke into a wide grin. "Young Master fell for Young Madam without even knowing it while awake! Hilarious! Hope they really make up so life gets normal again!"
Right then, dreaming of Zhu Shuzhen punching him into a ditch, Chen Haoyu jerked awake rubbing his eyes. "What hour is it?"
"Hour of the Monkey—mid-afternoon."
"Already?! I didn't memorize a single line! What do I do?"
"Only thing to do—cram right now!"
Before he could grab the book, Hongdou stepped in. "Study time's up, Young Master. Miss is waiting to test you."
"Give me a few more minutes?"
"No! Miss's schedule doesn't bend!"
"Fine, no need to yell!"
Dragging his feet, Chen Haoyu headed out with Qinggua trailing in dread, bracing for an eruption.
#CH 235
#TITLE: Soft Touch, Soft Touch, Use the Soft Touch
Stepping into Suxin Thatched Hall, Chen Haoyu breathed in the crisp scent of ink and paper—and for the first time in his life found it pleasant.
Zhu Shuzhen sat sipping tea at her desk. Without a scowl on her face, she really was striking—no mystery why Prince Yi lost his head over her, when even Chen Haoyu felt his own pulse skip. Shaking his head, he scolded himself: Snap out of it!
"Young Master is here, Miss," Hongdou announced.
Setting down her bowl, Qianli Xun dismissed her. "Got it. Go tend your chores."
Unable to peel his eyes off her, Chen Haoyu mused silently: If the tigress softened up a bit, she'd be a catch—gifted with a brush, lethal with her fists. Best of all, if anyone messed with me outside, I'd have a wife who could flatten them!
"What are you gawking at, Chen Haoyu?"
Her sharp bark snapped him back to earth. "Came to report to you—who else am I supposed to look at, Kuqiao or Qinggua?"
"Skip the banter. What did you memorize?"
"Too short on time—didn't get any memorized."
"None?! I gave you over an hour and you didn't learn one line? Not a single poem?!"
Chen Haoyu nodded sheepishly.
"Kuqiao! Fetch the bamboo ruler!"
Kuqiao handed over the disciplinary ruler. Rising, Qianli Xun stepped in front of him. "Hold out your palm!"
Shocking even himself, Chen Haoyu held his hand straight out like a chastised schoolboy.
His docile obedience caught her flat-footed. "Since when do you turn meek?"
Launching straight into his soft approach, Chen Haoyu smiled. "No more fighting you. Whatever you order, I'll obey."
Instant buzzkill. Qianli Xun tossed the ruler back to Kuqiao and returned to her desk. "Not wasting energy smacking your hand—need it intact to clean my room."
Score! The soft touch worked like magic! Behind his back, Chen Haoyu flashed a hidden thumbs-up to Qinggua, making the servant beam with pride at his first-ever praise.
"Wipe down every speck of dust inside and out of this hall," Qianli Xun ordered. "Qinggua doesn't lift a finger—and stay at least three feet clear of my desk."
"Dusting's a cinch—I'll do every bit myself! Letting Qinggua rinse the rag is fine, right?"
Unnerved by this sudden personality swap, Qianli Xun snapped, "Less mouth, more dusting!" and went back to writing her huaben, ignoring him.
Rolling up his sleeves with Qinggua's help, Chen Haoyu took a damp cloth and started wiping shelves, sneaking glances toward her desk. Lost in writing, she looked nothing like a tigress.
He remembered spotting her on West Lake last year nestled gentle as silk in Qiao Wenxian's arms. Back then he couldn't fathom why a fierce woman melted for a scholar with zero strength; now it clicked—she loved him. Now she was Mrs. Chen; if she ever fell for Chen Haoyu, she'd turn soft as a kitten too!
Then gloom hit: how could he compete with Qiao Wenxian's literary polish and pretty face, or Prince Yi's royal rank and martial prowess? He had nothing!
Glancing at plain-faced Qinggua—built for Chouya yet chasing Hongdou anyway—sparked an idea. He beckoned Qinggua close and murmured, "Quick question: if everybody told you you're way out of Hongdou's league, would you still chase her?"
"Course! Who cares what outsiders think? I like her—that's all that matters!"
Chen Haoyu flashed a second thumbs-up, sending Qinggua soaring. Right—screw overthinking! For all their pedigree, she still ended up married to me! Proof Heaven picked me for her—dive in and win her over!
Fired up, he scrubbed every beam, shelf, and chair for a full hour until the room shone. Kuqiao swiped a testing finger across a sill and nodded impressed. "Aside from haunting brothels, you dust just as clean as a maid, Young Master!"
"Watch that tongue—your young master is done with brothels for good! I'm a married man now; hanging out in brothels invites gossip and hurts my wife's feelings."
Glancing toward Zhu Shuzhen—who ignored the speech completely—he watched her lay down her brush. "Qinggua, fetch Asheng."
While Kuqiao stowed the manuscript and Qinggua went for Asheng, Qianli Xun flipped open a book and read absorbed, leaving Chen Haoyu staring in wonder. What's so gripping in those pages? If he wanted her respect, he needed some book learning—starting tomorrow, he'd actually study!
Asheng stepped in and bowed. "Your orders, Miss?"
"Report on Chen Haoyu's running."
"Eight and a half laps total, Miss. Stopped twice—once to watch a commotion, once to chat with Eldest Young Master in a pavilion."
Qianli Xun's face turned stern. "Two tasks passed out of four. Starting tomorrow, Chen Haoyu, you empty my chamber pot two days straight. Clear?"
Hot anger flared in his chest—and he hammered it down with an inner chant: Soft touch, soft touch, use the soft touch! Soft touch, soft touch, use the soft touch!
Miracle—the rage melted. Breaking into a sunny smile, he said, "Whatever Wife says goes! I listen to Wife!"
Qianli Xun froze, goosebumps rising. "Don't call me that!"
"Teach me what to call you then, Wife!"
"Still saying it?!"
"Shuzhen."
"Swallow the wrong pill? Put the surname back on!"
"Zhu Shuzhen."
Same three syllables, yet delivered in a warm tone totally unlike before. Was he faking some fresh scheme? One day of punishment couldn't rewrite a man overnight! Hard resistance she could smash; this slippery sweetness threw her off balance. Do not fall for it!
"Kuqiao, bring his pay."
Kuqiao placed the coins into his palm. "One tael of silver and fifty wen, Young Master. Miss honors her word, and expects you to take your punishment tomorrow."
Deciding to double down on the soft touch—already feeling strange new warmth toward her anyway—Chen Haoyu pressed the money straight back into Kuqiao's hand and faced Qianli Xun. "Since this is punishment, how can I pocket wages? Keep every copper—I accept every penalty willingly."
Both maid and mistress stared stunned.
"Miss, what do I do with it?" Kuqiao stammered.
Refusing to bend to his act, Qianli Xun countered, "Take it to the West Courtyard and split it among the servants—treat it as silver Chen Haoyu earned for them!"
Catching the bait meant to make him blow up, Chen Haoyu chanted silently twice more—Soft touch, soft touch!—and beamed wide. "Listen to Young Madam! Split it up, and I'll earn even more tomorrow so everyone eats well! Don't forget a share for my boy Qinggua!"
"Thank you, Young Master!" Qinggua cheered.
Thoroughly thrown off stride, Qianli Xun hurried onto tomorrow's slate. "Besides emptying my chamber pot, complete your full ten laps and memorization. Tomorrow's chore: sweep the entire main courtyard, inspected by Asheng—fail and earn another chamber-pot day. As for your apology..." Her gaze slid to Qinggua. "Apologize to Qinggua in front of every servant in Moxiang Courtyard."
Panicked his master would snap, Qinggua rushed in, "No need, Young Madam! Bullying other servants was wrong, bullying me is fine!"
One silent chant later, Chen Haoyu smiled easy. "Don't talk like that, Qinggua. Tomorrow I'll apologize proper."
Keeping her mask icy, Qianli Xun's eyes caught the angry welt still darkening his cheek. "Kuqiao, bring a jar of bruise salve."
Kuqiao fetched ointment from the inner room and handed it over. "Apply once tonight, Young Master, and the bruise clears by morning."
Thrilled—her first sign of care!—Chen Haoyu clutched the jar like treasure while Qianli Xun waved them off gruffly. "Get back to Boya Study and quit clogging my air!"
Once they left, Kuqiao ventured, "Miss, doesn't Young Master seem like a whole new man?"
"Illusion! Pure illusion!" Qianli Xun scoffed. "Nobody reforms in two days. Just some new trick—watch him show his true colors in a couple days! Besides, my revenge isn't done—don't want him reforming yet!"
"He's your husband, Miss. Him turning good only helps you."
"You don't want me getting payback?"
"If anyone else spread those lies, I'd slap them senseless myself! Yet he's your husband, Miss—I don't want you going through another divorce! If he shapes up and treats you right, isn't that the best of both worlds?"
"Even if he turns into a saint, it's nothing to me." Keeping polite peace was her max; falling for him was out of the question.
After dark in Boya Study, Qinggua dabbed salve across his master's bruised cheek, beaming. "Soft words worked wonders, Young Master! Young Madam couldn't even stay mad at you!"
"Smart plan, kid—once my life's back to normal, I'll reward you big."
"Skip rewards—just keep your promise and help me win Hongdou!"
"Hongdou's clearly not into you—why not switch? Chouya's practically throwing herself at you!"
Setting the jar down, Qinggua stood firm. "Don't want easy—I want Hongdou."
"Fine, fine, once I win Zhu Shuzhen over, I'll play matchmaker for you and Hongdou."
Left alone with his thoughts, Chen Haoyu realized today had flipped his entire world upside down. He would never forget the first woman who ever made him shed a tear—an offhand tavern vow suddenly come true. Falling for a woman felt dizzy and sweet; every sharp quirk of his tigress suddenly looked adorable. A goofy grin stretched across his face, spilling into a silly chuckle. "Hehe..."
Staring at him, Qinggua shook his head: Emptying a chamber pot tomorrow morning, and he's sitting here giggling like a fool!
#CH 236
#TITLE: Roses to Speak the Heart
Only when Asheng pulled Boya Study's door shut and snapped the padlock did Chen Haoyu snap out of his dreamy grin. "Locking me in again? Scared I'll sneak off to Chunxiang Brothel—just admit you like me already! Relax, even if you chased me out to the brothel with a broom, I wouldn't go!"
Qinggua pressed the back of his hand to his master's forehead. "No fever... why are you talking crazy?"
Chen Haoyu swatted his hand away. "Quit poking me! Why would I have a fever?"
"You think Young Madam locks your door because she likes you and worries you'll visit brothels?"
"What else?!"
"Does the soft-touch plan cause brain damage? Otherwise why the gibberish?"
"Not gibberish—God's honest truth!"
"You're seriously done with Chunxiang Brothel?"
Propping his chin on his palm with a dreamy smile, Chen Haoyu sighed. "What's fun about Chunxiang Brothel? Sick of it! All I want now is staying home right here in Moxiang Courtyard."
Certain now, Qinggua beamed. "Awesome! Good days ahead for us!"
"Flipped fast, didn't you? Calling me crazy one second, grinning the next!"
Bracing to dodge just in case, Qinggua ventured, "Young Master... did you fall for Young Madam?"
Far from snapping, Chen Haoyu blinked. "That obvious? Even you spotted it? Me falling for the tigress—tell anyone and they'll call me insane! Feels like a dream even to me."
"Sure feels unreal!" Pouring tea for his master, Qinggua marvelled, "Cursing her all morning, smitten by night—isn't that a bit fast?"
"Ever heard the phrase 'shifting in a heartbeat'?"
Remembering the naptime mumble, Qinggua asked, "Think about it, Young Master—maybe you liked her ages ago without realizing it?"
Replaying the past made everything click: ever since she beat him at Yongtai Teahouse and crossed paths in the bamboo grove, he had obsessed day and night over getting back at her, her name looping nonstop inside his head—more thought than he had ever spent on any other woman. Had she snuck into his heart behind his grudge, until today's tear tore off the blindfold? Had to be!
"Didn't realize you could read me so clear!"
Beaming at yet another compliment, Qinggua confessed, "You muttered Young Madam's name in your sleep this afternoon saying you liked her—heard every word."
Chen Haoyu glowed brighter—falling in love for the first time in his life, and by sheer crazy luck already married to her! "Starting tomorrow, Qinggua, I'm turning over a new leaf to blow Zhu Shuzhen's socks off!"
"No need to wait till tomorrow—your soft talk today already had her reeling!"
"Hehehe! Found her kryptonite at last! Keep laying on the soft touch until she melts into a sweet little bride curling right into my arms—no more punching me, maybe even using those fists to guard me! Hehehe!" Picturing it nearly had him drooling.
At dawn, Asheng and Sanbao unlocked the door and roused him. Normally waking this early would have set him cursing; energized by love, he hopped straight up, dressed with Qinggua's help, and both tied strips of cloth across their noses and mouths.
Outside Suxin Thatched Hall, Asheng tapped the door. Kuqiao cracked it open, set a chamber pot on the threshold without a word, and slammed it shut.
A pungent stench slammed out like a wall. Asheng and Sanbao pinched their noses and turned away; even through cloth masks, Chen Haoyu and Qinggua gagged.
Whoa, stinky business doesn't match that pretty face at all—never mind, love overlooks small flaws! Chen Haoyu mused, barking, "Quit freezing, Qinggua! Grab the pot and haul it to the latrine!"
Unable to refuse, Qinggua held his breath, grabbed the handle, and bolted toward the latrine while Asheng and Sanbao backed off gasping until the air cleared.
Inside, Kuqiao waved a lit censer around the hall grumbling, "Reeks to high heaven! Are we really doing this again tomorrow?!"
Already dressed and staring blankly at her tea set, Qianli Xun answered, "One more day."
"Pray Young Master nails every chore today, or I'll be sneaking out at dawn every morning scooping stinky slop into that pot until Suxin Thatched Hall turns into Stinky-Slop Hall!"
Truth be told, Qianli Xun's own stomach still rolled from the fumes. Watching her mistress sit stone-silent since waking, Kuqiao grew concerned and stepped up with the censer. "You look down, Miss—feeling sick?"
"Kuqiao," Qianli Xun asked quietly, "am I right or wrong taking revenge on Chen Haoyu?"
"Right, of course! He spread those rumors—he deserves punishment!"
"Then why don't I feel happy one bit?"
"Because you have a soft heart, Miss, and hate watching anyone suffer."
Probing her own chest, she felt it ran deeper than mere softness. "Making him empty a chamber pot stank us out right along with him. Hurting someone bounces straight back onto yourself. Wonder if it's even worth it?"
Half grasping her drift, Kuqiao asked, "Thinking of calling off the payback?"
"I want payback—of course I do!" Qianli Xun paused. "Just wondering if it's worth it when it brings me zero joy."
Not knowing how to reply, Kuqiao waited until the incense masked the odor and the men departed, then threw open the doors and windows for fresh air.
After breakfast, Chen Haoyu hit the main lane for his ten laps—cheerful and willing today, surprised to find running actually felt brisk and energizing once he stopped fighting it.
Halfway through, he spotted Chen Haodong ahead and waved without breaking stride. "Gotta finish my laps, Brother Dong—no time to chat or it's chamber-pot duty again! Catch you later!"
Staring after his beaming cousin, Chen Haodong scowled: Why's he grinning while being punished? Yesterday's advice bombed? Irritating—need a nastier way to make the tigress wreck him!
Passing the gardeners' quarters, Chen Haoyu spotted a patch of blooming roses—never noticed them before, yet inspiration struck today. "Qinggua, go ask the gardener to cut me a bouquet of roses!"
Thrilled to dodge five more laps, Qinggua peeled off at once. The gardener happily snipped a lush bunch and tucked them into a plain ceramic vase of water to guard against thorns. Counting laps in his head, Qinggua figured he had time to kill, ducked into a shaded corridor, set the vase on a bench, and leaned against a pillar for a nap—until a splat of bird droppings hit his cheek! Wiping his face and hands clean with torn leaves, he checked the sun, grabbed the vase, and hurried back to the main road just as Chen Haoyu finished his tenth lap and dropped gasping onto a roadside boulder.
"Solid run today, Young Master," Asheng commended.
Spotting Qinggua trotting up with the roses brought a grin to Chen Haoyu's face. "Gorgeous blooms—vase is cheap though. Swap it into the celadon vase in Boya Study once we get back."
After catching his breath and switching the blooms into fine green porcelain, Chen Haoyu carried the vase himself to Suxin Thatched Hall, waving Asheng and Sanbao back at the door. "Scared I'll bolt? Wait outside—Qinggua's enough."
Stepping inside, he found Kuqiao engrossed in whisking tea and Qianli Xun bent absorbed over her writing, neither noticing him. Hugging his vase of roses, he stood gazing fondly at her, wondering why on earth he had ever picked fights with her.
Turning with the fresh tea bowl, Kuqiao jumped at the sight of Young Master hugging roses and grinning goofy at her mistress. "When did you walk in, Young Master?"
"A moment ago. Saw your mistress busy, so I waited."
Setting down her brush, Qianli Xun looked up. "What do you want?"
"Brought you flowers."
Eyes widening at the vase of roses, Qianli Xun froze speechless while Chen Haoyu nodded to Qinggua. "Set them on Young Madam's desk."
Snapping awake as Qinggua approached, Qianli Xun flipped her huaben manuscript face down just before the vase landed beside it, while Kuqiao stood gaping with the tea bowl still frozen in midair.
Plenty of guys had sent Qianli Xun flowers in her modern life—hardly enough to turn her head—yet seeing Chen Haoyu pull this baffled her completely. "What game are you playing, Chen Haoyu?"
He broke into a goofy grin. "Hehehe..."
"Quit grinning like an idiot and speak!"
Reining in his laugh, he said, "This obvious and you still can't tell?"
"Tell what?! Spit it out or get out!"
"Even mad you look gorgeous! Since you want it blunt, I'll swallow my pride and say it right in front of the servants: Zhu Shuzhen, I like you! Hehehe!"
Kuqiao's eyes popped wide, tea bowl still hovering in her hands.
Certain it was a scam, Qianli Xun scoffed. "Think a stunt like this makes me drop my payback? Dream on!"
Unbothered—no inner chant even needed anymore—he grinned easy. "Makes sense you doubt me after all our battles. Give it time and you'll see I mean it! Hehe, who'd have guessed I'd end up married to the woman I love—beats Qiao Wenxian and Prince Yi by miles! Hehehe!"
His goofy beaming gave Qianli Xun chills: Wait—is he actually serious? True or fake, never buy it! "Stop laughing!" she barked.
He zipped his lips instantly.
Kuqiao finally set the tea bowl down. Qianli Xun downed it in one gulp to hide her flustered nerves, then pointed sharp. "Per yesterday's schedule, apologize to Qinggua right now in front of me!"
Expecting him to bristle now that the moment arrived, she blinked as he agreed without hesitation.
"Done!" Turning to Qinggua, Chen Haoyu spoke earnestly. "Qinggua, I've bullied you nonstop and I was wrong. In front of Young Madam today, I'm sorry—never laying a finger on you again. Just know that rough as I was, in my heart you've always been my one true right-hand man."
"One true right-hand man" burst Qinggua's tear ducts wide open. "I didn't know I meant so much to you, Young Master! Kick me all you want—I'll gladly take it! Boo-hoo!"
"Talk about a glutton for punishment! My word stands—never throwing another punch or kick at you." Turning back to Qianli Xun, he asked, "Apology delivered. Satisfied?"
Reminding herself not to fall for this bewildering new act, she answered stiffly, "Fine. Task two passed."
"Nailed task one too—even Asheng praised me! From now on, whatever you say goes without a peep of complaint! Oh—that chamber pot this morning sure reeked, hehehe, yet I don't even mind! Hehehe!"
Unable to listen to one more chuckle, Qianli Xun waved him out. "Done here? Scram and quit annoying me!"
"Right away! Off to memorize my books! Hehehe!" Chen Haoyu bounced out on air.
#CH 237
#TITLE: The Servants Hold a Strategy Meeting
Watching his retreating back and glancing at the roses on the desk, Kuqiao murmured, "Miss... do you think Young Master really fell for you?"
Qianli Xun glared at the blooms. "Don't buy one word of it! Pure scheme—too bad it won't work on me! Think a handful of flowers and a creepy grin buys him a free pass? Not a chance!"
"Still, he looked like he was actually enjoying himself, not suffering at all..."
Qianli Xun tapped her chin. "Means I'm going too easy on him. Need to cook up something tougher."
Half agreeing, half doubtful, Kuqiao held her tongue as Qianli Xun snapped, "Toss those roses out."
Hugging the celadon vase, Kuqiao said, "Too pretty to toss! If you don't want them, I'll put them in Yuening Room."
Setting the roses in her room, Kuqiao stepped back outside—and Chouya rushed up grabbing her sleeve. "Sister Kuqiao, let's talk over something!"
"What?"
"Huge business!" Chouya whispered.
"Spill it or I'm leaving."
Leaning to her ear, Chouya breathed, "About Miss and Young Master's future happiness!"
Any talk of her mistress's happiness hooked Kuqiao immediately. "Where?"
"West Courtyard."
"Hang on—let me tell Miss first so she doesn't wonder where I ran off to."
"Meet you there!" Chouya dashed away.
Inside, seeing her mistress already buried in her huaben again, Kuqiao excused herself briefly and hurried to the West Courtyard. Headed toward Qin'an Lodge, she spotted Chouya waving from the doorway of the servants' dining hall.
"Thought it was just us two," Kuqiao laughed walking over. "Clearly not!"
"Everyone's coming except the three men Matron Wei took on errands."
"Funny—since when are you rallying the troops for Miss's happiness?"
Chouya blushed bright red. "You know why, Sister Kuqiao! Miss's happiness decides mine—Qinggua swore he won't be with me until Young Master and Miss make up!"
"Thought you were pure selfless—turns out you've got skin in the game!" Kuqiao teased, drawing a shy grin from Chouya.
Stepping across the threshold into the hall with its three dining tables, Kuqiao found two male servants already seated who jumped up chorusing, "Greetings, Sister Kuqiao!"
"Jiaxiang and Fulu—early birds! Where's the rest?"
"Sister Hongdou went rounding them up—right behind us!" Chouya answered just as Hongdou, Amei, Qinggua, and Asheng walked in.
Without shyness, Chouya pulled Qinggua onto the bench right beside her, leaving Qinggua watching helplessly as Hongdou settled next to Asheng.
Scanning the table, Kuqiao frowned. "Asheng and Qinggua both here—who's watching Young Master?"
"Sanbao alone is plenty," Asheng replied.
"Relax, Sister Kuqiao," Qinggua piped up. "Young Master's meek as a lamb now—Young Madam points east, he doesn't glance west! Right now he's buried deep in his books. Never once seen him study like this!"
"Wonderful!" Chouya cheered. "One little push and Miss and Young Master will bury the hatchet—then Qinggua and I can..." She trailed off blushing.
Qinggua finally realized his own excuse had sparked this whole summit—torn between rooting for his master and dreading falling into Chouya's clutches, especially seeing Hongdou smiling happily beside Asheng without giving Chouya a second glance.
"Every meeting needs a chair," Kuqiao noted. "Who leads?"
"Chouya called it—she chairs," Hongdou proposed.
Straightening up off Qinggua's shoulder—letting him breathe again—Chouya took charge. "Aside from Qinggua, we all belong to Miss. She treats us well, so we want her happy. Finding out Young Master spread those rumors, she started getting payback—ought to feel great, yet she looks gloomier than before. Sister Kuqiao knows best."
Kuqiao sighed. "Married to the guy who smeared your name—who could smile? Crucial question is helping Miss get past this hurdle. None of us want her going through a second divorce—in-laws as kind as Eldest Master and Eldest Madam are rare as gold!"
"Exact right! No second divorce!" everyone echoed.
Qinggua grinned. "Actually, Young Master and Young Madam already took a giant leap forward! Sister Kuqiao heard him confess his love to her today!"
Gasps erupted all around. "What?! Young Master confessed?!"
"True enough," Kuqiao admitted, "except Miss thinks he's faking and wants to turn up the heat. Honestly, even I can barely believe it."
"Same here!" the table chorused.
"It's for real!" Qinggua insisted. "He even confessed to her in his sleep!"
"If Qinggua says so, I believe it!" Chouya backed him up.
"I suggested he drop fighting her, use soft words, and copy Eldest Master's devotion to Eldest Madam—old Young Master would've kicked me across the room, yet he actually listened! If that isn't love, what is?"
"Guess he really did fall for her," Kuqiao conceded, thinking of the hidden weight in her mistress's heart. "Problem is, Miss doesn't feel the same right now."
Asheng cut straight to the core. "Young Master's already turned around; Miss is the key. If she falls for him too, smooth sailing for everyone!"
"Quiet as a mouse, then hits the bullseye!" Hongdou praised, trading a warm smile with him that made Qinggua sour: Couldn't have hit the bullseye without my setup!
Ignoring him, Hongdou snapped her fingers. "For Miss's happiness, let's help Young Master out! Starting today, keep an eye out for his good points and slip praise for him into Miss's ears every chance we get—especially you, Sister Kuqiao! Hear enough good things and her view of him will soften!"
"Will that even work?" Amei doubted. "Miss despises whoever spread those rumors—hard to let that go."
"No better plan on the table," Kuqiao sighed. "Worth a shot!"
"I'm in!" Chouya voted first, followed by unanimous nods.
Chouya summed up briskly: "Great meeting! Remember the three magic words: Praise Young Master! Qinggua, tell Young Master to step up his game so we actually have good points to praise!"
Locking eyes on Hongdou, Qinggua nodded. "Count on it."
Walking back to Boya Study with Asheng, Qinggua couldn't hold it in. "Asheng... do you like Hongdou?"
Hit square in the heart, Asheng owned up straight. "Y-yes, I do."
Bluffing bold, Qinggua muttered, "Hate to break it to you—Hongdou likes me."
Asheng blinked hard. "Wait—isn't Chouya the one who likes you? Practically glued to your arm back there!"
"Chouya liking me doesn't bar other girls from liking me! I'm still single!" Before his fib could unravel, Qinggua bolted inside Boya Study.
Left brooding outside the door, Asheng frowned: How could Hongdou like Qinggua?
Noticing his glum face, Sanbao asked, "What's eating you, Asheng?"
"Sanbao... think Hongdou likes Qinggua?"
"No way!" Sanbao scoffed instantly. "Chouya likes Qinggua—Hongdou likes me!"
Asheng's jaw dropped—stumbled straight onto a real rival! Better confess to Hongdou fast before anyone beats him to it!
Inside, Chen Haoyu sat memorizing with unprecedented focus. Pouring tea, Qinggua stood listening to classical Chinese sail clean over his head until his master slammed the book shut.
"Disappeared for ages—sneaking off with Chouya?"
"As if! If I sneak off, it's with Hongdou! Anyway, huge news, Young Master: every servant in Moxiang Courtyard just agreed to start singing your praises to Young Madam! Better bring out your A-game so they have ammunition!"
Chen Haoyu blinked. "Wait—aren't they all Zhu Shuzhen's die-hards? Why help me?"
"Because they love her and don't want her going through another divorce! Once I told them you fell for her, they want her to fall for you too!"
"Aha!" Chen Haoyu jumped up. "Come on—time to showcase my top talent right in front of Zhu Shuzhen!"
"Which talent?"
"Photographic memory!"
Stepping out of Boya Study, he waved at the two guards. "Quit shadowing me—I'm not running anywhere."
Still following out of duty—and hunting for praiseworthy traits—Asheng eyed him up and down and whispered to Sanbao, "Spot any good points on Young Master besides decent looks?"
Sanbao shook his head blankly. "Not a one."
"Does being handsome count?"
"Guess so?"
Waiting outside Suxin Thatched Hall, the guards watched Chen Haoyu stride inside—sinking for a second seeing the desk empty, then lighting up at spotting her seated at the tea table holding the bare-bottomed clay doll with a soft smile on her lips.
Stunned by how radiant she looked smiling, he thought: Gorgeous wife like this at home—why on earth did I ever roam outside?! Yesterday I cursed Zhu Xiyan; today I want to kiss his boots for handing me the Poetess of Jiangnan!
"Young Master is here," Kuqiao announced, alerting her mistress.
Ignoring Kuqiao, eyes glued to Shuzhen, he grinned. "Love clay dolls? Once I'm out I'll buy you hundreds—fill the whole hall!"
Handing the doll to Kuqiao to stow away, Qianli Xun rose and met him coldly. "Penniless as you are, buying them with what? Know your own wallet before bragging."
"Once you cool off and let me out, I'll ask Father to put me to work in the family business! Earn monthly wages and buy you all the dolls you want!"
Volunteering for family business after two days?! Shocked yet refusing to drop her guard, she fired back, "I've got more money than you—need you buying me toys? Besides, one doll is charming; filling the hall turns my home into a toy stall!"
"Mad again? My bad, all right? Cool down and sit—time to test my memorization!"
With zero temper left in him to push against, every sharp comeback jammed in her throat. Fine—let's hear him recite and shred him on the mistakes!
#CH 238
#TITLE: Everyone Sings the Groom's Praises
Chen Haoyu started reciting the opening "Xue Er" chapter of the *Analects*. Qianli Xun expected ten short passages at best—instead he rattled off the entire chapter from start to finish. Unfamiliar with the text herself, she had to flip the pages line by line to check him: smooth, crisp, not a single character missed. Genuine respect flickered inside her.
Finishing, he puffed out his chest. "Well? Impressive, huh? Exceeded quota!"
Qianli Xun set the *Analects* down coolly. "Nothing to brag about. Probably hammered it into your skull at private school as a kid."
"Went to school, sure, never opened a page! Knew the title *Analects*, didn't know a single line inside—ask Brother Dong if you don't believe me!" Reaching for a chair—figuring stellar work earned him a seat—he froze an inch above the cushion at her bark:
"Recitation isn't over—stand straight!"
He snapped upright. "Have to bark so fierce at your own husband? A softer tone wouldn't kill you."
Rising, Qianli Xun paced two slow circles around him, sizing him up: Guy actually has real brains—put them to decent use and he might amount to something.
Standing so close, her faint fragrance drifted over him, making him lightheaded; being studied like this sent hope surging. "So? Decent catch, right? Hit the jackpot marrying me."
Where did this bottomless ego come from? Nobody else in the Chen family had it! "Less bragging, more poetry!"
"Warning upfront—never liked poetry, so I only memorized the exact quota."
Saying nothing, she listened as he reeled off ten Wang Wei poems—"Autumn Dusk in the Mountains," "Countryside at Weichuan," and eight more—flawlessly.
Figuring the material was just too easy, Qianli Xun scoffed, "Clearly too simple. Starting tomorrow, memorize the *Complete Library of the Four Treasuries*!"
Chen Haoyu blinked. "*Complete Library of the Four Treasuries*? Never heard of it."
Oops! Transmigrator brain slip—that mega-encyclopedia belonged to the Qing dynasty centuries ahead! Never admitting a slip, she bluffed smooth: "A slacker like you barely knows three book titles—no shock you've never heard of it! Fine, I'll take pity on you: memorize the *Records of the Grand Historian* instead!"
Han dynasty—safe for Song times, and massive enough to bury him!
Sure enough, Chen Haoyu winced. "You already stacked a tower of books on my desk—why switch to something that monstrous? Out to kill me?"
His pain brought back her grin. "My authority, my rules—changing my mind is my right!"
"Fine, whatever you say—on one condition!"
Conditions?! Qianli Xun's eyes widened in fury. "What nerve gives you the right to set conditions?! When your lies had the entire city cursing me with nobody to turn to, who let me set conditions?! Hand you a couple books and you bargain? Zero conditions accepted!"
Guilt flickered across his face at the reminder. "All right, all right—no conditions! Torture me however you please; I'll take it all until your anger cools. Just... how long till it cools?"
"Never!" she glared. "Not for my entire life!"
Instead of flinching, a goofy grin split his face. "My entire life? Means you're sticking with me for life! Hehe—luckier than Qiao Wenxian and Prince Yi combined! Hehe!"
Her fury felt like chucking a pebble into a pond—swallowed without leaving a ripple. Looking away before his goofy smile wrecked her resolve, she snapped, "Drop the act! Think I can't see through your new tactic? Won't work!"
"Think what you want—time to sweep the courtyard so the servants earn more silver! Besides, hate seeing you upset anyway. Hehe!" Stepping toward the door just as Hongdou and Amei entered carrying lunch trays, he brightened. "Hey, Zhu Shuzhen—after acing my books, let me stay and eat lunch with you?"
She whipped back. "Dream on! I'd share a bowl with a street beggar before eating at the same table with you!"
Undented, he grinned wide. "No rush—plenty of time ahead since you're keeping me for life! One day we'll share a table." And out he bounced before she could snap back.
Setting out the dishes, Hongdou and Amei traded a quick look before Hongdou began: "Lunch is served, Miss. While you eat, let me report on Young Master—courteous and measured with every servant today, studied hard without dozing off once!"
Amei chimed right in: "Didn't kick Qinggua once either! Really seems changed!"
Not catching their plot yet, Qianli Xun rolled her eyes. "Couple coins split your way and you're bought off already? Don't trust surface shows—distance tests a horse, time tests a heart. Wait and see! Leave us—Kuqiao will attend me."
Exchanging a glance with Kuqiao, both maids withdrew.
Laying out chopsticks and ladling soup, Kuqiao hunted for a natural opening. "Miss, haven't seen Matron Wei in two days."
"Out running business dawn to dusk—haven't seen her myself."
Kuqiao put on a wistful sigh. "Traditionally men handle outside and women handle inside. If you had a capable man by your side, Matron Wei wouldn't have to wear herself out running around town."
Qianli Xun clacked her chopsticks down. "Kuqiao, how long have you been with me and you're spouting narrow nonsense? What's wrong with women? Women hold up half the sky! Who matches Matron Wei's brains, or my poetry? Look at Chen Haoyu—male body, empty head, bullying girls and haunting brothels! If men are like him, give me women any day!"
Perfect segue! Kuqiao jumped right in: "Young Master isn't that hopeless—did great today, swore off brothels, even wants to join family business! Must be your discipline transforming him!"
