Qianli Xun set down her soup spoon. "Use your head! Rotten his whole life, reformed in forty-eight hours? Tell that to a toddler! Sudden weird behavior means hidden tricks."
Half agreeing yet remembering Qinggua's scoop, Kuqiao pressed on. "Heard something from Qinggua—not sure I should mention it..."
"Already teased it—spit it out."
"Ran into Qinggua in the West Courtyard. He said Young Master confessed in his sleep yesterday that he likes you, Miss..."
Her soup spoon halted midair and sank back down. "You believe Qinggua's tall tales?"
"He said Young Master changed overnight solely because he fell for you..."
Qianli Xun stayed silent, lifting her spoon far slower than before. Having planted the seed, Kuqiao said no more.
After lunch, Qianli Xun went straight into the inner room without a word. Kuqiao cleared the dishes, ate with the staff in the West Courtyard, and returned to find Chen Haoyu diligently sweeping the front yard—waving off Qinggua's help while Asheng and Sanbao watched.
Indoors, she found her mistress lying fully dressed on the bed staring at the canopy. "Let me help you change before your nap, Miss."
Qianli Xun sat up. "Couldn't sleep a wink. Come out with me."
"Where to?"
"The Divine Willow."
Knowing her mistress sought that tree whenever turmoil brewed inside, Kuqiao asked nothing and followed.
Crossing the courtyard past Chen Haoyu—broom swishing crisp and steady across the paving stones—Qianli Xun watched him sidelong. Hard to believe he was faking that focus. If he genuinely fell for her and turned good, where was she supposed to aim her grudge?!
Right then Asheng and Sanbao stepped up bowing. "Young Master's working hard today, Miss—spotless sweep!" Sanbao began.
Asheng added earnestly, "Noticed Young Master's actually quite a looker too—tall, solid build, handsome face!"
Catching onto the coordinated campaign at last, Qianli Xun put on a sharp frown. "Whose payroll are you two on—mine or Chen Haoyu's?"
"Yours, of course, Miss!"
"Then why are you campaigning for him?"
Silenced, both bowed as she swept out the gate. Asheng sighed, "No rushing this—slow and steady." Sanbao nodded.
Overhearing everything, Chen Haoyu grinned at them. "Much obliged, boys! Keep putting in good words—I won't forget you!"
"Words only go so far, Young Master—keep up the action," Asheng advised as Chen Haoyu returned to sweeping with gusto.
Walking toward the front gate, Qianli Xun ran right into Chouya returning from kitchen errands. Blocking her path eagerly, Chouya blurted, "Miss, gotta tell you something—Young Master really changed! Way better than before!"
Another cheerleader?! Had Chen Haoyu hypnotized her entire staff?! Saying not one word, Qianli Xun brushed right past her while Chouya called after her, "I'm not lying, Miss—it's true!" until Kuqiao frantically waved her back.
Inside the carriage rattling toward the hills, mistress and maid sat in heavy silence. First time stepping outside Chen Manor since her wedding, the bustling street noise felt almost foreign.
After learning Chen Haoyu spread those rumors, she had dreamed up a dozen punishments before settling on relatively mild discipline—expecting his misery to feed her joy. Instead she felt miserable while he smiled through every chore and declared his love! Heaven wouldn't even grant her the clean satisfaction of hating him.
#CH 239
#TITLE: Lightness After Letting Go
Where the mountain path began, the driver halted and set down the stepping stool. Kuqiao hopped down first and extended her hand. Reading her mistress's long silence throughout the ride, Kuqiao felt certain of the reason: Young Master turning sweet had bottled up all Miss's bitterness over last year's rumors, driving her back to the Divine Willow.
Seeing her maid's cautious gesture, Qianli Xun rested her palm on Kuqiao's to reassure her and stepped down.
Pilgrims of every age streamed up and down the trail bound for the hillside nunnery. Before transmigrating, Qianli Xun had scoffed at superstition; now she no longer judged. Everybody needed an emotional anchor—ordinary folks burned incense at temples, while she came sitting beneath a thousand-year-old willow whenever her heart knotted up.
Walking toward the Divine Willow, she noticed far fewer catkins drifting now. Counting days, Prince Yi and Mengrong had been gone over half a month, the same stretch she had lived in Chen Manor. Wedding underway or already heading back, she hoped joy helped Mengrong let go of her hatred.
Wait—why do I hope Mengrong lets go of hating me, while I refuse to let go of hating Chen Haoyu? All the way here she had fantasized crueler tortures: hoisting him in a well three days straight, burying him neck-deep in a field for passersby to mock, stripping him to his undershorts with soot smeared across his face and acupoints locked to beg on the street! All for putting her through hell last year!
Ahead beneath the willow, four young couples were knotting red ribbons onto boughs—nearly half again as many ribbons as last visit, spring fever blooming everywhere. A sudden ache of envy tightened her throat. When would someone hold her hand to tie a ribbon here? She almost regretted forgetting to buy one during her sweet days with Wenyuan—maybe the Divine Willow would have shielded them, sparing her this bitter tug-of-war.
As the couples wandered away, quiet settled back over the pond. Stepping close, cool air radiating from bark and leaves washed over Qianli Xun's skin, soothing the tight knot in her chest—easing the anger, yet not erasing it. Out loud, she addressed the ancient tree.
"Tell me what to do, Divine Willow. Marrying someone I loathed was bad enough; finding out he started those rumors makes me want to slice him into pieces..."
Called it! Kuqiao thought silently.
"...yet overnight he flipped into a different person—stopped fighting, took every punishment smiling, even..." Skipping his confession, she finished, "...leaving me unable to swing hard, yet walking away with my grudge unsettled leaves me choking!"
A warm breeze swept past, setting willow fronds and crimson ribbons swaying in the slanting afternoon sun, silk strands tangling softly around slender twigs. Zhu Shuzhen's verse rose unbidden to her lips:
"High strands, ten thousand threads of slender warm wind, / Veiled in mist and haze east of the market bridge. / Knotted into hidden sorrow beneath the slanting sun, / Snapped off as parting grief amid the fine rain." —"Willows" [TN: Translator's rendering]
"Fine poem!" a familiar voice called behind her.
Turning, Qianli Xun recognized the elderly nun from the nunnery—still unaware she was Master Jingguang—and bowed respectfully. "Master! Fate brings us together again."
Fingering her prayer beads with palms pressed together, Master Jingguang murmured, "Amitabha. Fine verse, young donor, yet hardly matching the scene. This willow stands by a pond outside town, so why 'east of the market bridge'? Clear bright skies today, so why 'parting grief amid the fine rain'? You're not parting from anyone here."
"Just recited whatever popped into my head, Master—no need to dissect it."
"Not quite random. 'Knotted into hidden sorrow beneath the slanting sun'—you carry resentment inside and hoped poetry would vent it. Yet venting never cures a heart poisoned by anger."
Struck straight in the bullseye, Qianli Xun asked, "Then what is the cure? Please enlighten me, Master."
"Merely passing along the Buddha's wisdom. The true remedy against anger is two characters: forbearance."
"Just swallow it? No! I can't let the man who ruined me walk off scot-free! I want payback until he's on his knees begging!"
"You refuse forbearance because you have not grasped the unarisen nature of anger," Master Jingguang said calmly. "Anger arises only from conditions; anything bound to conditions is illusory. Why let an illusory emotion torment you?"
"Illusory?!" Qianli Xun protested. "My rage sits solid in my chest, and his slander rang across all of Lin'an! Too deep for a mortal woman like me—I can't pretend it's fake."
"Have you taken revenge on the person who sparked this anger?"
"Sure did—only I'm too softhearted, my punishments weren't nasty enough, and he ended up enjoying them! Leaves me itching to go nastier, yet my heart holds back."
Standing beside her, Kuqiao silently prayed the nun's words would reach her mistress.
"Because your nature is kind," Master Jingguang said gently, "which is why karma brought us beneath this willow so you might hear the Dharma. Tell me: after exacting your revenge, did you feel happy?"
Searching her own chest—from hauling him out of the brothel in ropes, slapping his cheeks, making him apologize to maids, down to the stinking chamber pot at dawn—not one genuine laugh had crossed her lips. "Honestly? Not happy for one second."
"If mild revenge brought you no joy, harsher cruelty will only bring deeper misery. Acting out of anger never brings peace. True wisdom lies in forbearance and total release—only letting go sets you free."
"Letting go sounds easy on your tongue, Master. Why should I let go when he trashed my name, wrecked my marriage, and turned my happiness to dust?"
"Have you considered that the happiness you mourn was never yours to begin with?"
A quiet ache passed through her—maybe Wenyuan and she were never meant to be—yet she still pushed back. "By that logic, if victims endure and forgive, doesn't every villain roam free until the world collapses?"
"Tell me: is the man you hate an irredeemable monster?"
That stopped her cold. Scoundrel, sure—yet an irredeemable monster? Flashing back to him apologizing sincerely to Qinggua, sweeping the yard with real focus, smiling through every scolding... "He's just a good-for-nothing jerk."
"Not an irredeemable monster, then. Step back one pace, and sea and sky open wide."
"Isn't he getting off way too easy?"
"Do you truly care about yourself, donor?"
"Of course!"
"Then why obsess over another man at the expense of your own peace? Someone who truly cherishes herself has no hours to waste nursing hatred. Life spans barely a few decades—why pour your days into a poison that wounds you before it ever touches him?"
That hit clean through every wall. Who was suffering right now? Chen Haoyu was grinning through chores back in Moxiang Courtyard, while she was brooding out in the hills! "Harm your foe a thousand, bleed eight hundred yourself"—pure truth. Not for his sake, purely for her own lightness and joy, she let the grudge drop. Last year's rumors: wiped clean.
The instant she released it, weight lifted clean off her shoulders. "Thank you for your patience and wisdom, Master. I'm letting it go."
"Amitabha. Letting go brings great merit. May your days be peaceful and bright." With a bow, Master Jingguang turned and walked up the hill.
Qianli Xun bowed after her retreating back. "Thank you, Master!"
Beaming bright, Kuqiao asked, "Miss, does 'letting go' mean you're done punishing Young Master?"
Qianli Xun arched a playful brow. "Since when did Chen Haoyu buy you off too?"
"Never!" Kuqiao protested eagerly. "I just hated seeing you miserable ever since you found out—even punishing him made you glum! Hearing the Master talk made everything click!"
The first bright, unburdened smile in days lit Qianli Xun's face. "Got real wisdom in that head after all—worthy of being my maid! Fine, clean slate with Chen Haoyu. Back to normal for all of you, so quit scheming to feed me compliments about him—think I didn't notice?!"
"Nobody outsmarts you, Miss!" Kuqiao giggled.
Lighthearted again, Qianli Xun wandered the lakeside picking wildflowers with Kuqiao and weaving matching flower crowns for their heads. Whenever young couples strolled up to tie red silk, her eyes flicked over each pair—Kuqiao thinking she was people-watching, only Qianli Xun knowing she was half scanning for two familiar figures, torn between longing and dread of seeing them.
As dusk softened the hills, she sat a short while beneath the willow before riding home with Kuqiao.
#CH 240
#TITLE: Second Young Master Wins Back His Freedom
By the time Qianli Xun returned to Chen Manor, dinner had passed; not hungry, she waved off Kuqiao's offer of food. Master Jingguang's counsel still rang clear in her mind—time to set herself free by calling off the war. Changing into fresh clothes, she walked out to inform Chen Haoyu in person.
Stepping from Suxin Thatched Hall under the glow of courtyard lanterns, she spotted a figure approaching and waited until Chen Haofei stepped into the light.
"Good evening, Second Sister-in-law," he bowed respectfully.
"What brings you here?"
"Came to check on Second Brother."
Catching on at once, she raised a brow. "The elders sent you, didn't they?"
Chen Haofei owned up straight. "Grandmother and Eldest Aunt swore not to meddle, yet their hearts ache—couldn't come themselves, so they sent me to peek in."
"Why put me in charge if they can't bear watching?" Qianli Xun sighed—the elders were bound to be let down that she was calling it off after two days anyway. "Since you're here, walk with me to Boya Study."
Trailing behind her toward the back courtyard, Chen Haofei silently prayed his own future bride would not be fierce like this, locking her husband in back rooms!
Boya Study stood unlocked yet flanked by Asheng and Sanbao like two temple door-gods. Chen Haofei stuck his tongue out inwardly: No wonder Second Brother calls her a tigress—even posting sentries!
"You two can knock off," Qianli Xun told the guards. "No need to watch him anymore."
Thrilled, both replied, "Yes, Miss!" and hurried off while Kuqiao pushed the door open.
Stepping inside behind her, Chen Haofei called cheerily, "Second Brother! Came to visit!"
Emerging from the bedroom with Qinggua, Chen Haoyu lit up—doubly thrilled at spotting Zhu Shuzhen herself! Throwing an arm around Haofei, he crowed, "Took you long enough!"
Haofei whispered quick in his ear, "Scared of Second Sister-in-law—Grandmother and Aunt sent me to check on you!"
Releasing him, Chen Haoyu grinned wide. "Look me over—fit as a fiddle! Your second sister-in-law treats me great, plenty to eat and wear—feel sharper than I have in years!"
Poor Second Brother—she must have broken him so bad he's terrified to complain! Haofei pitied him silently, while Qianli Xun stood amused at his theatrical act.
Suddenly Chen Haoyu swung toward her. "Hey! Disappeared all afternoon—where were you till dark?"
"Since when do I report my schedule to you?" she shot back, then spoke plain. "Chen Haoyu, starting right now, you're free. Lockup's over, and my grudge against you is wiped clean. I'll draft a fresh copy of our old contract tomorrow—just stamp your thumbprint."
Free—joy leaped up, then crashed at "fresh contract." "No way!"
"What, begging for more discipline?"
"I mean the contract! Burned it already—no need to rewrite it!"
Puzzled, she frowned. "Meaning what?"
Stepping closer, Chen Haoyu said, "My thinking flipped completely these past two days. Those old terms don't fit anymore—why waste ink copying them?"
Fair point. "Suit yourself. Think over what you want tonight, come to Suxin Thatched Hall tomorrow, and we'll hash out fresh terms. Chat with your cousin—I'm turning in."
Just as she reached the threshold, he called after her, "Hey! Seriously done holding those rumors against me?"
Without turning back, Qianli Xun delivered a crisp parting line. "If a dog bites a person, does the person drop down and bite the dog back? What's the difference between them then? I'm human—refuse to turn into a dog, so I'm done beefing with that hound."
Even insulted, Chen Haoyu grinned beaming at her back. "Definitely fell for me—why else set me free so fast and drop hints about loyalty?! Hehe!"
Completely lost, Chen Haofei blinked. "Hint about loyalty? Did I miss something, Second Brother?"
"Dead certain!" Chen Haoyu turned beaming. "Didn't you hear her bring up dogs?"
"Uh, yes?"
"What are dogs famous for?"
"What?"
"Loyalty to their owner! She's hinting I better stay faithful to her! Fair enough—handsome as I am, girls drop at my feet outside; natural she worries!"
Chen Haofei shot a helpless look at Qinggua. "Was that what she meant?"
Qinggua just waved both hands in mute surrender.
"Tell Grandmother and Mother it's late so I won't disturb them tonight," Chen Haoyu instructed. "I'll pay respects first thing tomorrow—and not one bad word about your second sister-in-law!"
Hurrying straight to Eldest Uncle's courtyard, Chen Haofei found Old Madam Xia waiting right beside his uncle and aunt. After two days of white-knuckling her locked gate, dusk had shattered the old woman's nerves—worried ten laps broke his legs and apologizing to maids crushed his pride—so she had marched to Eldest Son's quarters demanding news. Chen Jianglin picked Haofei as spy: Shuzhen liked him enough to gift him poems, and the boy never stirred gossip.
Bursting inside, Haofei announced, "Good news! Great news!"
Tension melted from both women's faces. "Spill it!" Old Madam Xia urged.
"Second Sister-in-law unlocked Second Brother, called off all punishments, and wiped the rumor grudge clean!"
All three faces instantly fell. "Quit after two days?!" Chen Jianglin frowned. "Nobody reforms in two days!"
"We hoped she'd whip him into shape AND spark romance," Old Madam Xia sighed. "Looks like wishful thinking—she never cared."
"Guess Haoyu—and we—just lack the luck," Yao Qingyan murmured.
Realizing what they actually hoped for, Haofei hurried to add, "Don't fret yet, Grandmother, Uncle, Aunt! Can't tell how Second Sister-in-law feels, yet Second Brother is a totally different man around her! Looks head-over-heels for her—even warned me not to say one bad word about her!"
Instant sunshine! All three burst into relieved laughter.
"Huge leap forward!" Old Madam Xia beamed. "Already protective of his wife—think he'll still roam wild?!"
"Proof Shuzhen is a miracle-worker!" Chen Jianglin grinned. "Two days beat fifteen years of my lectures!"
"Did Haoyu send any message?" Yao Qingyan asked eagerly.
"Said it's too late to disturb you tonight, so he'll come pay respects first thing tomorrow morning!"
Tears of joy brimmed in Yao Qingyan's eyes. "When has Haoyu ever been this considerate? Usually vanishes without a word—now checking in politely!"
"Speak for yourself—he always visits me!" Old Madam Xia teased.
Chen Jianglin grinned. "Every time he visits you, Mother, he's fishing for handouts!"
"Asking me means he keeps me in his heart—when did you ever ask me for anything?"
Knowing better than to argue, Chen Jianglin just smiled as the night wound down.
Walking his grandmother home—bearing zero jealousy toward his favored cousin—Chen Haofei bowed at her gate, only for her to pause him. "Wait, Haofei. Grandmother believes you're going to win an official post."
"May your words come true, Grandmother."
"Know why I say that?"
"Because I'm family and you root for me."
"Wrong. Past fifty, I've read plenty of hearts. Yours is pure and upright—Heaven never lets a soul like yours down. You're the hope that lifts the Chen clan's standing."
Warmth flooded his chest. "Thank you, Grandmother."
"Off to bed now."
Next morning after breakfast, stepping out of Boya Study with Qinggua, Chen Haoyu bumped straight into Matron Wei—who had vanished during his entire lockup only to pop up the second he went free.
"Congratulations on regaining your freedom, Young Master," she curtsied.
He jumped a fraction. "Didn't even see you around—how'd you know Zhu Shuzhen let me out?"
"Seen or unseen, nothing in this courtyard escapes me."
Feeling as if her sharp eyes x-rayed straight into his ribs, he backed off fast. "Carry on—got errands!"
Watching him bolt, Matron Wei smiled softly. "Miss stays steady, Young Master turns fast—progress at last."
Passing Suxin Thatched Hall, Chen Haoyu gazed fondly at the door plaque. "Say, Qinggua, how come 'Suxin Thatched Hall' suddenly looks classy and refined to me today?"
"Because Young Madam named it and lives inside," Qinggua nailed it dryly.
Chen Haoyu chuckled, sweeping a fond eye across the courtyard. "Whole yard looks gorgeous today! Wife's got real taste—lotus brightening the pond, and that hehuan silk tree right there! 'Harmonious union'—think she planted it hinting she wants lifelong harmony and wedded bliss with me? Hehe!"
From reckless playboy straight into lovesick daydreamer—Qinggua sighed silently, then answered when his master nudged him: "Hint or not, having a hehuan tree in Moxiang Courtyard definitely promises wedded bliss and devotion!"
"Sweet talk! First payday, you get first bonus! Come on—time to see Father and Mother!" Full of fresh fire to amount to something real, Chen Haoyu strode out of Moxiang Courtyard.
#CH 241
#TITLE: He Really Changed
Eager to see her grandson—knowing he would visit his parents first—Old Madam Xia had marched straight to Eldest Son's courtyard at dawn, eaten breakfast there, and waited.
Hardly had Yingxue announced "Second Young Master is here" than Chen Haoyu breezed through the door with Qinggua, bowed briskly—"Father, Mother"—and dove straight into Old Madam Xia's arms. "Grandmother, I missed you to death!"
Stroking his hair fondly, Old Madam Xia asked, "Missed you too! Not mad at us for locking our gates on you?"
"Too grateful to ever be mad!"
Every face beamed until Chen Jianglin put on his stern father mask. "Warm morning—quit draping yourself on Grandmother and stand straight to answer questions."
Chen Haoyu hopped upright. "Before you quiz me, Father, I have something to ask you."
"Answer your elders first."
Yao Qingyan jumped in. "Did Shuzhen truly forgive those rumors?"
"Would I lie? I'm her husband—we're one family! Since when does family hold grudges against family? Right, Mother?"
Hearing him toss out "one family" cracked even Chen Jianglin's stern mask into a chuckle. Taking a sip of tea to steady his face, Chen Jianglin probed, "Shuzhen let you off way too easy—ought to have kept you locked up longer! What progress could you possibly make in two days?"
Chen Haoyu paused, then spoke straight. "Two days isn't long, yet something shifted in me. I'm done roaming outside—want to stay home and pitch in. Father, assign me some business work so I can earn my own silver, instead of coming up empty-handed when I want to buy my wife a gift and getting laughed at."
Surprise flashed across Old Madam Xia and Yao Qingyan's faces, blooming instantly into joy. Even Chen Jianglin dropped all pretense and grinned. "That's what you came to ask?"
"Exact right!"
"Seriously quitting the streets and joining family trade?"
"He just said yes—why keep grilling him?" Old Madam Xia chided her son. "Drop that habit of doubting your own boy!"
"My fault, Mother—I'll fix it," Chen Jianglin chuckled while Yao Qingyan dabbed happy tears: "Haoyu truly grew up! Happiest day of my life!"
Seeing how ecstatic one decent choice made his family suddenly struck Chen Haoyu with a sharp pang of sheepish guilt over years of disappointing them. Changing the pace fast, he asked, "When do I start, Father?"
"Whenever you're ready."
"Sooner the better!"
"With our southwest trade booming, the silk house is swamped. Report there—I'll send word to Steward Qiu to mentor you. Veteran hand; learn everything you can from him."
"Count on me, Father! Uh—what's the monthly pay?"
Chen Jianglin laughed. "Haven't lifted a finger and already asking wages? Nail the work first, and silver won't be short!"
"Not sending him today, are you?" Old Madam Xia cut in. "Once he gets busy I'll barely see him—let him start tomorrow!"
Nobody said no to Mother. Agreeing every bit of this miracle belonged to Shuzhen, all three decided to walk over and thank her together.
Meanwhile, right after Chen Haoyu left Moxiang Courtyard, Matron Wei had invited Qianli Xun for a morning stroll through Chen Manor's sprawling gardens—her first real look around since arriving.
Strolling amid birdsong and spring blossoms, Matron Wei marveled, "Didn't realize Chen Manor hid gardens this lush—flowers on every path!"
Remembering Chen Haoyu's bouquet, Qianli Xun remarked, "Even got roses here."
Glancing around, Matron Wei saw none. "Where?"
Not wanting to bring up his gift, Qianli Xun hedged, "Maids mentioned a small rose garden somewhere."
Dropping flowers, Matron Wei steered to her real reason for the walk. "Letting go of last year's slander in mere days—even at my age I couldn't match your generosity, Miss."
"Holding grudges only tortures myself. Letting him off lets me off too—now the whole garden looks twice as bright."
"Grasping that wisdom at your age is remarkable."
"Hardly my wisdom—that elderly nun at the hillside nunnery woke me up."
"Plenty listen to her sermons; few actually wake up. You have genuine insight, Miss."
"Takes insight to spot insight, Matron!" Both shared a warm laugh.
Reaching a wide pond dotted with broad lotus pads soon to push up buds, they rested inside a lakeside pavilion. Gazing over the water, Matron Wei asked out of the blue, "Miss, do you truly plan never to consummate your marriage with Young Master—living separate lives forever?"
Unfazed, Qianli Xun answered crisp, "Our prenuptial contract said no meddling."
"Heard you burned it."
"We'll draw up a new one."
"I usually indulge everything you wish, Miss, yet on this I must speak plain."
Knowing Matron Wei never wasted words, Qianli Xun turned attentive. "Go ahead, Matron."
"How do you find the Chen household?"
"Wonderful in every way—except Chen Haoyu."
"What if Young Master genuinely reforms?"
"Him? Reform?"
"From what I've watched, brief as your discipline was, he has changed deeply."
"Don't fall for it, Matron! Just a fresh act to disarm me."
"I disagree. He's truly changing."
Forced to entertain it for a second, Qianli Xun shrugged. "So what? Him changing doesn't mean I have to sleep with him!"
"I've lived long enough to know how hard marriage is for a woman, and how rare a decent family is. The Chens outshine the Lins and Qiaos ten times over—treasure it, Miss. If Young Master turns good, learn to open your heart slowly."
Hearing Matron Wei push this meant the inevitable loomed closer than she wanted. "I don't love Chen Haoyu."
Matron Wei sighed. "How many women in this world ever get to marry for love?"
Just then Kuqiao hurried up. "Miss! Old Madam Xia, Eldest Master, and Eldest Madam are waiting at Moxiang Courtyard!"
Matron Wei stood. "Come greet the elders—bet they came to thank you."
Rising with a tangled mind, Qianli Xun knew full well society expected her to share her husband's bed and bear children—yet another man still filled her heart. Where was Shen Jianchen now? Remembering his vow at Zuiyun Tower—"I'll stay chaste for you"—flooded her with equal parts ache and guilt toward Mengrong. Trying to shove his image out only made it dance sharper before her eyes.
Walking back into Moxiang Courtyard, she paused at the scene beneath the hehuan tree: Old Madam Xia lounging in the rattan chair, Chen Jianglin and Yao Qingyan seated around the table sipping Hongdou's whisked tea, Chen Haoyu perched on a low stool leaning affectionately against his grandmother. Warmth and peace radiated from the cluster—the exact big, loving family she had always longed for. In her bones, Chen Manor already felt like home... if only Chen Haoyu weren't part of the deal.
Before she could finish the daydream, a warm hand caught hers. "Hey! Where'd you run off to? Kept us waiting!"
Snapping awake, she whipped her hand clean out of Chen Haoyu's grasp. "Keep your hands off me!"
"All right, all right, no touching!" Grinning sheepish, he trotted back to his stool beside Old Madam Xia.
Matron Wei read the whole picture at a glance: Miss loved this home, just needed time for the husband. Stepping forward, she greeted them warmly. "Had I known you three were visiting, I wouldn't have dragged Miss off for a walk!"
Hongdou and Amei fetched two more chairs while Qianli Xun lingered a beat taking in the cozy tableau. Yao Qingyan rose and walked over. "Why stand back there, Shuzhen? Still mad at us?"
"Not at all—just looked so peaceful I wanted to freeze the picture in my head."
Taking her hand, Yao Qingyan smiled. "If it's lovely, step inside and become part of the picture."
Those simple words struck a deep chord—why stay outside gazing in when she could step inside? Walking under the tree, she curtsied. "Had I known you elders loved this spot, I'd have set more chairs out."
"One rattan chair and table keep the yard uncluttered," Old Madam Xia said. "Sit down!"
As Qianli Xun took a seat beside Matron Wei, Chen Haoyu's gaze stayed glued to her face with shameless fondness—ducking only to steal more looks whenever she glared back. Watching her son, Yao Qingyan beamed silently: Love really did move mountains, turning a wild rake overnight—making Shuzhen the Chen family's lucky star!
"We came to thank you today, Shuzhen," Yao Qingyan began.
"First for letting last year's rumors go," Chen Jianglin added, "and second for turning Haoyu around!"
Refusing stolen credit, Qianli Xun spoke straight. "No thanks needed. Dropping the rumors was for my own peace of mind, and I can't claim credit for Chen Haoyu—I only punished him for payback, and nobody transforms in two days."
"Exactly!" Chen Haoyu jumped in. "I was always a gem underneath—needed zero discipline!"
"Whatever your motive," Old Madam Xia chuckled from her rattan chair, "the result is pure gold, so credit belongs to you! Name any reward you want for giving me back a good grandson—even if it's tough, Grandmother will deliver!"
About to wave any reward aside, Qianli Xun suddenly remembered Matron Wei's warning by the lotus pond. Sooner or later these elders would press her to consummate the marriage—better cash this blank check right now to buy herself breathing room!
"Since you offered, Grandmother, I won't be shy! Keep gold, jewels, estates, and trinkets—I only ask one promise from you elders."
#CH 242
#TITLE: Children Walk Their Own Fate
Before Qianli Xun even opened her mouth, Matron Wei knew exact what was coming. Bringing up consummation by the lotus pond had served two aims: preparing her mistress for reality, and giving her space to negotiate smoothly instead of clashing head-on with the elders. Right on target!
"Name it," Chen Jianglin invited.
Rising solemnly to curtsy before all three elders, Qianli Xun stated, "Please promise me, Grandmother, Father-in-law, and Mother-in-law: until I genuinely develop feelings for Chen Haoyu, do not pressure me to consummate our marriage."
Heavy disappointment dropped over all three elders' faces, locking the yard in awkward silence.
Chen Haoyu felt a sharp sting too—proof she hadn't fallen for him yet—yet jumped right in to shield her from scolding. "Deal, deal! I promise! Only reason you don't like me yet is you haven't seen my good points—once I show them off, you'll fall for me soon enough!"
Breaking the stiff silence lifted a stone off Qianli Xun's chest; for the first time, his cocky bluff didn't grate on her ears. Matron Wei hid a small smile too—Young Master was growing downright likable.
"If Haoyu agrees, I won't object," Old Madam Xia broke the silence. "Your views sure differ from ordinary girls—takes an old woman time to adjust."
Awkward discussing bedroom affairs with a daughter-in-law, Chen Jianglin nudged his wife with a glance.
Yao Qingyan sighed. "Shuzhen, we elders treated that prenuptial contract as youthful games. Now that you're wed and Haoyu is reforming, why cling to paper?"
"I already burned the contract," Qianli Xun noted.
Yao Qingyan brightened. "Good riddance! Then live as real husband and wife—consummation is only a matter of time."
"Burned it in a fit of anger—meant to draw up a new one."
"No way!" Chen Haoyu cut in. "A real man's word is gold! Promised you already—why hide behind paper? Relax, I won't force you!"
As if you ever could! Qianli Xun scoffed silently.
"Skip a new contract, Shuzhen," Yao Qingyan pleaded gently. "A wife enters her husband's house to carry on the family line. If you refuse to consummate, when will your father-in-law and I ever hold a grandson?"
Confident he could crack her armor within twelve months, Chen Haoyu grinned. "Relax, Mother! One year from now I guarantee you're holding a grandson!"
All three elders chuckled—until Qianli Xun tossed cold water over it: "Want a grandson fast, Mother-in-law? Easy—I'm happy to arrange concubines for Chen Haoyu to bear children right away!"
Every smile vanished.
"No way!" Chen Haoyu blurted instantly. "I only have children with the woman I love!"
Sensing deadlock looming, Matron Wei stepped gracefully into the breach. "Old Madam, Master, Madam—congratulations are in order!"
"Congratulations for what?" Yao Qingyan sighed.
"Think back," Matron Wei said smoothly. "Normally Young Master would have bolted out the gate the instant he got free. Instead he stayed home chatting with elders, stood up for Miss, and completely shifted his tune—giving the Chen family another capable heir! Isn't that worth celebrating?"
"True!" Old Madam Xia lit up. "Even asked his father for business work today!"
Yao Qingyan cheered right back up and waved Shuzhen down. "Stop standing—sit!"
With only Old Madam Xia agreeing so far, Qianli Xun sat reluctantly, waiting to see where Matron Wei steered this.
"Everything is already moving naturally toward good," Matron Wei continued. "You cannot rush ripeness; push against the grain and everyone chafes, flow with it and good results bloom on their own. Who among us predicts tomorrow? Two days ago Young Master haunted brothels, and today—"
"Swear I'll never step foot in a brothel again!" Chen Haoyu chimed in on cue, making all three elders beam wide.
"Miss's request sounds blunt today," Matron Wei finished lightly, "yet who knows how her heart will feel tomorrow?"
Chen Jianglin finally spoke up. "Leave young folks' business to young folks. Worrying won't help—children walk their own fate."
With her husband yielding and Matron Wei's calm logic sinking in, Yao Qingyan smiled warm. "Fine, Shuzhen—consummation is between you and Haoyu. If Mother isn't fussing, neither will I! Drop the subject."
Unexpectedly, their grace left Qianli Xun feeling a twist of guilt—any parents would ache hearing a bride refuse their son's bed. Standing to bow deep, she spoke from the heart: "Thank you for your kindness, Grandmother, Father-in-law, and Mother-in-law. Words fall short—from today on, I truly treat Chen Manor as my home."
"One family needs no fancy speeches," Old Madam Xia grinned. "Consider your reward paid—no asking for anything else!" Laughter rippled under the tree.
For the first time in his life, Chen Haoyu truly tasted family warmth, gazing fondly at Shuzhen—funny how loving one person painted the whole world bright.
Once the elders left, Qianli Xun threw her arms around Matron Wei in thanks. Watching from the side, Chen Haoyu thought: That matron's a genius—maybe she can coach me on winning Zhu Shuzhen over!
When Qianli Xun returned to Suxin Thatched Hall and Matron Wei headed back toward Jingyi Hall, Chen Haoyu trailed after the matron—waving Chouya off to drag a reluctant Qinggua toward the West Courtyard—only to find Matron Wei waiting calmly at the rear archway.
"Something on your mind, Young Master?"
Grinning wide, he flashed a thumbs-up. "Just realized you're the real MVP, Matron! Amazing!"
"What do you want to ask?"
"You know the tigress inside out—coach me! How do I make her fall for me?"
"Simple. Rule one: stop calling Miss 'tigress.'"
"Done! Never again!"
"Rule two: learn to care for her thoughtfully."
"How?"
"Watch Eldest Master."
"Got it! Anything else?"
"Your soft approach works nicely—keep at it." With that, Matron Wei turned into Jingyi Hall.
Chen Haoyu blinked after her: She wasn't even around when I used the soft approach—how'd she know?! Must have spies everywhere!
Eager to score points with his wife, he headed back to the front yard—and bumped into Qinggua drooping like frostbitten eggplant.
"What's with the long face?"
"Walked into the West Courtyard and caught Asheng confessing to Hongdou—and she said yes! Mad out of my mind, I dragged Chouya right in front of Hongdou yelling, 'Fine, I've got Chouya!' Regretted it two seconds later!"
Chen Haoyu burst out laughing and slung an arm over Qinggua's shoulders. "Saves me playing matchmaker—guess Chouya's your destiny! Honestly she's a catch: knows martial arts, nobody dares touch you when she's around!"
"Hongdou knows martial arts too!"
"Yeah, and her heart's on another guy—even if you married her, she'd stray sooner or later. Chouya's dead loyal to you! Leave Hongdou surrounded by those seven male servants—would you sleep easy?"
Qinggua shook his head. "Nope."
"Leave Chouya surrounded by them?"
"Sleep like a baby!"
"See? Marry Hongdou and you'll go gray guarding her instead of serving me, till I fire you! Marry Chouya and you live stress-free while she pampers you! Which is smarter?"
"Put like that... Chouya wins!"
"Attaboy! Settle down with Chouya and follow me to see Young Madam!"
Qinggua caught his sleeve. "Wait, Young Master—which kind is Young Madam? Sleep-easy or worry-gray?"
"My league isn't your league!"
"Didn't Prince Yi raid your wedding though?"
"Shoo! Quit comparing yourself to me! I've got looks, wealth, charm—she married me, she's your young madam, what's to worry about? Quit wasting breath—I'm off to woo my wife!"
Trailing behind, Qinggua smirked silently: Look at Young Master calling it "wooing"—dropped his ego straight through the floor!
Slipping into Suxin Thatched Hall, Chen Haoyu found her bent over her desk writing—wondering how short poems took hours of scribbling.
"Young Master is here," Kuqiao alerted softly.
Qianli Xun lifted her head. "Halt right there! Talk from where you stand!"
"I'm your husband and master of Moxiang Courtyard—can't stand here like a lackey!" Plopping into a chair uninvited, he called, "Kuqiao, hear you make killer tea—whisk me a bowl!"
"He's got Qinggua—since when do you wait on him?" Qianli Xun cut her off.
Unfazed, he told Qinggua, "New mission: learn tea-whisking from Kuqiao! I'll set up a tea table in Boya Study too and try out this classy hobby!"
Setting down her brush, Qianli Xun snapped, "Spit out what you want—I'm busy."
"Hunched over a desk all day will pad your waist—come out for a stroll with me!"
Clear now he had genuine feelings she couldn't return, she shut it down crisp: "Zero interest strolling with you."
"Say what you like, never makes me mad. Quick tip though—"
"Talk fast or leave."
"Could you quit calling yourself 'yours truly like a tough old dame'? Doesn't match your pretty face."
"None of your business! Love talking how I talk—hate it, stay out of my sight!"
"Can't stay out of sight sharing one courtyard—and as your husband, I'll drop by plenty!"
Dreading daily visits, Qianli Xun put on a breezy smile. "Seriously, Chen Haoyu, agree to take a concubine! Say yes, and I'll treat you like my best bro—even back you up if anyone picks on you outside!"
#CH 243
#TITLE: Winning Over a Wife Takes Method
Twice in one day pushing concubines on him—how desperate was she to offload him? Swallowing the sting, Chen Haoyu refused flatly. "You alone are plenty—add another woman and I'd go nuts! Quit bringing up concubines."
Hoping to splash cold water over his newfound ardor, Qianli Xun spoke blunt. "Seen your type a hundred times: rich playboys sleeping around shameless, then one day acting 'reformed' chasing one true love so everyone applauds like the dirty past never happened! Makes my skin crawl. Save the cheesy lines—not buying it."
Nothing she flung could dent his mood anymore. "What rich playboy? I'm dead broke! Never met anyone I loved before, so I fooled around; now I found her, so I quit. Why hate me for that?"
She let out a cool huff.
Recalling Matron Wei's rule two—care for her—and remembering how she had wolfed down his lamb skewers at the chuiwan field, an idea popped up. "Stuck inside Chen Manor gets boring—I'm free today, let's head out to West Lake! I'll grill fresh skewers by the shore while we admire the water—my barbecue's unbeatable!"
Memories of his noisy lakeside picnic last year lit her fuse instant. "Have you zero civic pride, Chen Haoyu?!"
"Inviting you for grilled meat and scenery—what's civic pride got to do with it?!"
"Last year your gang trampled the grass by West Lake, choked the air with grease smoke, and left piles of trash ruining gorgeous scenery! Trash the lake yourself—don't drag me into it!"
Chastised by the woman he loved, shame actually pricked him. "My bad, my bad—swear I won't grill by West Lake again! Don't stay mad. Pick wherever you want to go and I'll tag along."
"Going nowhere! Vanish right now and leave me in peace!"
Struck out again—even trying to be sweet earned a lecture! Realizing cracking her shell took real strategy—and not starting at the silk house until tomorrow—he decided to go study under the family master: his father. Rising politely, he said, "Won't bother you further, Wife."
"Don't let the door hit you."
Stepping outside with a sigh, he muttered, "How'd I marry the hardest nut in Lin'an—and go crazy enough to fall for her?"
"Call it karma," Qinggua piped up.
"Watch that mouth—" Automatically checking his boot—having sworn off kicking him—Chen Haoyu left Qinggua feeling sheepish. "Just joking, Young Master!"
"No, explain why it's karma."
"Too many girls fell easy for you before, Young Master. Heaven sending an impossible boss like Young Madam balances the scales!"
"Fair point! Guess I'll own it—can't help falling for her anyway."
Reaching his parents' courtyard, Chen Haoyu waved Yingxue quiet so he could spy on how his father actually treated his mother. Peeking past the doorway, he saw Chen Jianglin standing at the dressing table delicately penciling Yao Qingyan's eyebrows while both smiled softly.
"Ever since marrying you, my own brow-drawing skills have gone completely rusty," Yao Qingyan teased.
"With me right here, you never need lift a brush."
"You spoiled me rotten."
"My wife—who else should I spoil?"
Chen Haoyu blinked in awe: Father puts every smooth line I ever tried to total shame! Clearing his throat—"Ahem!"—he stepped inside as Chen Jianglin laid down the brow pencil.
"Haoyu! Come sit!"
Yao Qingyan rose beaming to take his arm. "Not keeping Shuzhen company in Moxiang Courtyard?"
"Need to ask Father's advice on something."

"Starting at the silk house tomorrow—ask anything you want about trade, I'll hold nothing back," Chen Jianglin invited.
Chen Haoyu gave a sheepish chuckle—"Hehe..."
"Quit giggling and talk."
"Not business, Father... came to ask your secret for winning over your wife! Hehe!"

Seeing his boy genuinely humble, Chen Jianglin happily laid out his three golden rules.
"Rule one: total devotion. Nothing poisons a home faster than a wandering eye leaving your wife weeping. Ever seen me take a concubine?"
"Check! Rule two?"
"Rule two: real companionship. No matter how busy trade gets, carve out time beside her—leave her lonely and suspicion creeps in, unsettling the home; when home shakes, nothing outside succeeds."
"Got it!"
"Rule three—the clincher: master one skill your wife truly needs better than anyone else. Every time she needs it, your face springs first to her mind—once she relies on you, her heart follows!"
Chen Haoyu flashed a huge thumbs-up. "Genius, Father! No wonder Mother never fights with you! Drawing her eyebrows is your secret skill?"
"What else?"
"How long did it take to master?"
"Practiced from our wedding day—she mocked my shaky lines at first, yet after a year I perfected it. Now she won't let herself or a maid touch her brows—only calls for me! Can't live without me!"
As a boy, Chen Haoyu had thought painting a woman's brows looked unmanly; today he saw pure genius. "Rule three's tough—need to scout what Shuzhen actually needs daily, then train like mad!"
"Seeing you pour heart into Shuzhen makes me proud," Chen Jianglin smiled. "Good girl underneath that fiery temper—the day she softens toward you, you've won."
Armed with a battle plan, Chen Haoyu bowed out—and spotted the shortcut walking right beside him. "Qinggua, sneak Kuqiao over to Boya Study—tell her I have urgent questions."
Returning to Moxiang Courtyard, Chen Haoyu waited in Boya Study while Qinggua crept up to Suxin Thatched Hall's doorway peering inside for Kuqiao—until Kuqiao tapped his shoulder from behind. "Creeping like a burglar, Qinggua?"
"Looking for you without interrupting Young Madam! Young Master needs you—just took wife-wooing lessons from Eldest Master and needs insider intel!"
Glad to back Young Master's efforts, Kuqiao smiled. "For his sincerity, I'll go!"
Stepping into Boya Study, Kuqiao blinked to find Chen Haoyu voluntarily flipping through a poetry volume—baffling even Qinggua. "Actually enjoying books now, Young Master?"
Setting down *Collected Poems of Wang Youcheng*, Chen Haoyu smirked. "Uncultured oaf! Never heard 'Within books lie houses of gold and jade-like beauties'?"
"Used to say 'Within books lies nothing but dog poop,' Young Master!"
"Asking for trouble dredging up ancient quotes?" Spotting Kuqiao, he beamed. "Pull up a chair for Kuqiao, Qinggua!"
"Never offered me a chair—she's a servant too!" Qinggua grumbled.
"Comparing yourself to Kuqiao? Sit anywhere you please, Kuqiao!"
"No need for ceremony, Young Master," Kuqiao curtsied. "Ask anything—if it helps Miss, I'll tell all."
"See how polite real servants talk?" Chen Haoyu jabbed at Qinggua before turning eager to Kuqiao. "You can see I'm dead serious about Shuzhen, yet she won't budge. Need your help: what does Shuzhen need most every single day?"
"Need most...?" Kuqiao racked her brain.
"Something she does every single day without fail."
"Oh! Either writing poetry, or..." Catching herself before slipping huaben, she pivoted quick: "...practicing Qianli Fist!"
Chen Haoyu wilted—poetry baffled him completely, and his puny grappling wouldn't survive one flick of her wrist! "Besides those two! Some small daily habit that throws her whole day off if she misses it?"
Kuqiao thought hard. "Drinking my whisked tea?"
Jackpot! Chen Haoyu sprang up beaming. "YES! Tea-whisking I can learn! How hooked is she on your tea?"
"Not obsessed, just deep habit—needs a bowl first thing every morning. Once I got swamped and missed making it, and she stayed fidgety and out of sorts until I finally whisked her a bowl."
"Brilliant! I'll master whisked tea till she can't live without me! Hehehe! Keep this secret from her for now, Kuqiao!"
"My lips are sealed, Young Master!"
To master tea-whisking fast, he needed a real pro—the female tea master at Yongtai Teahouse! Needing tuition silver with empty pockets, he marched straight to Old Madam Xia and told her the honest reason; thrilled he was investing heart into his wife, Old Madam Xia handed over silver on the spot and waved off repayment, calling it seed capital for her future great-grandson!
#CH 244
#TITLE: Eldest Cousin's Plot Goes Up in Smoke
Within twenty-four hours, news of Chen Haoyu's sudden transformation reached Second and Third Brothers. At Old Madam Xia's courtyard after the storyteller left, the old woman and Yao Qingyan beamed nonstop praising Shuzhen and calling Haoyu's turnaround a blessing for the whole clan.
When Chen Jiangmu came home after dark, Madam Cui sat sulking with her lip thrust out until he pried out why.
"Great news!" he laughed. "Another capable Chen son pitching into business!"
"Means I have to write my surname upside down!" she grumbled.
"Huh?"
"Swore inside my head that if the Zhu girl reformed Haoyu, I'd write 'Cui' upside down!"
"Say it out loud?"
"No—just in my head!"
"Nobody heard—why stress yourself?" he chuckled. "Help me change; long day."
Relaxing as she unfastened his robe, she sighed, "Lesson learned—never underestimate folks again. Turned Haoyu around in days! That girl's got real tricks."
"Her fists scare him straight—never dreamed a wife could whip a husband into shape!"
"Makes sense though," Chen Jiangmu mused sitting on the bed. "Divorced once, can't afford a second divorce, so she rolled up her sleeves and forged herself a decent husband. Plus Chen men treat their wives right in their bones, or even she couldn't have turned him this fast."
Hanging his robe, Madam Cui countered softly, "Most Chen men—not Haodong! Passed Anya today and saw fresh marks on her neck."
"Pity—Haodong shines everywhere else, yet beating his wife is shameful. Still, Second Brother's branch; not our place to meddle." Snuffing the lamp, they turned in.
Over in Chen Jiangshu's rooms, Chen Haodong burst in late at night reeling from the reversal. Madam Bao sat untouched at her dressing table while Chen Jiangshu brooded on the bedside.
"Father, is it true Haoyu reports to Steward Qiu at the silk house tomorrow?!"
"True enough," Madam Bao snapped. "Old Madam and Eldest Sister-in-law crowed over it all afternoon! Pushing Haoyu into that marriage backfired!"
"My fault for getting greedy wanting him tortured by a fierce wife," Chen Haodong cursed himself.
Chen Jiangshu looked up calmly. "So he joins the silk house—you've traded since boyhood; scared he'll catch up? Relax!"
"Catch up? Never!" Chen Haodong scoffed, then frowned. "Just baffled how he flipped in two days! Think the tigress broke him so bad he's putting on an act for the elders?"
"Could be—Haoyu's always been a smooth actor," Madam Bao agreed.
"Quit guessing," Chen Jiangshu advised. "Ride with him to the silk house tomorrow and sound him out—he trusts you blind and spills everything. Then plan our next move."
Agreed, Chen Haodong returned to his own courtyard, where Xiaocui met him bowing. "Young Master, Miss asks to speak with you."
Rarely summoned by his wife, a flicker of hope stirred as he hurried inside—only to freeze at Ye Anya's icy stare as she rose. "What do you want?"
"My mind is made up. Give me a mutual divorce."
Thunder from a clear sky! Lunging forward, he seized her collar. "Barren hen! Haven't cast you out yet and you dare demand mutual divorce?! Never!"
Two tears slipped down Ye Anya's closed lashes. "Then write a letter of repudiation and cast me out."
Suspicion flared cold. "Hanging around Haoyu's divorced bride lately—did she put this poison in your head?!"
"This is between you and me—nobody else." Ready to burn every bridge, she stood unyielding.
He shoved her back. "Not casting you out yet—wait till I feel like it!"
"What does it take for you to cast me out?" she asked flat and steady.
Her frozen tone snuffed every spark of warmth inside him. Stepping close, he hissed, "Play the spotless, obedient wife and keep your mouth shut until next year when the clan names the Chen heir! Once I claim the estate, maybe I'll be generous and grant you a mutual divorce."
"Swear it?"
The flash of joy on her face stabbed straight through his ego. "That desperate to get away from me?!"
"Every waking breath I want out."
Already raw over Haoyu joining the silk house, that knife-twist broke his last restraint. Yanking her onto the bed, he struck her over and over while outside the closed door Xiaocui wept silently: When will this hell end?
At daybreak, Chen Haoyu rose bright and early for his first day at the silk house. Stopping outside Suxin Thatched Hall's shut door, he called loud through the wood: "Off to the silk house, Wife! First day might run long—won't be back till dark! Don't miss me too much!"
Doing her hair at the mirror, Qianli Xun ignored him.
"Pretty thoughtful, saying goodbye before work," Kuqiao smiled.
Sliding a hairpin home, Qianli Xun snapped, "His business, not mine! Go tell him to stop filing attendance reports at my door!"
Cracking the door, Kuqiao relayed, "Miss says no need to check in whenever you come or go, Young Master."
"Just her being considerate—all the more reason I must check in so she never worries I'm chasing skirts outside! When her heart's at ease, mine is too!"
Kuqiao hid a giggle as Qianli Xun marched to the doorway scowling. "Quit saying 'Wife'—gives me the creeps! Beat it to work and quit bugging me!"
Seeing her freshly groomed and glowing, Chen Haoyu beamed. "Seeing my gorgeous wife at dawn feels like a spring breeze—won't get tired all day!"
"More cheesy poetry and you eat knuckles!"
"Easy, easy—leaving now!" Bang! The door slammed right in his face.
Chanting silently—Not mad, not mad at all!—he strolled out with Qinggua trailing in amazement. "She slams the door in your face and you aren't even mad, Young Master?"
"Mad at my own wife? Chen men never lose their tempers with their wives—look at my father!"
"Exceptions exist," Qinggua muttered. "Word is Eldest Young Master beats Eldest Young Madam nonstop."
Chen Haoyu halted. "Where'd you hear that? News to me!"
"Always out partying—when did you ever notice home drama?"
"If that's true, I'm giving Brother Dong a talking-to!"
Outside the main gates stood Chen Haodong waiting beside his carriage with a bright smile. "Thrilled you're joining the silk house, Haoyu—waited to ride over together!"
"Awesome!" Climbing aboard with Qinggua driving, Chen Haoyu grinned while Chen Haodong studied him keenly.
"Seriously let Sister-in-law whip you into shape?"
"Nobody whipped me—I was solid all along!"
Relaxing at that familiar cocky swagger, Chen Haodong tested him. "If the silk house gets dull, slip out and play—I'll cover for you."
"Thanks, Brother Dong—always had my back since we were kids! Thing is, married now, can't bum around forever. She's the Poetess of Jiangnan—if I stay a deadbeat, folks will mock her for marrying a loser."
Tension crept back into Chen Haodong's gut. "Reforming for real? Not faking to dodge her fists?"
"Think I'm that good an actor?" Leaning in, Chen Haoyu grinned sheepish. "Never guess it in a million years, Brother Dong—I actually fell head-over-heels for the tigress! First time truly loving someone—feels amazing! Gotta level up so she falls for me too! Hehe!"
Half his master plan just went up in flames! Envy bit bitter inside Chen Haodong—how did this airhead stumble into love with his own wife while he, who secretly wanted Ye Anya, ended up beating her in cold rage?! Masking his venom, he probed, "Swear you're not messing with me?"
"Swear to Heaven—dead serious!"
Certain he meant every word, Chen Haodong iced over inside: Fall in love all you want—I'll dredge up enough skeletons from your dirty past to blow your marriage sky-high!
"Say, Brother Dong," Chen Haoyu lowered his voice, "heard a rumor you hit Sister-in-law Anya—true?"
"Pure servant gossip," Chen Haodong brushed it off smooth. "Don't listen to trash."
Accepting it—how could polished Brother Dong beat his wife?—Chen Haoyu dropped it.
Back in Moxiang Courtyard after breakfast, Qianli Xun tried writing her huaben about star-crossed lovers, only for her own sorrow to choke the ink dry. Setting down her brush, she had Kuqiao stow the pages and fetch the wooden box with the peeing clay doll.
Studying the little figurine, memories of Shen Jianchen brought a soft smile that quickly died. Surrounded by maids, guards, warm elders, and Chen Haoyu hovering daily, a raw chill of loneliness still hollowed her out. This room was a bridal chamber meant for a loving pair, yet she sat solo because she refused to betray her heart by sharing a bed with a man she didn't love. Even watching Chen Haoyu transform, loving him felt miles away.
Trapped in the same century, tasting Zhu Shuzhen's exact ache, she tucked the doll back in its box, unrolled fresh paper, and penned a ci lyric:
"Alone I walk, alone I sit, / Alone I sing, alone I drink, alone I lie down. / Standing idle, spirit bruised, / Helpless as a light chill touches my skin. / Who sees this feeling? / Tears wash away more than half my fading rouge. / Sorrow and sickness follow close— / Snuffing the cold lamp's wick, no dream will come." —"Jianzi Mulan Hua: Spring Resentment" [TN: Translator's rendering]
#CH 245
#TITLE: Sisters-in-Law Talk of Royal Guests
Reading back over Zhu Shuzhen's lyric, Qianli Xun felt the deeper abyss beneath the original poetess's grief—clearly ill on top of lonely, crying through cold nights without one warm hand beside her.
A memory from her own previous life surfaced sharp and clear. Rarely sick, whenever a heavy flu caught Qianli Xun, it floored her completely—fever, chills, aching bones. One month all her disciples at Qianli Fist Manor had gone out of town for a film stunt gig, leaving her solo in her twenty-second-floor apartment writing web fiction.
A brutal fever hit overnight. Too weak to type, she slipped a thermometer under her arm and collapsed onto the mattress—thirty-eight point six degrees. Too shaky even to shake the mercury down, she spotted fever pills in her nightstand drawer with zero water nearby, cursing herself for not keeping one female student behind. Pushing herself off the bed for water, martial arts strength useless against a virus, she took two dizzy steps and crumpled onto the floorboards—mind clear, limbs lead.
Her phone rang on the nightstand. Crawling over hoping it was a student, she saw Shen Jianchen's name glowing on the screen. Desperate for any help, she swiped the green answer button and tapped speaker.
"Hey, Qianli Xun! Heard your whole squad left town—bored solo? Want company?"
"I'm... sick... come over... door code's my birthday..."
"Whoa—hang tight, I'm on my way!"
The line clicked dead, and an odd rush of safety washed over her—the very first time she realized Shen Jianchen was someone she could lean on. Resting her cheek against the bedframe, she drifted out.
When she opened her eyes, she lay tucked under her quilt with a cooling patch on her forehead, rich tomato-and-egg aroma drifting in as Shen Jianchen walked up in an apron carrying a steaming bowl of noodles.
"Awake! Whipped up tomato-egg noodles—best thing for a fever!" Setting the bowl on the nightstand, he perched beside her. "Never saw anyone catch a cold this hard—lucky I called!"
"Thanks... grab me water for the pills in the drawer?"
"Checked already—every box in there was expired! Ran down to the pharmacy and got fresh medicine—already gave you a dose."
"I took medicine? Don't remember a thing!"
"Found you slumped on the floor half out of it—anyone could've kidnapped you! Carried you to bed, grabbed fresh meds downstairs, and you opened right up like a good patient!" Peeling off the patch, he tested her brow with his palm. "Fever's coming down. Sit up and eat before your next dose—can't take pills on an empty stomach."
Feeling strength return as she propped herself against the headboard, a quiet thought crossed her mind: Guy's actually decent—if I ever decide to date or marry, someone like him wouldn't be bad.
"Not taking the bowl?" he teased. "Want me to spoon-feed you? Gladly, just scared you'll deck me!"
Snatching the bowl, she snapped, "I've got two hands—feed yourself!"
"Shuzhen! Quit hiding at your desk—finally got free to visit, come chat!"
Chen Wenxin's cheerful voice yanked Qianli Xun back to Song-dynasty Lin'an, walking in with Yuegui—her sister-in-law's first call since the wedding.
Brushing off her nostalgia, Qianli Xun rose warmly. "Missed you, Sister-in-law!"
Scanning the room, Chen Wenxin marveled, "Whole courtyard feels fresh, and this room looks totally transformed! No wonder Haoyu shaped up overnight!"
"Where's Laibao?"
"Playing in Grandmother's yard with plenty of elders and maids watching, so I slipped over to see you. Hard to believe how much Chen Manor brightened since you arrived—Mother and both aunts all gathering around a beaming Grandmother, everyone crediting you, while you hide solo in here!"
"Grandmother's got plenty of company—gives me quiet to write verses and earn my poetess title."
"New lyric?" Stepping to the desk, Chen Wenxin read the fresh sheet and chided, "Why still writing mournful stuff like the Chens mistreat you?" Catching Shuzhen's hand, she eyed her rosy cheeks. "Glowing skin, smooth brow, bursting with health—writing 'sorrow and sickness'? Pure melodrama!"
"Pure melodrama" cracked Qianli Xun up. "Just scribbling—call it melodrama! Sit down!"
"Sitting indoors wastes gorgeous spring weather—let's stroll the garden!"
Agreeing, they wandered into the south flower garden past white fringe trees, blazing crimson kapoks, golden trumpet blossoms, and creamy magnolias while Kuqiao and Yuegui trailed gossiping behind.
The path lay carpeted in fallen petals impossible to avoid stepping on, bringing Lu You's famous line to mind: "Fallen into mud and ground to dust, only its fragrance remains." [TN: Translator's rendering]
Petals blooming bright, crushed to earth yet leaving scent behind—human life mirrored in miniature. She had lived two vivid lives—modern web novelist and martial master leaving books and disciples behind; Song poetess borrowing Zhu Shuzhen's name. Surely Qianli Xun herself could leave one bright trace of fragrance in this century too.
"Thank you for reforming Haoyu, Shuzhen," Chen Wenxin broke her reverie.
"Too many thank-yous already—scared everyone will end up disappointed in a few days."
"How so?"
"Bet Chen Haoyu doesn't last a week," she said bluntly—safe to speak candidly with Wenxin.
Chen Wenxin laughed. "Nobody knows my kid brother better than me—once he truly flips, he stays flipped!"
Leaving time to prove who was right, Qianli Xun held her tongue as Chen Wenxin added, "Since he's turning good, open your heart to him. I wasn't madly in love with your brother on our wedding day either, yet look how happy we are! Good character comes first; love grows after marriage."
Recognizing a gentle lobby from the elders, Qianli Xun gave a neat stall: "Character takes time to prove. Once I see he truly holds steady, we'll talk."
"Fair enough!" Satisfied, Chen Wenxin dropped it, crunching petals a moment before asking, "Know why I really came home today?"
"Tell me."
"Father-in-law sent me."
"My father sent you to visit your own maiden home?"
"Big banquet coming up in Lin'an—Chen family's invited, so Father-in-law sent me personally to relay his message while visiting home."
"Usually an invitation card does the trick—what's so special about this banquet?"
"Haven't heard?"
"Heard what?"
"Prince Yi and Princess Jiahe just got back from Bianliang!"
Back already! Joy and dread tangled instant in Qianli Xun's chest before she managed, "When did they arrive?"
"First thing this morning—slipped in quiet as they left! Lucky Father-in-law thought ahead and tipped off Butler Li at Youlin Retreat to send word the minute they arrived. Led all the Lin'an officials to pay respects at dawn, and tomorrow they're throwing a grand banquet—half welcome-home, half makeup wedding feast!"
Makeup wedding feast—married for real now. From today on, Mengrong was Princess Consort Yi. "Even for a royal wedding feast, why send you in person instead of a card?"
"Think a second and you'll know," Chen Wenxin hinted gently.
Realization stung instant: Prince Yi barging into Zhu Manor before the wedding forcing Prefect Zhu to kneel, then raiding the bridal procession leaving Mengrong wounded—both families walked on eggshells terrified of royal wrath!
"Chen family's footing the entire banquet bill," Chen Wenxin added.
Guilt pricked sharp. "Prince Yi isn't petty—he wouldn't punish either family, especially since he acted on his own!"
"Royal pride still matters," Chen Wenxin explained. "Even back at the chuiwan match I saw he had eyes for you—technically the Chens snatched the bride right out from under a prince! Father and Father-in-law started prepping this feast the day Prince Yi left just to smooth things over and keep both families safe."
Dreading an invite before she felt ready to face Mengrong, Qianli Xun asked carefully, "Who's on the guest list?"
"Lin'an officials and dignitaries with primary wives only—I'm only going on your brother's coattails. You and Haoyu aren't even invited. One exception: besides Master and Madam Qiao, Qiao Wenxian's invited with Miao Zhiyu."
Relief washed through Qianli Xun—spared for now. Meeting Mengrong was inevitable eventually, just not tomorrow. Yet guilt jabbed back: I did nothing wrong to Mengrong—wait, silently missing Shen Jianchen every other day, is that really clean?
#CH 246
#TITLE: Prince Yi and His Consort Return to Lin'an
The Bianliang wedding had been grand enough to satisfy Qiao Mengrong—at last she had married her brother, imperial decree sealing the bond even with his heart elsewhere.
Yet on their wedding night, he never lifted her bridal veil, leaving only cold words before walking out: "Mengrong, I can step into Zhao Pu's shoes to protect you and marry you, yet I cannot share your bed as him—because to me you have always been my little sister."
Alone in the red-draped bridal chamber, her tears burned straight into venom against Zhu Shuzhen—the thief who stole her brother's heart.
The very next morning, he rushed to depart for Lin'an, and Qiao Mengrong hurried just as fast, burning to launch her revenge. Needing a sharp ally beyond Xilan, she recruited Matron Zou—who agreed on the spot: "Staff can mind the capital manor; I'm coming to Lin'an to see what kind of vixen bewitched His Highness into abandoning his own bridal chamber!"
Battered from days on the road, Qiao Mengrong arrived back at Youlin Retreat ready to rest, sharpen her claws, and plot payback against her former confidante.
Shen Jianchen knew full well his coldness gutted Mengrong, yet closing his eyes and claiming her without love would make him feel like a beast. Maybe someday time would heal him into accepting her as a real wife—just not now, especially after promising Qianli Xun he would stay chaste for her.
"Staying chaste"—part noble, part absurd. In a rotting dynasty run by a foolish emperor and corrupt ministers where honest men stood powerless, clinging to personal honor felt noble; yet marrying one woman under fate while keeping his heart locked to another married woman sounded downright ridiculous.
All the road back from Bianliang, one resolution grew clear: he needed work. Staying cooped up at Youlin Retreat facing Mengrong drowned him in guilt, and idle hours kept Qianli Xun looping through his skull. Burying himself in real work might dull the longing—and once he let Qianli Xun go, he could finally look at Mengrong as his wife without shame.
Barely settled back at Youlin Retreat, Zhu Xiyan arrived leading a delegation of Lin'an officials. "Forgive us disturbing Your Highness after a tiring journey! Having missed seeing you off to Bianliang, we hurried over at once to beg pardon."
Annoyed internally—if you knew I was tired, why barge in?—Shen Jianchen kept his voice smooth since he had a favor of his own in mind. "No fault of yours, Prefect Zhu; I left quietly to spare everyone hassle."
Bowing lower, Zhu Xiyan presented their proposal: "The officials of Lin'an have arranged a feast tomorrow to welcome Your Highness home and celebrate your wedding with Princess Consort Yi. We beg you both to honor us with your presence."
Sick of the wasteful luxury modeled by his imperial father's court, Shen Jianchen frowned. "Bianliang's banquet was lavish enough—no need bleeding taxpayers dry for a second feast. Cancel it."
Instantly Zhu Xiyan and his entourage dropped to their knees. "We merely regretted missing Your Highness's capital wedding and began preparations the day you left! Not one copper comes from public funds—the Chen family is sponsoring every expense!"
Catching on—the wedding raid had terrified the Chens into buying peace, and refusing might leave them taking it out on Qianli Xun—Shen Jianchen relented. "Very well. Princess Consort Yi and I will attend tomorrow. Rise."
"Thank you, Your Highness!"
Waving the rest out to wait in the courtyard, Shen Jianchen spoke privately with Zhu Xiyan. "Since taking office as Military Commissioner of Lin'an, I've barely touched city affairs and feel remiss toward His Majesty's trust..."
Sweat prickled Zhu Xiyan's neck: If Prince Yi starts auditing Lin'an administration, half my skeletons spill out!
Spotting his panic—no shock in a dynasty rotten to the roots—Shen Jianchen had zero illusions about saving the empire; helping ordinary folk beat fighting hopeless court rot. "Keep managing prefectural politics, Prefect Zhu; military affairs need little of me too. I want a hands-on post dealing directly with commoners—what office fits?"
Exhaling in huge relief, Zhu Xiyan replied, "Your compassion for the people humbles me, Your Highness! There is a post—Judicial Adjutant of the Judicial Office, handling civilian criminal investigations and trials. Only rank eight-B, far too lowly for a prince..."
Perfect! "Rank eight-B falls under your transfer authority, Prefect Zhu. Move the current adjutant to a decent post—I'm taking over as Lin'an's Judicial Adjutant."
Thrilled, Zhu Xiyan bowed. "I'll arrange it today! Only, since His Majesty named you Military Commissioner, altering your title risks my head—we'll announce that Military Commissioner Yi acts concurrently as Judicial Adjutant on His Majesty's behalf to care for the people."
Credit the incompetent emperor for my work? Classic bureaucrats! Swallowing his disgust, Shen Jianchen nodded. "Fine. I report for duty right after tomorrow's feast."
Once the officials left, Bai Feng couldn't hold back: "Your Highness, haven't seen Kuqiao in ages—may I go visit her now?"
"Go," Shen Jianchen granted—then added quietly, "Ask Kuqiao how she's doing."
"Yes, Your Highness." Feeling a twinge for Princess Jiahe—still pining after a married woman right after his own wedding—Bai Feng put himself in the prince's boots: forced to marry someone other than Kuqiao, he'd probably act the same! Off he rushed to see his girl.
Needing to explain his new post before Mengrong drew wild conclusions, Shen Jianchen walked over to her courtyard.
Waking from her nap, Qiao Mengrong stood gazing bitterly around her maiden pavilion: wedded wife now, yet still parked in a side wing! She had every right ashow could they give me a hard time?"
Old Madam Xia laughed and chided him. "Talking nonsense again. Did you forget why your father-in-law forced you to marry the Zhu girl?"
"A real man doesn't rehash old humiliations! Don't bring that up, Grandmother, or if that tigress overhears, your grandson will be in deep trouble."
Wanting a fat handout, Chen Haoyu pivoted fast to butter her up. "Grandmother, I pray every day for your long life and good health. Today at Zhu Manor, I hunted down their Old Ancestor's longevity secret. Want to hear it?"
Knowing full well her grandson was spinning tall tales, the old lady played along. "Of course I do."
Chen Haoyu leaned in with mock mystery. "I circled the Zhu family's Buddhist hall for ages and heard nothing but wooden fish clacking and chanting. I figured their Old Ancestor lived this long from knocking a wooden fish and reciting sutras every day. How about I buy you a wooden fish and a string of prayer beads tomorrow so you can give it a try?"
"Save it. I'm not cut out for chanting sutras. Just drop by every day to sit with me and keep me company. Keep me happy, and longevity will take care of itself."
"Deal—I'll come keep you company more often." Casting about for a way to sic his grandmother on Zhu Shuzhen, he put on his best pitiful face. "Grandmother, you have to help me."
"Help you with what?"
"Zhu Shuzhen is bullying me."
"How?"
"She drove off my maids, stole my courtyard, seized all my property, and doles out a measly twenty taels a month! I'm practically a pauper now. Nobody outside even believes I'm a Chen."
Old Madam Xia chuckled. "That bride of yours has a razor-sharp tongue. Even I can't out-talk her. Besides, who told you to kneel and beg her to marry you, and sign some prenuptial contract? Blame yourself for gambling with Master Jiang and nearly losing the whole Chen estate."
Chen Haoyu blinked in shock. "How do you know about that?"
"Your new wife told me. Your parents know too."
Zhu Shuzhen! You didn't leave me a scrap of face in front of my own family! Just you wait! Chen Haoyu seethed inside while keeping his voice coaxing. "Gambling was my mistake, Grandmother, and I swear I'll never touch dice again. Still, you have to put Zhu Shuzhen in her place. She's walking all over my head now."
"How should I put her in her place?"
"Find excuses to give her a hard time whenever you can."
"She's your wife now. Giving her a hard time is giving you a hard time."
"Grandmother, we're husband and wife in name only."
A shadow crossed the old lady's face. "My grandson waited so long to take a wife, and this is how you two live. Breaks my heart just thinking about it."
"Don't be sad, Grandmother," Chen Haoyu wheedled. "Sooner or later I'll marry someone who pleases you."
The truth slipped right out of Old Madam Xia. "I'm already plenty pleased with the wife you have."
Seeing his scheme to weaponize his grandmother flop, Chen Haoyu went straight for the silver. "Grandmother, Zhu Shuzhen won't give me spending money, and I dare not ask Father or Mother. Could you spare me a little silver?"
She never pinched pennies with her favorite grandson. Motioning a maid to bring a wooden box, she drew out a five-hundred-tael silver note. "Spend it wise. Not a copper on courtesans or gamblers, hear me?"
Chen Haoyu snatched it up in delight and showered her cheeks with kisses until she laughed from ear to ear. After a few more minutes of banter, he tucked the note into his sleeve, dashed out of the manor, and swept Scarface and his crew back out onto the town.
